# Rule questions and missed comparisons from the v3 re-draft

Collected from each tile's checker verdict (`<slug>.check.json`). A rule question is a case the v3 rules did not decide; a missed comparison is evidence the checker found that the draft left out. Neither changed a stored status: those went through adjudication.

746 rule questions and 598 missed comparisons across the checked tiles.

## acute-myeloid-leukemia-cd33-directed-depleters

Rule questions:

- 1. Dose when the model stated no exposure, occupancy or regimen target: is 'reached' inferred from clinical activity or observed target saturation (so the cell follows efficacy: same / contra derived_from efficacy), or does the dose cell stay untested? The draft applied the first reading to the ADC and the second to the antibody.
- 2. A randomized trial whose surrogate co-primary (CRc) trended in the predicted direction but never separated because the trial was stopped early for toxicity, with the outcome (OS) going the wrong way: does efficacy score contra (primary endpoint failed) or same + partial with outcome_tested negative_confounded (status follows the surrogate)?
- 3. Dating: when a tier-5 press release (CASCADE discontinuation, 2017-06-19) made the comparison public and a tier-3 registry record (2018-12-12) later set the status, is `year` the press-release date (evidence rule 6, earliest public date) or the date of the status-setting source?
- 4. Engagement when direct target engagement is shown (CD33 saturation on blasts) but the PD readout the model used (NK degranulation / ADCC) did not move: score on the model's readout (contra) or record the direct tier as same?
- 5. Are conference abstracts published in a journal supplement with a DOI (ASH abstracts in Blood) tier 1? If so the BI 836858 phase 1 result may have been public before the 2022 paper and the entry would re-date again.
- 6. Is a human genetic predictor (CD33 rs12459419) derived from a non-member drug's trials (gemtuzumab) and tested on a member (SGN-CD33A) a model system with its own patient-level track (model_kind human_genetic), or outside the class?

Missed comparisons:

- Vasu S et al. 2022, Haematologica 107(3):770-773, doi:10.3324/haematol.2020.274118 (PMID 34854276, PMC8883544): peer-reviewed report of NCT01690624 (BI 836858 monotherapy, R/R AML) three years before the registry posting; reports marrow CD33 target saturation at 40 mg, 'lack of pharmacodynamic effects' and low effector-cell levels as the termination reason, and states the preclinical-vs-clinical mismatch itself. Should source the antibody track's efficacy (2022), a new engagement entry and a dose entry.
- Fiedler W et al. 2022, Haematologica 107(12):2977-2982, doi:10.3324/haematol.2022.281128 (PMID 36005556): peer-reviewed report of NCT02632721 (BI 836858 + decitabine); ORR 38.8% (19/49) uncontrolled, and 'CD33 molecules were not fully saturated' at 80 mg in some patients. Belongs in patient_trials with its DOI, not registry-only.
- Stanchina M et al. 2019, J Hematol Oncol 12:85, doi:10.1186/s13045-019-0771-0 (PMID 31439003): the CD33 splice-site SNP rs12459419 (a human_genetic, patient-level predictor of CD33-antibody benefit derived from gemtuzumab trials) tested against SGN-CD33A outcomes, 'not associated with clinical response (30% CR/CRi in both groups)'. A patient-level comparison with a member drug that has no track; whether a genotype predictor from a non-member counts as a model system is rule question 6.
- Not a missing track but unverifiable: the sponsor's cynomolgus toxicology for vadastuximab (the only species that could have shown SOS or myelosuppression-driven infection) remains unretrievable; no peer-reviewed CASCADE report exists in PubMed (only Walter 2018, a review).

## acute-myeloid-leukemia-flt3-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- LACEWING opened in 2016; the model paper that predicted the combination (Ueno 2019) was published in April 2019, before readout but after the trial started. Does a model published mid-trial count as 'the system the class relied on', and as prospective?
- When the same drug works as monotherapy (same) but fails in a combination regimen (contra), does 'one drug's contra beats another's same' make the whole efficacy cell contra, or is a combination a separate prediction?
- Dose table: in LACEWING, gilteritinib exposure was at target (troughs equal to monotherapy) and the regimen failed and was abandoned. Should dose get 'contra, derived_from: efficacy' even though the monotherapy dose worked?
- Safety: quizartinib entered humans around 2007 on a rat and monkey package whose QT prediction matched (public in 2023). Is a different tox species package the same model_id ('rat-dog-glp-tox'), or a model_change that could lift the gilteritinib contra?
- Can a single-arm trial's response-rate primary endpoint (quizartinib phase 2, 2018) score efficacy 'same', or only a randomised primary endpoint?
- A patient-level resistance-genotype prediction (which kinase-domain mutations emerge at relapse) is filed under efficacy with endpoint_role 'primary', but relapse genotype is not any trial's primary endpoint. Does decision 4's primary-endpoint rule apply to prediction_level 'patient' tracks, or do they need their own endpoint_role value?

Missed comparisons:

- Efficacy, earlier 'same' in 2018: Cortes 2018 Lancet Oncol quizartinib single-arm phase 2 (doi:10.1016/S1470-2045(18)30240-7, epub 2018-05-31; NCT00989261), co-primary endpoints CRc and CR. The FDA Xospata review (Nov 2018, in bundle) also approved gilteritinib on ADMIRAL's co-primary CR/CRh. The strip's first 'same' should be 2018, not 2019.
- LACEWING sponsor release: Astellas 2020-12-21, 'did not meet endpoint of overall survival' (https://newsroom.astellas.com/2020-12-21-Astellas-Reports-XOSPATA-R-gilteritinib-in-Combination-with-Azacitidine-Did-Not-Meet-Endpoint-of-Overall-Survival-in-Newly-Diagnosed-FLT3-Mutation-Positive-Acute-Myeloid-Leukemia-Patients-Ineligible-for-Intensive-Induction-Chemotherapy); dates the contra to 2020.
- MORPHO (gilteritinib post-HCT maintenance, NCT02997202): missed primary RFS (HR 0.68, p=0.0518); Astellas topline release 2023-03-09; Levis JCO 2024 doi:10.1200/JCO.23.02474. Post-transplant maintenance was not modelled, so it is a patient_trials/untested note with no status change, but it is a Phase 3 failure the draft does not show.
- QuANTUM-R regulatory reading: FDA ODAC (May 2019) and a complete response letter (June 2019) questioned the robustness of the OS result; quizartinib was not approved in the US until 2023. The 2019 entry does not record this tier-2 view.
- Crenolanib (a class member) is absent: phase 3 NCT03250338 in relapsed/refractory FLT3-mutated AML was reportedly presented at ASH 2025 (benefit reported in NPM1/FLT3 co-mutated patients), and ARO-021 (NCT03258931) vs midostaurin is completed. Not verified here; the drafter should check primary-endpoint results. AKN-028 is also not mentioned.
- Gilteritinib in-vitro activity against FLT3-TKD (Ba/F3 D835Y), the basis for enrolling and labelling FLT3-TKD patients, has no patient-level track. The FDA Xospata multidisciplinary review (NDA 211349, 2018, in bundle) reports CR/CRh 0/18 in TKD vs 35/161 in ITD in the pooled interim analysis and issued PMR-3 (>=30 TKD patients) and PMC-1 (in-vitro sensitivity of other non-ITD mutations). Candidate 2018 patient-level untested/contra entry; ADMIRAL's later TKD subgroup should be checked before scoring.

## allergic-disease-allergen-immunotherapy

Rule questions:

- Track identity for human-challenge systems: is an allergen exposure chamber one model_id across allergens and products (same facility class, same TRSS readout, same prediction), as drafted, or one per allergen/member ('a member drug's own model', decision 8)? As one track, Cat-PAD's 2016 contra hides the tree tablet's 2019 field success under decision 6 ('one drug's contra beats another's same'); split by allergen, the figure shows green for birch and red for cat. The same question hits the sibling allergic-rhinitis tile.
- Engagement on a human-challenge track: the 'PD readout the model used' is the model's own symptom endpoint in the same dosed humans, so engagement is same whenever the chamber study was positive and untested otherwise (tautological). Should human_challenge tracks score engagement on a mechanistic biomarker (IgG4, basophil, T-cell), leave the cell empty, or keep the downstream readout with readout_tier downstream as drafted?
- Safety null-null on a human-challenge track (decision 4, #58): the chamber subjects are themselves patients, so 'well tolerated' in the chamber paper and 'well tolerated' in the field are the same kind of observation at two sizes. Is a chamber tolerability sentence a stated prediction, and is a route-specific local reaction (sublingual tablet oral reactions) a platform harm split by modality or an on-target allergen harm kept pooled?
- An uncontrolled model result whose own authors disclaim it as an efficacy test (Ellis 2018 NAC, 'no firm conclusions of efficacy can be drawn'): does it make a scoreable prediction (contra when the field fails, as drafted) or is it untested (as proposed)? Decision 4's #57 covers a disclaimed patient trial, not a disclaimed model study.
- Decision 16 on allergen products: the 'tolerated dose at or above the effective one' is a DU regimen with an AE profile from the same chamber study, not a NOAEL/MTD margin. Does margin_stated apply to any human dose-ranging study that reports tolerability, or only to a stated ceiling?
- models: Engagement on an ex-vivo patient-cell track: the opener (Worm 2011) read proliferation and cytokines, the dosed-patient entry (Rudulier 2019) read tetramer frequency and chemokine-receptor expression on the same Fel d 1-specific CD4+ T cells. Does 'the PD readout the model used' mean the opener's readout (then untested) or any readout on the model's cell system (same + partial as drafted)? And does one uncorrected P=0.047 among ~12 readouts, with the authors' 'may decrease', meet decision 11 partial or #90 unresolved?
- models: A retrodiction scored on a positive key secondary (SQ tree tablet, alder/hazel-season TCS) when the primary (birch-season TCS) was also met: decision 4 ties efficacy to the primary or a poc_gate secondary, but the ex-vivo cross-reactivity panel predicts only the cross-season effect. Admissible with endpoint_role null as drafted, or should the retro be scored on the primary via the birch-specific IgG4/IgE-blocking readouts?
- models: A model paper submitted after a Phase 3 failure (Moldaver received 2017-03-29 vs 2016-06-20) whose experiments may have run before it and whose stated motivation is not the failed trial: decision 10 requires 'motivated by the patient result' for retrodiction: true. Is the flag decided by motivation (false, as drafted) or by run date when unknown? Status is contra either way.

Missed comparisons:

- ToleroMune Ragweed (member) chamber results exist that the draft names only in judgement text: 2010 chamber study NCT01198613 (Circassia PR 2012-03-08, AAAAI 2012 abstract: optimal regimen 97% greater TRSS reduction than placebo only in the moderate-baseline subgroup) and the 2014 Phase IIb chamber NCT02061709 (Circassia PR 2014-12-08: 8 x 12 nmol vs placebo 1.25 TRSS points, p=0.149, 'marked placebo effect'). Both are model-side (tier 5, not admissible as model_src) and no field result followed; the field observational study NCT01398137 (2011 season, n=90, 'to inform the design of a phase III field study') has no posted results and no publication. Nothing scoreable, but the chamber track should record that the ragweed chamber itself missed its primary in 2014, two years before the cat field failure.
- Cat-PAD Phase 3 follow-on NCT02040844 (results posted 2018-05-14): year-2 Combined Score 1.99 / 1.91 vs 1.93 placebo, n=430. Add to patient_trials on efficacy 5/6; no status change.
- SQ tree SLIT-tablet paediatric Phase 3 NCT04878354 (12 DU; results posted 2025-01-22; Allergy 2024, doi:10.1111/all.16363) is a second field confirmation of the chamber-chosen dose on the 'other' strip. patient_trials entry; no status change under decision 6.
- Cat-PAD paediatric safety study NCT01921257 (phase 2, results posted 2018-04-17) is a second dosed population for the peptide safety strip (basophil and chamber tracks); no status change, could raise confidence on safety 5/6 if read.
- No animal model found that was compared with patients for any member (Europe PMC searches on Cat-PAD, Ragweed-SPIRE, hdmASIT+, tree SLIT-tablet); the draft's 'no animal model' premise stands.

## allergic-rhinitis-allergen-immunotherapy

Rule questions:

- For model_kind human_challenge the engagement readout (chamber symptom score) is the dosed-patient readout itself; the rules do not say whether engagement is scored same, left untested, or the cell emptied for a human-challenge track.
- A year-only correction with the status unchanged (engagement 2: 2017 -> 2013) does not enter the adjudication queue, which keys on status_draft != status_checked; the brief gives proposed_year but no path for it.
- When a tier-5 press release makes a comparison public first (2017) and a tier-3 registry results record for the same trial is posted later (2018-06-15), rule 2 says the higher tier sets the status but not whether the entry keeps the press-release year or the registry posting date.
- gp-ASIT+ BTT009 met its primary at p=.041 but with a 15.5% effect below the sponsor's pre-specified 20% clinical-relevance bar, and the confirmatory trial then missed: is the first trial same + source_calls_it partial, or does a pre-specified effect-size bar on the primary govern the status?
- Safety rule 'a harm the species could have shown': a 219-subject human-challenge trial was silent on anaphylaxis that appeared once in 1084 programme subjects; does the test turn on species or on the model's power to show a rare harm?
- Dating a drafter-made retrodiction pairing (rBet v 1 mouse, efficacy 7): rule 7 keys on 'the earliest admissible source that makes the comparison', but no source makes a drafter pairing; is the year the model paper's (2013, both sides public via a tier-5 PR) or the tier-1 patient record's (2015)?
- Should the opening untested entry of a post-hoc model (efficacy 6, Tourdot 2013) carry retrodiction: true, since the model was built after the clinic knew the answer, even though that entry makes no comparison?

Missed comparisons:

- gp-ASIT+ conjunctival provocation test (CPT) track, human_challenge, absent from the draft (its judgement says 'no model system compared'): Phase IIb dose-finding scored on CPT, 170 ug chosen, Mosges 2017 doi:10.1111/all.13358 (epub 2017-12-22, PMID 29150857); Phase III BTT009 NCT02560948 (2016 season) CSMS -15.5% peak p=.041 and CPT reactivity reduced p<.001 in dosed patients, Mosges 2018 doi:10.1111/all.13433 (epub 2018-06-21, PMID 29512827); confirmatory Phase III ABT-gpASIT011 NCT03724240 (first posted 2018-10-30, 2019 season) missed primary: CSMS 0.15 vs 0.30 target, p=0.05, ASIT biotech 2019-12-20 (https://firstwordpharma.com/story/4919807, tier 5, model_src 10.1111/all.13358). Engagement same 2018, dose 170 ug reached then failed, efficacy same/partial 2018 then contra 2019 (not 2018 as the draft says).
- NCT02150343 (HDM-SPIRE field Phase 2b, n=715): results posted on ClinicalTrials.gov 2018-06-15, a tier-3 record of the trial the draft calls 'never published' and 'press release is the only record'; primary CS wk 50-52 1.69/1.51/1.40 (active) vs 1.56 (placebo). Under evidence rule 2 it sets the status for dose 3, efficacy 2, safety 3 (still contra/contra/same) and trial_unreported becomes false.
- Circassia PRNewswire 2013-09-12 (https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/circassias-short-course-house-dust-mite-allergy-treatment-achieves-significant-long-term-symptom-reduction-223419011.html): HDM-SPIRE chamber Phase II, 172 subjects, four doses over 12 weeks, symptoms improved vs placebo one year after start (p=0.02), 'All treatment regimens were well tolerated'. Earliest public member-drug chamber comparison (engagement year 2013) and the HDM-SPIRE model-side safety statement.
- Health Canada Regulatory Decision Summary RDS00632 for Itulatek (https://dhpp.hpfb-dgpsa.ca/review-documents/resource/RDS00632, approval April 2020): tier-2 record stating authorization rested on four trials including 'a Phase II dose-finding EEC trial'; sets model_filed true for the birch-tablet entries; reports 35.69% TCS reduction, one anaphylaxis at 8 SQ-Bet.
- Not found / not searched: Europe PMC returns no primary animal-model paper for the SPIRE peptides (reviews only), so no missed animal track for the peptide members; DP/MG/14-1 and Salsola kali pollen extract were not pursued beyond one Europe PMC query (no model found); no mouse-model search was run for rBet v 1.
- Stallergenes press release 2009-09-16, BioSpace copy (https://www.biospace.com/stallergenes-s-a-immunotherapy-tablet-containing-recombinant-allergen-rbet-v-1-of-birch-pollen-positive-results-for-a-phase-iib-iii-trial): VO59.08 Phase IIb/III, 2009 season, 483 patients, 12.5/25/50 ug rBet v 1 tablets all significantly reduced AASS vs placebo. Tier-5 patient record predating Tourdot 2013 (received 2012-09-04); dates the rBet v 1 mouse retrodiction (efficacy 7 year 2013) and is the earliest patient source for the patient-blood-dc-pbmc track as well. No further model-system search was run on the models pass.

## allergic-rhinitis-ptgdr2-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Dating a drafter-paired comparison whose halves went public at different times: the Phase 3 miss was public via a sponsor release on 2012-04-02 (tier 5) but the model sides only in Dec 2013 (Krug) and 2014 (Diamant). Does the entry carry the later half (2013/2014), the tier-5 date (2012), or the first tier-1 statement (2017)? And does a news report quoting a sponsor release count as tier 5 when the sponsor's own page is gone?
- Decision 4 (#50: a post-hoc PK/PD justification is untested with source_calls_it scaling) and decision 10 (a post-hoc model that reproduces the result is retro) both claim #50. Which governs an occupancy model built after the trials and invoked to explain a failed dose, and does a model that reproduces the Phase 3 null but not the Phase 2 positive at the same dose 'reproduce the human data'?
- A sibling-dosed animal model (OVA mouse; AM156, ARRY-063) that no member drug ever used and no source compared with patients: can a drafter pairing open a track and score it contra, or does #73's reliance requirement (decided for the inverse miss) apply to the direct miss too?
- Cross-member human-challenge contra under #35: the chamber was never run with the failing member (setipiprant) and the trial source leaves the failure 'not fully known' with a hedged exposure explanation. Is that contra (one drug's contra beats another's same on the track) or untested (failure attributed to conditions the model never tested)?
- Decision 16 double count: when a model's only safety statement is that the effective regimen was tolerated, is it scored as dose margin_stated true, as a safety same, or both (the draft does both on the asthma-challenge track)?
- Human challenge model in engagement: when the chamber's readout is itself the dosed-patient readout and there is no separate model number, is engagement 'same' a comparison at all, or should the chamber appear only in dose and efficacy?

Missed comparisons:

- Actelion media release, Allschwil/Basel 2 April 2012: setipiprant Phase 3 SAR and Phase IIb asthma 'did not confirm efficacy findings made in earlier studies'; development discontinued. Tier 5, patient side; dates the Phase 3 miss to 2012. Sponsor page no longer online; carried by RTTNews 2012-04-02 (https://www.rttnews.com/1852195/actelion-to-end-setipiprant-clinical-development-in-asthma-allergic-rhinitis.aspx) and PharmaTimes 2012-04-03 (https://pharmatimes.com/news/actelion_ends_development_of_asthma_and_sar_drug_977550/).
- Horak 2012, Allergy 67:1572 (doi 10.1111/all.12042, PMID 23025511, epub 2012-10): OC000459 200 mg b.i.d. in the Vienna grass-pollen chamber (n=35, NCT01448902) reduced nasal and ocular symptoms. A sibling non-member model-side source for the chamber track that predates Krug 2014 and would date the chamber-vs-field contra to 2012 if sibling proxies are admitted on the model side (the draft already admits them for the OVA mouse).
- No untracked model system found: fevipiprant Phase 1 (Erpenbeck 2016, doi 10.1002/cpdd.244) reports PK and an in-vitro-derived occupancy statement but no ex vivo PD; setipiprant Phase 1 (Sidharta 2014, doi 10.1111/fcp.12079) is tolerability/PK only; Shiraishi 2014 (ARRY-063) is the same OVA-mouse system as Stebbins 2010; no publication of the fevipiprant chamber study NCT01804400 exists (Europe PMC, 2026-11-05); ramatroban is non-selective and outside the class.

## alzheimer-disease-anti-app-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Engagement: when the model's main PD readout (brain protofibrils) was never measured in patients, may the cell be scored on a secondary readout that the model said would NOT move (plaque), where the patient readout moved more than the model predicted? Or is it untested?
- Dose: when no member's model stated a human exposure target, does 'reached, failed, abandoned' still give dose contra derived_from efficacy, or is dose untested because there was no target to compare?
- Safety and best model: rule 6 makes one drug's contra override another's same within one model. Ponezumab's Tg2576/2H6 model matched (2017) and lecanemab's mAb158 model missed ARIA (2023). These are different models of one class, so which status does the cell show after 2023?
- Safety: ARIA-E (edema) cannot be seen with histology in mice. Does a mouse study that reported only microhemorrhage count as silent on ARIA-E (not a miss), leaving only excess ARIA-H in Clarity AD to score?
- Efficacy: Study 201 favoured drug in direction (64% probability of a 25% effect) but missed its Bayesian gate. Against a model predicting benefit, is that a primary-endpoint miss (contra) or a directional match?

Missed comparisons:

- safety, lecanemab, contra (model_id tg-arcswe-mab158, plus Tg2576 with mAb158), year 2023. FDA SumR, BLA 761269 (sources/761269/761269Orig1s000SumR.txt, line 346): 'In studies specifically conducted with mAb158 to microhemorrhage in Tg2576 and APPArcSwe transgenic mice, no mAb158-related microhemorrhage was seen.' PharmR studies AD-TR-009 (3 months) and the 4-month Tg2576 study are consistent. Patients: Clarity AD (van Dyck 2022, doi 10.1056/NEJMoa2212948) had ARIA-E 12.6% (abstract) and excess ARIA-H in the full text; Study 201 had ARIA-E 10% vs 1% in the Jan 2023 label (ARIA-H 6% vs 5%, no excess). The model-side numbers were first public in the Jan 2023 FDA review. Under rule 6 this contra likely sets the safety cell's latest status.
- efficacy, lecanemab (only if the protofibril model is scored as predicting benefit): Study 201 missed its 12-month Bayesian ADCOMS primary (64% vs the 80% bar; Eisai release Dec 2017; Swanson 2021, doi 10.1186/s13195-021-00813-8), before Clarity AD met its primary in 2022.

## alzheimer-disease-anti-mapt-antibodies

Rule questions:

- neg in a class with no successful drug: the definition needs 'the class's usual effect bar'. When every member failed, may the model's own selection bar (here UCB's antibody D, a non-member) stand as class_threshold, or is neg unavailable, which makes the one case where a model called the whole class's failure unscorable as such?
- Engagement in another indication's patients: healthy volunteers count; do PSP patients (Yanamandra 2017, tilavonemab lineage) count as 'dosed humans' for an AD context, so that the stated comparison dates the engagement same to 2017 rather than 2021?
- Dating against a tier-5 record: rule 6 lists registry posting, online publication, label or print as year anchors. When a sponsor press release (Biogen 2021-06-16) made a failure public before the registry posting (2022-11-08) and a tier-1 paper later set the status, does year follow the press release?
- A reference antibody carrying a member's V-region in another backbone (Courade's mouse-IgG1 versions of IPN002, HJ8.5 and MC-1): is product_in_model true or false, and do its data count as 'the model's own data for a drug of this class' for neg?
- Same group, same parameters, same conclusion, different numerical method: is Goff 2025's spatial Monte Carlo (MCell) cleft model the same system as Geerts 2023's ODE QSP model (one model_id, strengthening entry, as drafted) or a 'later or better model' with its own track under rule 8? Goff itself contrasts its model with 'well-mixed' models.

Missed comparisons:

- Engagement, P301 track, 2017: Yanamandra et al. 2017 Sci Transl Med 9:eaal2029 (doi 10.1126/scitranslmed.aal2029, PMID 28424326) put HJ8.5 in P301S mice beside ABBV-8E12 (tilavonemab lineage) in PSP patients, plasma tau rising dose-dependently in both, four years before Ayalon 2021. Qureshi 2018 (doi 10.1016/j.trci.2018.10.007; gosuranemab in healthy volunteers, CSF unbound N-terminal tau suppressed 67-97%) paired with Bright 2015 (IPN002 clearing ISF eTau in human-tau mice) is a second pre-2021 comparison.
- Model with no track: iPierian's eTau model, AD-patient-derived cortical-neuron conditioned media plus two human-tau transgenic lines, in which IPN002 (gosuranemab's parent) neutralizing eTau lowered Abeta (Bright et al. 2015 Neurobiol Aging 36:693, doi 10.1016/j.neurobiolaging.2014.09.007, epub 2014-09-16). Patient side: TANGO CSF Abeta42 change at week 76 was 'small and comparable between groups' (-1.90% high dose vs -3.81% placebo; Shulman 2023). A member drug's own patient-derived-cell model whose stated prediction was not met.
- Mouse-seed biosensor assay (Yanamandra et al. 2013 Neuron 80:402, doi 10.1016/j.neuron.2013.07.046, PMC3924573): HJ8.5 was selected because it fully blocked P301S-brain-lysate seeding, and tilavonemab then failed (Florian 2023). It is the mouse-seed counterpart of Courade's AD-seed assay and predicted the opposite; the draft folds its in-vivo arm into the P301 mouse track but gives the selection assay no track. Courade 2018 itself contrasts the two results for antibody B.
- Tier-5 record not cited: Biogen press release 2021-06-16 (https://investors.biogen.com/node/23611) reported TANGO missed CDR-SB at week 78 and discontinued gosuranemab, seventeen months before the registry posting (2022-11-08) the draft dates its 2022 entry to. Status is unchanged (tier 1 governs); the dating is a rule question. The AC Immune/Genentech Tauriel topline release (September 2020) was not verified in this check.
- Within the seeding-assay track: Courade's antibody F took its V-region from WO2016137811A1 (Eli Lilly; conformational epitope aa 7-9 + 312-322, i.e. MC-1, zagotenemab's parent) and 'showed only partial activity on AD Tau'. The draft states zagotenemab was not among the reference antibodies; three of four members' V-regions were in fact tested, and the effect_size and notes should carry F.
- Immunodepletion track, engagement, prospective entry: Sopko 2020's ex-vivo depletion of unbound N-terminal tau in pooled AD-patient CSF (Neurobiol Dis 146:105120, epub 2020-09-28) preceded TANGO's AD-patient CSF readout (NCT03352557 results 2022-11-08; Shulman 2023 doi 10.1038/s43587-023-00523-w, all doses P < 0.0001). After the 2020 entry is scored retro, this is the track's first prospective prediction and a fresh paired `same` under decision 10.

## alzheimer-disease-bace1-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Can a dose 'contra derived_from efficacy' be paired across sibling drugs when the model that set the exposure target (Lilly dogs, LY2886721) never set the failed drug's dose? Atabecestat doses came from its own human CSF PK/PD. And when the failed trial (EARLY) was stopped for toxicity while the efficacy readout was interim, which dose-table row applies?
- Does a later safety prediction made from a non-model heuristic (low daily dose lowers idiosyncratic DILI risk, LY3202626) count as model_change and let the safety cell turn back, or is it out of scope because it is not a model system?
- The opening year for unpublished preclinical work is proxied to a member's Phase 2 start (2012). Should it be the first-in-human date (LY2886721 Phase 1, about 2011) instead?
- Efficacy 4 (A673T genetics vs atabecestat): A673T lowers beta-cleavage of APP only, while drug BACE1 inhibition also blocks other substrates (e.g. SEZ6), the likely cause of cognitive worsening. Is that a 'condition the model never tested' (#35/#72 -> untested) when no trial source attributes it so? Kept contra.

Missed comparisons:

- Willis 2022 (doi:10.3233/ADR-210037, PMC8842743): LY3202626 was 'predicted to have reduced risk' of hepatotoxicity because of its low dose. NAVIGATE-AD (NCT02791191) showed 'no evidence of hepatotoxicity'. This is a later safety prediction that held, on a dose-load heuristic rather than an animal model, so it is probably not model_change; see rule questions.
- J&J's May 2018 atabecestat discontinuation for liver enzymes (tier 5): no atabecestat nonclinical tox source was found either way. Safety stays contra from LY2886721, but the drafter should say that atabecestat's tox prediction was searched for.
- Elenbecestat (Eisai/Biogen) Sept 2019 MISSION AD stop press release: already covered by the NCT02956486 registry record, which is the higher tier, so no status change.

## alzheimer-disease-hrh3-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Co-primary endpoints: when one co-primary is nominally met (CogState episodic memory ES 0.35, p=0.0495, unadjusted) and the other plus ADAS-Cog are null, and the sponsor concludes the drug 'failed to show benefit', is the efficacy cell same + source_calls_it partial (v3 table) or contra (primary not met under the co-primary convention)? The v3 rules do not define how a co-primary design is read.
- Decision 16, success case: when a stated tolerability margin held (dose same, margin_stated true) and the predicted on-target harm appeared where predicted (safety same), is the one prediction counted in both cells or in one? Decision 16 only says a failed harm is counted once.
- A preclinical exposure/affinity target corrected by human PET before any patient was dosed (GSK239512: pig PET and in vitro under-called affinity 9-40x, doses 'would be erroneously high'): engagement same with magnitude (as drafted), a dose-cell contra on the model's target, or dose untested because nobody was dosed on it?
- Safety import (imported_from) where the model's only statement is a generic 'wide CNS safety margin' and the imported harm (psychosis-related AEs in schizophrenia at 3-8x the AD dose) is one the species cannot show: does a generic stated margin count as a prediction, and is a susceptible population's harm importable to an unselected AD context?
- Rule 6 dating: does a Wiley Accepted-Article online posting (PubMed-indexed 2012-09-29, print May 2013) count as 'online publication' for year? Affects dose 2 and safety 2 (2013 -> 2012), status unchanged.
- Model identity across sponsors: is a 'system' defined by the assay (species, readout, paradigm: rat sleep-wake EEG after oral H3 antagonist) or by the programme that ran it? Decision 6 #3 (one drug's contra overrides another's same on the same track) presumes assay identity, and this draft pools three sponsors' rodent cognition on one track; by that reading Sanofi's rat EEG (SAR110894 no wake-promotion at 3-30 mg/kg) and AbbVie's rat EEG (ABT-288 wake above procognitive exposure) are one track, and the safety cell headline flips from same to contra from 2014. The drafter split them under 'a member drug's own model'.
- Untested-condition attribution: the Sanofi DRI10734 summary both flags HLT 'Disturbances in initiating and maintaining sleep' as a safety signal (trend with dose vs donepezil-matched placebo) and calls the TEAEs 'of possible cholinergic origin' with every arm on donepezil. Is that an attribution to a condition the drug-alone rat EEG never tested (untested), or a mechanism note on an on-target class harm seen without donepezil in ABT-288 healthy volunteers (contra, as drafted)?
- Rule 7 dating: does a conference abstract in a journal supplement carrying only the supplement's collective DOI (CTAD 2013, 10.1007/s12603-013-0399-7) count as an admissible tier 1 source for the entry year? If so and it reports the sleep AEs, safety 4 dates 2013, not 2014.
- [orchestrator 2026-11-05] Track merge applied per the models check: Sanofi rat sleep-wake EEG re-keyed onto rat-safety-pharm-eeg (same assay, different sponsor); the 2012 Sanofi opener was folded into safety entry 1's note and safety verdicts 4-5 renumbered 3-4.

Missed comparisons:

- ABT-288 schizophrenia safety, importable with imported_from MONDO:0005090: Haig 2014 Schizophr Bull (PMID 24516190, PMC4193706) psychosis-related AEs 11.1%/15.9% at 10/25 mg vs 5.6% placebo, AE discontinuations 13.9%/8.7% vs 1.4%; Othman 2014 Br J Clin Pharmacol (PMC4093922) four early terminations for psychotic events up to 45 mg. Against Esbenshade 2012's stated 'wide central nervous system ... safety margin'. Numbers from search summaries, papers not opened; whether a rat margin can be scored against psychiatric AEs is a rule question.
- EU Clinical Trials Register results record 2010-022596-64 for the SAR110894 trial (NCT01266525), results v1 published 2016-05-10: a tier 3 patient-side source the draft could not read (readable via fc.py); supplies registry_date 2016-05-10 and could replace the tier 5 Sanofi summary as the trial record. The Sanofi DRI10734 summary (issue date 11-Nov-2014) would also support its own 2014 tier 5 entry with trial_unreported true.
- GSK239512 PET occupancy study protocol NCT00474513 (stated in Ashworth 2014 Methods); the draft says no NCT was found.
- GSK239512 product-in-model model side: EU CTR document for 2007-004117-33 (clinicaltrialsregister.eu attachment) states GSK239512 is 'active in animal cognition models', a tier 3 model_src with product_in_model true, instead of the GSK189254 sibling paper. No peer-reviewed GSK239512 rodent paper found.
- Stemmelin 2013 CTAD oral OC42 'A phase 2 study to investigate the effects of SAR110894 on cognition, daily function, apathy and sleep in mild to moderate AD' (J Nutr Health Aging 17(9):774-856, supplement DOI 10.1007/s12603-013-0399-7, Nov 2013): the earliest public patient-side report of NCT01266525 and a possible 2013 date for the sleep-disturbance contra under rule 7; text not retrievable (Springer/ResearchGate blocked), so unverified whether it reports the sleep AEs.

## alzheimer-disease-htr6-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Dating with a tier-5 source: the STARSHINE failure was public in Lundbeck's press release 2016-09-22 and the masupirdine Phase 2 miss in Suven's 2019-12-02 release, before any registry posting or paper. Rule 7 says 'earliest admissible source that makes the comparison'; a press release reports only the patient side and the drafter pairs it. Does it set `year` (efficacy 3 -> 2016, efficacy 5 and dose 6 -> 2019), or only registry/epub/print?
- Journal-supplement conference abstracts: rule 2 says 'a PMID-only supplement counts (#60)'. Does a DOI-bearing AAIC abstract in Alzheimers Dement (Cornery 2011, 7:S794) count as tier 1? If yes the NHP engagement `same` dates 2011 not 2023, and the 2011 proxy openers become real entries.
- Human PD data reported only as 'data not shown' inside a trial paper's Discussion (Atri 2018: healthy-volunteer PET, >80% occupancy at 30/60 mg, no study ever published): rule 2 says low confidence, but is it enough to move a track from untested to same (engagement) and to contra derived_from efficacy (dose), or must the PET study itself be a source?
- Intepirdine (RVT-101/SB-742457), a selective 5-HT6 antagonist with a Phase 3 failure (MINDSET) that Fullerton 2018 groups with idalopirdine as the class's Phase 3 record, is absent from the tile's member list (class_programs 3). The draft correctly did not widen; is the omission from `preclin.atlas_register` intended?
- A member drug whose human efficacy failure has no published disease-model side (SAM-760: Pfizer published only PET/PK, no efficacy model): the efficacy cell shows nothing for it while its dose cell scores contra on the NHP PET track. Should the NHP track carry an efficacy `untested` marker, or is 'no model relied on' decided per drug?
- CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models: can an `untested` opener rest on a registry protocol record with no results? Decision 2 (#125) calls it a sponsor statement, not a source, yet it is the only document showing the SAM-760 healthy-volunteer scopolamine challenge (efficacy 9) and the AD-patient PET (engagement 5) existed. If inadmissible, the human-challenge track has no source and disappears, hiding a model that demonstrably ran; if admissible, what tier does it carry (drafter used 3)?

Missed comparisons:

- Lundbeck rat AChEI-potentiation models for idalopirdine (dorsal-hippocampus ACh microdialysis + network oscillations, Herrik 2016 Neuropharmacology PMID 27039041; frontal gamma oscillations with donepezil, Mork 2017 Neuropharmacology PMID 27647493; mPFC microdialysis Eur J Pharmacol 2017 PMID 28188762; awake-rat fMRI with donepezil, Front Pharmacol 2017 PMID 28659792). This is the class's actual AD add-on rationale, run after LADDER (2014) so a retrodiction (`retro`), then prospectively contradicted by the three Phase 3 nulls on donepezil (Atri 2018). The draft names Herrik/Mork in efficacy judgement 1 but gives them no track.
- Healthy-volunteer PET 5-HT6 occupancy of idalopirdine (unpublished; reported only in the Atri 2018 JAMA Discussion, https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jama/fullarticle/2668349: 'in vivo positron emission tomography receptor occupancy study conducted among healthy participants', '>80% ... at doses of 30 and 60 mg/d'). This is the human side the draft said it could not retrieve; it drives the two corrections above. The same paragraph notes 5-HT6 expression is ~40% lower in AD brain, the caveat behind the terminated AD-patient PET NCT03307993.
- Cornery 2011, 'Receptor occupancy of the 5-HT6 receptor antagonist SAM-760 in non-human primates and healthy human volunteers', Alzheimers Dement 7:S794 (cited as Fullerton 2018 ref 13): the stated NHP-vs-human comparison was public in 2011, not 2023. Admissibility of a journal-supplement abstract is a rule question; if admissible, engagement entry 6 dates to 2011.
- Patient-side only, no status change: idalopirdine systematic review and meta-analysis, Int Psychogeriatr 2019 (PMID 30560763), tier 4; could sit in patient_trials of efficacy entry 3.
- CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models: SAM-760 rat scopolamine / MK-801 object-recognition model (Pfizer), a member drug's own efficacy model with a patient comparison (NCT01712074 futility stop). Reported only as AAIC 2011 supplement abstracts: Comery 2011 Alzheimers Dement 7:S794 (doi 10.1016/j.jalz.2011.05.2278) and Bell 2011 7:S778 (doi 10.1016/j.jalz.2011.05.2237), as cited by the Biomolecules 2023 review PMID 36830678 (PMC9953539, Table 2). Trackable only if the supplement-abstract rule question resolves admissible; it would then answer rule question 5 (SAM-760 efficacy model). Note the 'Cornery 2011' in this file is Comery 2011.

## asthma-adrb2-agonists

Rule questions:

- Decision 6 (a failed model stays failed on its track) against the decision 4 dose table ('reached, failed, a higher dose later worked' gives efficacy contra then same): when the higher dose succeeded only inside a fixed combination with a non-class drug and no monotherapy or budesonide-alone arm (CHF 5188 400/4 ug vs placebo, EU CTR 2008-001773-15), does the efficacy track turn back to `same`, and does a combination trial count for the member drug at all?
- Decision 7 lists registry posting, online publication, label and print as dating anchors. Can a tier-5 sponsor press release (MediciNova 2012, MN-221-CL-007 missed primary) set `year` to 2012 when the tier-1 record that carries the quote and status (House 2015) came three years later?
- A drafter-made pairing (comparison_stated false) where the trial population is a condition the model never tested (beta2-refractory exacerbations on maximal nebulised albuterol, against a naive pregnant-rat trachea organ bath) but the trial source does not itself attribute the miss to it: does the do-not rule's 'source attributes' test leave efficacy #6 `contra`, or is the organ bath `untested` for that question?
- Must the dating anchor and the quote come from the same document? NCT00679263 posted a secondary FEV1 result without statistics on 2011-12-15 (engagement #8 proposed 2011), while the plateau/PK and heart-rate quotes (dose #6, safety #5) exist only in the 2012 paper; the three entries on one track would then carry two years for one trial.
- Safety when the model paper claims only an 'excellent over all profile' after unspecified 'selectivity and safety profiling' (AZD3199, Stocks 2014) and patients showed mild class effects with less potassium suppression than formoterol at matched bronchodilation: `untested` (no named harm or margin) or `same` (stated favourable profile held in direction)?
- Decision 10 vs a same-experiment readout validation: the Antwerp CFD (engagement #12) is built from the dosed patients' own CT scans and read against the same patients' spirometry in one paper. Is that a model system track at all (patient-derived in-silico) or a biomarker/readout validation outside the atlas, and if a track, is it retro (proposed) or excluded?
- Decision 6 lets one trial miss score on every track it tests (EU CTR 2006-005689-38 is contra on both the Chiesi guinea-pig and the Voss 1992 kinetics tracks). The headline takes the best latest status so it is unaffected, but any per-track tally counts one failure twice; should a second track of the same drug on the same trial carry a flag?
- Voss 1992 states 'long duration of action' without a quantity; the kinetics track opens efficacy on it and has no dose entry. If the duration claim is a regimen prediction (decision 4, #91: half-life -> once daily), a dose entry mirroring Chiesi dose #9 ('target too low', 4 ug in CHF 5188) is owed on this track; if not, the efficacy pairing rests on a 24-h claim the 1992 source never made.
- A model run in another indication (pregnant-sheep tocolysis, IV stepwise infusion) paired with asthma patients on the same drug: the cross-indication rule sets imported_from for patient comparisons made elsewhere; does a model-side indication difference need imported_from or its own flag, or only the dose note the entry carries?

Missed comparisons:

- EU CTR 2008-001773-15 (https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/trial/2008-001773-15/results; results posted 2017-01-01, CSR 2009-07-15): CHF 5188 budesonide/carmoterol 400/4 ug once daily, single-dose 3-way crossover in 38 moderate-severe asthmatics; trough FEV1 +0.26 L vs placebo (95% CI 0.17-0.35, p<0.001), AUC12-24 +0.37 L, comparable to Symbicort bid (trough 0.05 L, p=0.170). The 'higher dose later worked' branch for the carmoterol track: owed as an efficacy `same` entry (tier 3, model_src Rossoni 2005; caveats: fixed combination, no budesonide-alone arm, single dose) and resets dose #9 to 'target too low' (magnitude off_by_2x).
- ClinicalTrials.gov NCT00683449 (MediciNova acute-exacerbation dose-finding, terminated, results posted 2011-07-29, n=29): primary FEV1 %predicted change at hour 2 after albuterol/ipratropium 16.57 (240 ug) vs 3.88 placebo, 3.03/4.27/-0.82 at higher doses, no analyses posted. An earlier tier-3 acute-asthma patient record for the bedoradrine track; belongs in efficacy #6 patient_trials (direction unresolved, changes no status).
- Pregnant-sheep infusion model of KUR-1246 (Kiguchi 2002 Obstet Gynecol, PMID 12220768, doi 10.1016/s0029-7844(02)02141-5; long-term sheep Kanayama 2005 BJOG, PMID 15663400): the draft's own note says hypokalaemia was a stated sheep finding, and hypokalaemia was the commonest AE in asthmatics (Matsuda 2012, verbatim in abstract). A safety track with a stated harm that held in patients, not drawn.
- MediciNova dog study 'Cardiovascular effects of i.v. MN-221 (bedoradrine) administered with nebulized albuterol in dogs' (MN-221 FY08-065, CHEST 2010 poster; press release https://www.nbcnews.com/news/amp/wbna39898303): the only member-drug airway-program safety model mirroring the CL-007 design (IV drug on top of nebulised albuterol). Not peer-reviewed, so it cannot be model_src (tier 5 is patient-side only); note-only.
- Tanabe TA-2005 airway models predate the Chiesi track: Kikkawa 1991 Jpn J Pharmacol PMID 1687479 (isolated guinea-pig trachea relaxation and beta2 selectivity), Voss 1992 Eur J Pharmacol PMID 1359974 (doi 10.1016/0922-4106(92)90158-r), Kikkawa 1994 Biol Pharm Bull PMID 7820105 (in vivo bronchodilating properties). Either model_first_use_year for the carmoterol guinea-pig system is 1991, or Tanabe is its own track; the draft left Voss 1992 unverified.
- Residual from the prior missing list (not a new system): Kikkawa 1991 (PMID 1687479) and Kikkawa 1994 (PMID 7820105) are folded into the Chiesi guinea-pig track by the drafter's judgement, but that track's model_first_use_year still reads 2005 on existing entries; either move it to 1991 or open Tanabe as its own airway track. Europe PMC search for human isolated bronchi / airway-cell carmoterol models (11 hits, reviews only) found no further system.

## asthma-anti-il13-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Decision 4 #32 names only the match-call rescue: when a trial misses its primary, a lung-function secondary is significant, and the source's own conclusion calls the mechanism failed (Brightling 2015; possibly STRATOS), is the entry untested (literal #32) or contra?
- Replicate phase 3s in the model-selected population with one primary met and one missed (LAVOLTA I/II, biomarker-high): same + source_calls_it partial, untested (#90 unresolved), or contra on the patient-level track?
- Decision 12 transfers cross-indication safety comparisons symmetrically. When the imported harm (AD conjunctivitis, monkey ophthalmoscopy clean) is directly contradicted by the context's own trials (>4,000 asthma patients, no excess), does the in-context null govern, or does the import still score contra with imported_from?
- Is a humanised-target knock-in mouse (IL-13-humanised OVA mouse, May 2012) the same track as the wild-type model of the same challenge (Wills-Karp 1998) when the two systems' stated predictions on a readout (eosinophils) differ?
- Decision 16 on a track whose floor is a derived contra: the cynomolgus NOAEL (90-100 mg/kg/week) held in patients but became public only in the 2022 FDA review, after the class failed. Is a 2022 ceiling entry (same, margin_stated true) written, and does it change the track's dose status?
- Engagement readout substitution: when the model's own readout (serum periostin) has a patient-side source but the drafter scored a different downstream readout (FeNO) as same, does the track carry same, or must the entry cite the model's readout (decision 4 'the PD readout the model used')?
- [models] A human-challenge model whose own primary missed significance (LAR -48%, 95% CI -19 to 90, n=29) but whose source calls it a reduction: is the source's call a stated positive prediction (mirror of #10), or is a non-significant model primary no prediction, leaving the track untested?
- [models] One drug, two populations on one track: does a contra in the population the model tested (no ICS; Noonan 2013, Korenblat 2018) outrank the same drug's same in a population the model never tested (ICS-treated MILLY)? Decision 6 (#3) ranks only across drugs.
- [models] Dose case 3 when the model's own readout was met: the tralokinumab PK/PD model predicted near-maximal FEV1 at 300 mg q2w and STRATOS 1 pre-BD FEV1 rose 6.03% (95% CI 2.34-9.73) at that regimen while AAER failed. Derived contra (decision 4) or a decision-9 surrogate-matched same with outcome_tested negative?

Missed comparisons:

- Lebrikizumab whole-lung allergen challenge (Scheerens 2014, PMID 24131304, doi:10.1111/cea.12220; NCT00781443, n=29, no results posted): a human_challenge model with a member drug, primary LAR reduced 48% but not significant (95% CI -19 to 90), larger in periostin/IgE/eosinophil-high subjects. Decision 8 puts allergen challenge in scope; the draft cites the paper only for CCL13/CCL17 and gives it no track.
- TROPOS (NCT02281357, phase 3 OCS-sparing, registry results 2018-05-16; Busse 2019 Eur Respir J, PMID 30442714, doi:10.1183/13993003.00948-2018): primary missed (OCS reduction 37.6% vs 29.9%, p=0.271) with no secondary met. A member phase 3 failure absent from the draft and from the STRATOS entry's patient_trials.
- Korenblat 2018 (Respir Med, PMID 29413502, doi:10.1016/j.rmed.2017.12.006, epub 2017-12-13): second lebrikizumab trial in ICS-free mild-to-moderate asthma, FEV1 primary missed (83 mL, p=0.06), 'inhibiting IL-13 in this population was not sufficient to improve lung function'. Replicates Noonan 2013 on the hbe-il13-signature and WT-mouse tracks; absent.
- IL-13-humanised OVA mouse dosed with tralokinumab (May 2012, doi:10.1111/j.1476-5381.2011.01659.x; FDA 761180 nonclinical pharmacology): the only mouse the product itself was dosed in and a genetically distinct system from Wills-Karp's WT OVA mouse; it needs its own model_id (decision 8) rather than sharing ova-mouse-il13.
- Out of class, for the record: Gauvreau 2011 (PMID 21057005, doi:10.1164/rccm.201008-1210OC) IMA-638/IMA-026 allergen challenge is the class's human-challenge precedent, but anrukinzumab is not a listed member, so it is not scorable.

## asthma-nr3c1-agonists

Rule questions:

- neg when the class has no successful member: may the reference ICS in the same crossover (fluticasone propionate, same target and action but outside the member list) serve as class_threshold, or is neg unavailable for a class whose every member failed, leaving only contra/same?
- Engagement: does 'the PD readout the model used' require the same quantity (rat lung oedema was never measured in humans) or does a downstream readout on the same axis (sputum eosinophils, FeNO) with readout_tier downstream suffice for same?
- Decision 16: does a qualitative 'improved therapeutic ratio over fluticasone propionate' in an abstract count as a stated margin (margin_stated: true) when no NOAEL, MTD or tolerated-dose number was read?
- A single-dose trial failure whose authors call the dose 'suboptimal' (GW870086 1 mg, Bareille 2013), where the model stated no exposure target: contra on the model's track, or untested because the trial tested a condition the model never set?
- When a registry's posted primary (minimum FEV1 LAR: 1 mg significant) and the peer-reviewed report's featured statistic (weighted-mean LAR: 1 mg not significant) disagree, which number is the model's number for neg/contra, and does the paper's 2014 date or the registry's 2018 posting set year?
- Are conference abstracts with a DOI (ATS 2021 A1202, A1418) tier 1 'peer-reviewed paper with DOI', inadmissible, or review-grade pointers only? GRANIT's only publication and velsecorat's later preclinical model both exist only in that form.
- Decision 4 case (4) when both the stated-dose failure (1 mg, J Asthma 2013-10) and the higher-dose success (2-4 mg, Curr Drug Ther 2013-09) were public before the model result (Leaker 2015): do contra and same both enter the track at 2015 with the same-year ordering of rule 7, or only the latest status (same), given 'a new entry is written only on a status change'?

Missed comparisons:

- AMP (adenosine) bronchoprovocation human challenge, GW870086X protective: Leaker et al. 2015 J Allergy Clin Immunol 136(2), doi 10.1016/j.jaci.2015.01.034 (PMID 25784273), trial NCT00483899 (started 2005-10, first posted 2007-06-07, no results). A human_challenge model run before the 2013 28-day failure; the draft uses NCT00483899 only as a date proxy and gives it no track.
- Tier-1 record of the GW870086 allergen challenge (NCT00857857): Bareille P, Allen A, Hardes K, Donald AC, 'Effect of a Novel Selective Inhaled Steroid on the Allergen-Induced Early and Late Asthmatic Response in Adults with Mild Asthma', Current Drug Therapy 8(3), doi 10.2174/15748855113089990005 (Crossref issued 2014-01-31; page is Cloudflare-blocked, unread). Re-dates efficacy entry 8 from the 2018 registry posting and lifts it from tier 3 to tier 1; its featured weighted-mean LAR shows 1 mg not significant, unlike the registry's minimum-FEV1 primary.
- SUN-597 (S-0597 / SPARC-1316): the draft says 'no public record'. NCT02041221 (Sun Pharma Advanced Research Co., phase 1/2 SAD/MAD in healthy volunteers and asthma, results posted 2016-03-31, primary = adverse events) and a UK HRA-registered allergen-challenge proof-of-concept study in mild asthma (hra.nhs.uk research summary 'A study to assess the safety, tolerability, PK and POC of Sun S0597') put a third member on the allergen-challenge track, uncompared (untested).
- Healthy-volunteer cortisol/osteocalcin PD as the human counterpart of the rat thymic-involution readout: Werkstrom 2016 (AZD5423, doi 10.1111/bcpt.12621) and Prothon 2022 (velsecorat, doi 10.2147/DDDT.S334960). Engagement entries on the rat track (subjects healthy_volunteers, readout_tier downstream) that the draft cites in judgement but never enters.
- Imported safety comparison: Kuna 2017 (AZD5423 in COPD, doi 10.1111/bcpt.12768, PMID 28212463) 'did suppress 24-hr cortisol' at doses effective in allergen asthma; admissible with imported_from MONDO:0005002 and consistent with the rat's 'moderate systemic GR-effect' for AZD5423. Discussed in judgement, not entered.
- Later velsecorat preclinical model, run after the 2b: Pinkerton et al., ATS 2021, 'Velsecorat (AZD7594), a SGRM Demonstrates Favorable Pre-Clinical Efficacy ...', doi 10.1164/ajrccm-conference.2021.203.1_meetingabstracts.a1418 (online 2021-05-03). A retrodiction candidate; atsjournals redirected to the OUP home page so content is unread.
- Sponsor's only publication of NCT03622112: Chupp et al., ATS 2021 A1202, doi 10.1164/ajrccm-conference.2021.203.1_meetingabstracts.a1202, reporting 360 and 720 ug as improving trough FEV1 (sponsor reading; registry primary analysis p=0.060 governs). Not a model, listed because the draft says the 2b has no publication. No AstraZeneca press release, SEC filing or pipeline document confirming velsecorat's discontinuation was found within budget.
- CONFIRMED lead: NCT01245426 was published as Bareille P, Hardes K, Robertson J, Davis A, Allen A, 'Efficacy of a New Selective Steroid (GW870086) in Asthma: An Adaptive, Randomised, Controlled Trial', Curr Drug Ther 2013;8(2):69-75, doi 10.2174/1574885511308020001 (Crossref issued 2013-09-01; abstract read on eurekaselect via Firecrawl). 135 persistent asthmatics, 2/3/4 mg once daily for 28 days (3 mg added at interim); day-28 FEV1 vs placebo 2 mg +0.172 L (0.015, 0.329), 3 mg +0.105 L (-0.053, 0.263), 4 mg +0.159 L (0.001, 0.316); conclusion 'a positive outcome with all doses'. Cazzola 2020 (PMC7495344) summarises it as 2 and 4 mg, not 3 mg, consistently improving FEV1 (MD 159-172 mL). Corrects the draft's 'never reported' (dose entry 4) and 'GSK ran no further asthma efficacy trial' (efficacy entry 5); under decision 4 case (4) the mouse-OVA, allergen-challenge and AMP tracks each owe a later efficacy same and their dose misses become 'target too low', not derived_from efficacy. Not scored here for the existing tracks.
- AMP track, efficacy: missing later same (year 2015, the model's epub, both patient results being public from 2013) pairing Leaker 2015 with the adaptive trial above: the class worked on the day-28 FEV1 primary at 2 and 4 mg; decision 4 case (4) 'contra, then same'. patient_trials for NCT01245426 should carry the tier-1 abstract quote and numbers, not the registry title.
- Allen A, Bareille P, Hardes K, Robertson J, 'Safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of single and repeat doses of GW870086: two randomised studies', Curr Drug Ther 2013;8(2):76-85, doi 10.2174/15748855113089990004 (NCT01160003): healthy-volunteer nebulised 5-8.75 mg, 5 mg gave a partial reduction in 24-h serum cortisol (Cazzola 2020 summary, text unread). A human HV counterpart to the AMP track's cortisol readout above the model's stated 1-3 mg; subjects healthy_volunteers, not a patient comparison.

## asthma-ptgdr2-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Dose 'reached and enough': a member reached target exposure and in the same year met a biomarker primary (Gonem 2016, sputum eosinophils) but missed an FEV1 primary (Erpenbeck 2016b). Does 'drug worked' in the dose table follow any positive primary, or the efficacy cell's status, which was already contra from 2013 on another drug?
- Safety: a member showed a patient harm that animals could show (GB001 hepatotoxicity) but no preclinical toxicology was ever published or filed. Is that untested, or 'silence contradicted' (contra) at low confidence?
- Efficacy: the rodent model was run on QAV680, but AZD1981 (the drug that failed) could not be tested in rodent cells (Schmidt 2013) and relied on guinea pig. Is a sibling-drug model admissible for a member that could not have been run in it?

Missed comparisons:

- Efficacy: ARRY-502 (member) phase 2 met its pre-bronchodilator FEV1 primary, +3.9% vs placebo at 200 mg BID, P=0.02, announced July 2013 (Array BioPharma FY2013 10-K, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1100412/000110041213000025/arraybiopharma6301310k.htm; tier 5, trial_unreported true). This 'same' precedes AZD1981's 2013-09-10 contra; the cell still ends 2013 contra.
- Efficacy/dose dating: Novartis press releases of October 2019 (ZEAL-1/2 missed FEV1 primary) and 2019-12-16 (LUSTER missed, development stopped), https://www.novartis.com/news/media-releases/novartis-provides-update-luster-phase-iii-studies-patients-uncontrolled-gina-45-asthma. ZEAL-1 (NCT03215758, results 2020-02-12) is also absent from patient_trials.
- Efficacy patient_trials: ADC3680B (PTR-36, Pulmagen/Teijin) positive phase II on morning PEF in mild-moderate asthma (press release, e.g. https://www.biospace.com/positive-phase-ii-in-eosinophilic-asthma-for-b-pulmagen-b-teijin-pharma). Date and whether mPEF was the primary are not verified.
- Engagement: ARRY-502 reduced Th2 mediators in patients (ERS 2014 abstract, https://publications.ersnet.org/content/erj/44/suppl58/4836). Conference abstract only, not read; possible downstream entry.
- Efficacy, human-allergen-challenge-lar track: OC000459 (timapiprant) reduced the LAR AUC 25.4% (95% CI 5.1-45.6%) (Singh 2013 Eur Respir J, PMID 22496329, epub 2012-04-10; NCT01056692, started 2006-02) and its own trials met pre-BD FEV1 primaries (Barnes 2012 Clin Exp Allergy, 28-day, NCT01057927; Pettipher 2014 Allergy, PMID 24866478: 25 mg QD P=0.028). A product_in_model 'same' entry dated 2012 precedes the AZD1981 contra.
- Efficacy, human-allergen-challenge-lar track: setipiprant's own proof-of-concept FEV1 trial in partly controlled asthma (NCT01225315, 2010-11 to 2012-02) has no posted results; a same-drug pairing for the challenge model, trial_unreported.

## atopic-eczema-anti-il33-antibodies

Rule questions:

- A tier-5 press release dates the entry (2019) while a tier-3 registry record (2023-05-24) sets the status: which document is `src`/`source_tier`, and does `trial_unreported` stay true once a registry results record exists?
- Decision 10 vs decision 6: may a `retro` entry be appended to a track already `contra` (Pietka 2020 on the IL-33/ST2 knockout MC903 track), and does a same-system re-run that overturns the model's earlier result count as the model 'reproducing the human data' or as a model-replication failure?
- Decision 7 when the registry results record lacks the datum the comparison rests on (NCT03738423 posts functional PK only; total-IL-33 saturation and eosinophils first appear in Kosloski 2024): does the registry date still set `year` for the dose and engagement entries?
- Is a GWAS locus shared by three receptor genes (IL1RL1-IL18R1-IL18RAP) with the causal gene unresolved an admissible class prediction for anti-IL-33 (`contra`) or `untested`; and what does `product_in_model` mean on a human_genetic track?
- Safety: can a member-specific specificity panel plus cyno PK (torudokimab) be paired with another member's tolerability (itepekimab) when decision 12 pools only on-target harms; and is a specificity statement a 'stated prediction' of no harm at all, or is the cell `untested`?
- #127 defines `strengthens: true` for a second miss; is it allowed on a same-to-same engagement entry (Kosloski 2024 direct readout), or should that datum go in `patient_trials`?
- models: efficacy, a prospective model whose stated prediction was worsening (DaSilva-Arnold: IL-33 attenuates Stat6VT disease) when patients showed a null (ATLAS arms ~ placebo): same on the no-benefit half (direction_only), contra by #58/decision 4 (predicted effect absent), or an efficacy analogue of decision 5's over-prediction? Under decision 6 the answer sets the tile's efficacy headline.
- models: decision 10 for an ex-vivo assay inside the trial paper itself, run after and 'given the clinical trial data' (Chen 2019 transwells on trial blister fluid): retro as proposed, and does a track that only ever retrodicted still open with an untested entry (#30/#112)?
- models: rule 7 proxy dating for a candidate-selection PD assay published four years after first-in-human (Okragly 2021; FIH Nov 2017): open the untested strip at publication (2021, as the draft's cyno track) or at first class use with the paper as proxy (2017)?

Missed comparisons:

- Peng 2018 (Inflammation, doi 10.1007/s10753-017-0673-7, PMID 28952069): anti-mouse-IL-33 antibody in DNCB-induced AD mice, 'blockade of IL-33 has a curative effect on AD' (abstract). A prospective pharmacological track (sibling antibody, product_in_model false) that predicted efficacy and was contradicted by ATLAS 2019; no track in the draft. The same paper also correlates serum IL-33/sST2 with human AD activity (comparison_stated in-source).
- Pietka 2020 (Sci Rep, doi 10.1038/s41598-020-63410-z, PMID 32296080) as a retro entry on il33-st2-mouse-dermatitis, year 2020: received 2020-01-28, its introduction cites the NCT03533751 failure ('the fate of IL-33 inhibition in atopic dermatitis is therefore highly questionable'), and IL-33-/-, ST2-/- and double-knockout mice develop MC903 dermatitis like wild type. Decision 10 draws it on its track with retrodiction: true, not only in judgement.
- DaSilva-Arnold 2018 (Arch Dermatol Res, doi 10.1007/s00403-018-1807-y, PMID 29368135, epub 2018-01-24): Stat6VT AD mice, 'AD-like disease was more severe in IL-33-deficient Stat6VT mice ... IL-33 may attenuate inflammation in this system'. A prospective pre-ATLAS model predicting no benefit (if anything harm) from IL-33 loss; candidate track scored same/partial on direction (no benefit seen, no worsening) or untested.
- Okragly 2021 (doi 10.2147/JIR.S320287): torudokimab's own in vivo human-IL-33-induced pharmacodynamic inhibition assay in mice (IL-5 endpoint), a member drug's engagement model never compared with patient PD; an untested engagement track distinct from the cyno specificity/PK safety track.
- Chen 2019 (doi 10.1126/scitranslmed.aax2945): 'etokimab also inhibited neutrophil migration to skin interstitial fluid in vitro', an ex_vivo human system compared in-source with the in vivo HDM challenge; candidate separate ex_vivo engagement track (same, comparison_stated true) rather than folded into the human_challenge track.
- Laquer 2022 (Br J Dermatol, doi 10.1111/bjd.21631, PMID 35481678, epub 2022-07-29; Lilly authors incl. Okragly, Sims): tier-1 report of the torudokimab AD trial, 'Interleukin-33 antibody failed to demonstrate benefit in a phase II ... study'. Not a model, but a missed source: patient_trials NCT03831191 has doi null. No abstract (research letter); not opened, paywalled.
- Checked and not applicable: England 2023 (Sci Rep, doi 10.1038/s41598-023-36642-y) tozorakimab characterization uses primary human cells and a murine lung epithelial-injury model, no skin or AD system; Imai 2019 (JID, 10.1016/j.jid.2019.04.016) is the same K14-IL-33 transgenic track already opened by Imai 2013.
- models: Peng 2018 serum-IgE readout vs Chen 2019 full text (ORA accepted manuscript, https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid:5e031a33-1e4d-4f51-b2e7-3c4001ee8f4d): 'There was no significant change in serum total IgE during the study (mean total serum IgE at study entry 10101+7594 kU/L; day 29 post-etokimab 9954+7394 kU/L; day 140 post-etokimab 10321+8103 kU/L)'. A missed engagement pairing on anti-il33-mab-dncb-mouse-ad: the model predicted serum IgE falls, 12 dosed AD patients showed none at day 29/140, while peripheral eosinophils fell 40% (lesional in the model). Candidate contra or same + partial, year 2019, low confidence (single open-label 300 mg dose, no placebo arm for IgE).

## atopic-eczema-hrh4-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Terminated-for-safety, underpowered trial (Murata 2015: 88/105 randomised, unblinded early) whose primary 'was not met': is that a patient result for a prospective efficacy contra, or does RULES_V3 section 10's 'a non-significant trend in a terminated trial is not a patient result' apply outside retrodictions? If the latter, the dermatitis-track efficacy contra and its derived dose contra date from 2021 (ZEST), not 2014.
- Dose 'reached' with no model exposure target ever stated (no mouse efficacious plasma level for JNJ-39758979 or adriforant): may decision 4 case 3 rest on human PD (eosinophil shape change) plus half-life, or is dose untested by analogy with decision 16 ('no stated margin: untested')?
- Decision 15 readout_tier for an in-vivo human challenge (histamine iontophoresis itch in dosed volunteers, Kollmeier 2014): direct ('blockade of a stimulus') or downstream (physiology)? Decision 15 names only ex-vivo blockade as direct.
- Safety: a positive stated prediction ('excellent safety profile' in rat and monkey) contradicted by an idiosyncratic human harm (agranulocytosis, mechanism never published) that the species classically cannot show: contra as drafted, or does the 'harm the species could have shown' test exempt stated-safe predictions too? The harm is compound-specific (asthma 300 mg x 12 weeks, 0/57 SAE; adriforant and toreforant clean).
- Track identity: is a different induction of the same read-out (MC903 calcipotriol vs FITC hapten Th2 dermatitis) a separate system with its own model_id? And when one paper runs two systems (MC903 inflammation and histamine itch), does each need its own entry on its own track?
- Register: the HRH4|antagonist member list is wrong both ways (pf-5212372 is a cPLA2-alpha inhibitor; izuforant/LEO 152020 with a registry-reported 2024 AD phase 2 failure is absent). Who corrects preclin.atlas_register before the kappa run, and does izuforant then score (it would add a 2024 contra on the inflammation tracks)?
- A model that predicted failure (Baumer 2011 dog; Rossbach 2009 inflammation) for a class that then failed: same with magnitude direction_only, or neg? neg needs effect_size against a class_threshold 'the class's own successful drugs cleared', and this class has no successful drug, so neg is undefinable here.
- [models check] Split prediction on one track (h4-mouse-hapten-contact-dermatitis: inflammation null, itch benefit): the inflammation half matched primaries twice (Murata EASI 2014, ZEST IGA 2021) and the itch half failed one (Werfel 2019 NRS). Decision 6 (#3) gives contra as drafted; decision 11 (#110) would give same + source_calls_it partial; or should each readout be its own track?
- [models check] Decision 4's sentence 'missed primary + significant secondary is untested unless the source's own conclusion calls it a match (#32)' read literally: Werfel 2019 missed its itch primary, hit EASI (one-sided P = .01) and concludes 'confirming H4 receptor antagonism as a novel therapeutic option', which would make it same for the inflammation-positive tracks (NC/Nga, FITC) and contra for the null tracks (dog, hapten inflammation); Murata missed EASI, hit itch PROs and concludes 'may be beneficial, particularly in controlling pruritus'. The tile assumes the primary governs absent endpoint_role poc_gate. Which reading?
- [models check] Mirror of the first check's rule question 1: Murata 2015 (88/105, unblinded early for agranulocytosis) now anchors two same entries for null predictions (dog, hapten inflammation). If 'a non-significant trend in a terminated trial is not a patient result' (decision 10) applies, a non-significant EASI cannot confirm a null either, and both sames date from ZEST 2021 with strengthens false.
- [models check] Human challenge as an efficacy model: Kollmeier 2014 is the human side of engagement 3 (same) and the model side of efficacy 17-18. Can one dosed-human PD study be both? And does its track open at first class use (NCT01068223, 2010, registry protocol record only) or at publication (2014), given this tile dates safety 1 to 2010 from Thurmond 2014 by the same proxy?
- [models check] A comparison-free patient-derived ex-vivo system (Glatzer 2013, engagement 5): decision 8 tracks 'every model system compared with patients'; the untested definition covers 'a model exists and was used for this class'. Is a never-compared, never-relied-on patient-cell system a track or a note? If a track, is subjects null (no dosed-human side) or patients (#56 precedent for patient-material ex vivo)?
- [models check] Proxy-dated opener with an interval: Uluckan 2023 places the MC903 run 'during the Ph2b' (2018-11 to 2020-08). Earliest bound (2018, as drafted), latest bound (2020) or publication year (2023) for model_first_use_year?
- [models check] strengthens: true on a same entry (dog and NC/Nga ZEST 2021 entries): #127 was written for a second miss that removes the first miss's excuse. Does it extend to a second match that removes an under-powering caveat, or should ZEST sit in patient_trials there?

Missed comparisons:

- Dog model of acute atopic dermatitis (Df-HDM-sensitised atopic Maltese-beagles): Baumer 2011, Exp Dermatol, doi 10.1111/j.1600-0625.2011.01268.x (PMID 21521369). Topical and oral JNJ 7777120 / JNJ 28307474, the same two antagonists as Cowden 2010, failed to prevent lesions (median score 2-2.5 vs 2 placebo). A 2011 prospective prediction of no anti-inflammatory benefit that the 2014/2021 EASI-IGA failures bore out: own track, product_in_model false, efficacy would score same (or neg, see rule question 7).
- Mouse hapten contact dermatitis (DNCB Th1 / TDI Th2): Rossbach 2009, Exp Dermatol, doi 10.1111/j.1600-0625.2008.00762.x (PMID 18647342). JNJ 7777120 15 mg/kg reduced hapten-induced scratching but not ear swelling or histology. A second prospective sibling-dosed track whose inflammation prediction (none) matched Murata/ZEST and whose itch prediction was contradicted by the Werfel 2019 pruritus primary.
- NC/Nga chronic picryl-chloride dermatitis: Ohsawa & Hirasawa 2012, Allergy, doi 10.1111/j.1398-9995.2012.02854.x (PMID 22686688). JNJ 7777120 improved clinical scores, pathology, skin cytokines and scratching. The same authors state the comparison with the JNJ-39758979 phase 2 in Allergol Int 2014, doi 10.2332/allergolint.13-RA-0675 (PMID 25249063), reading the itch secondaries as 'marked effects'. Own track; contra on the inflammation primaries.
- MC903 (calcipotriol) dermatitis with adriforant, Uluckan 2023, doi 10.1016/j.ejphar.2023.175533: currently filed under the FITC track; should be its own model_id (see efficacy 6).
- Patient-derived system never compared: outer-root-sheath keratinocytes from AD patients, Glatzer 2013, JACI, doi 10.1016/j.jaci.2013.06.023 (PMID 23932072), H4R-driven proliferation blocked by JNJ 7777120. An untested ex_vivo patient track if the atlas lists patient-derived systems.
- Register, member list: izuforant (LEO 152020), a selective oral H4R antagonist, ran a 216-patient phase 2 in moderate-to-severe AD (NCT05117060, JW Pharmaceutical/LEO, completed 2023-07-26, results posted 2024-05-31): EASI change to week 16 -9.99/-8.83/-8.87 on three regimens vs -9.11 on placebo, a third class failure the tile's member list omits. Phase 1 PD in healthy subjects: Jin 2024, doi 10.1111/cts.70032.
- Register, member list: pf-5212372 is CHEMBL4650312 (synonyms PLA-950, ZPL-521), a cPLA2-alpha inhibitor (Hewson 2012, JPET, doi 10.1124/jpet.111.186379, PMID 22160268), ChEMBL has no H4R mechanism or activity for it. It is not a member of HRH4|antagonist and contributes nothing; class_programs 3 is really 2 (3 with izuforant).
- [models check] Mouse chronic allergic contact dermatitis by repeated hapten challenge, JNJ 7777120 reduced inflammation: Seike 2010, Allergy, doi 10.1111/j.1398-9995.2009.02240.x (PMID 19886918, epub 2009-11-02; same group's H4+H1 follow-up Matsushita 2012, doi 10.1111/j.1600-0625.2012.01559.x, PMID 22897580). A positive inflammation prediction distinct from Rossbach's acute null, so its own track: untested 2009, contra 2014 on Murata. Title-level only, abstract not read.
- [models check] Hapten-induced dermal inflammation and pruritus model, Suwa 2011, Eur J Pharmacol, doi 10.1016/j.ejphar.2011.05.037 (PMID 21664903): an H4R antagonist reduced both; check hapten and regimen before deciding whether it is the Seike 2010 system or its own. Title-level only.
- [models check] Th2-dependent antigen-specific (OVA) skin inflammation with H1R/H4R blockade, Mahapatra 2014, PLoS One, doi 10.1371/journal.pone.0087296 (PMID 24503582), Hannover group: another positive inflammation system never compared with the clinic in the draft. Title-level only.

## atopic-eczema-pde4-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- When several class members went in on different efficacy models (crisaborole: TPA ear edema; LEO 29102: mouse allergic contact dermatitis, failed primary; difamilast: chronic contact dermatitis), must the cell record every model's track as its own entries, or only the best model's? The rule 'one drug's contra beats another drug's same on the same model' does not say what happens across models within one cell.
- Decision 16 margin_stated: the minipig NOAEL (5% ointment, above the 2% clinical strength) was filed under the IND before Phase 3, but the AUC-based margin (about 1x) was first stated in the 2016 NDA review using patient PK. Does a pre-trial NOAEL at a higher strength count as a stated margin, or does the margin need to be expressed in exposure before the trial?
- Does a sponsor's own optimistic reading in a tier-1 paper (Felding 2014: 'clinically relevant efficacy') hold the status until the registry posts a failed primary (2019), per rule 7 (what was known at the date)?
- An ex-vivo explant measuring free drug and tissue cAMP (Eirefelt 2020) is a PK/engagement readout; should its comparison with a failed efficacy primary be scored in the efficacy cell (as drafted, contra) or in the dose/engagement cells?

Missed comparisons:

- LEO 29102 (member): Phase 2 NCT01037881 primary endpoint (EASI change) not significant vs vehicle for any dose (p=0.27, 0.83, 0.08, 0.16), results posted 2019-10-14. Model = mouse topical allergic contact dermatitis (Felding 2014, doi:10.1021/jm500378a, which called the efficacy 'clinically relevant'). This is a contra for a second model_id (mouse-acd) dated 2019, currently buried in patient_trials with no status. It does not lower the crisaborole model's cell under the best-model rule, but it should be recorded.
- DRM02 (member): Dermira 10-K filed March 2015 (sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1557883/000104746915002713/a2223853z10-k.htm): 'Initial efficacy data in proof-of-concept clinical trials indicated that DRM02 gel was not more effective than vehicle-only gel'. Tier 5 failure (NCT01993420, no posted results). Its model side was not found, so it scores untested or is set aside, but the note should say it failed rather than 'stopped'.
- Difamilast (member): its FDA multidiscipline review (NDA 219474) names a mouse chronic contact dermatitis model; the Japanese Phase 2/3 primary IGA endpoints were met. A same entry for a separate model_id; the cell status does not change.
- LEO 39652 (topical PDE4 soft drug, LEO): Phase 2 in mild-moderate AD NCT02219633 (2014-07 to 2015-01, no posted results); Eirefelt 2020 (doi:10.1007/s11095-020-02962-1) states its 'lack of clinical efficacy' in AD patients. Not listed as a class member; the explant track's retro reproduction of its failure has no entry.
- Difamilast psychological-stress idiopathic dermatitis mouse model: JID 2022 abstract 576 (jidonline.org/article/S0022-202X(22)01026-0), run after Japanese approval; a further retro track if conference abstracts are admissible.

## breast-cancer-anti-igf1r-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Members-only vs same-mechanism: ganitumab and figitumumab are anti-IGF1R antibodies with randomised breast-cancer data, and ganitumab is a register member for IGF1R|binder_neutralizer on the NSCLC tile, yet neither is on this tile's member list. Do same-mechanism drugs missing from a tile's member list score, and can a retrodiction (Fagan 2012, Becker 2016) rest on their results?
- neg for a class with no successful drug in the indication: what anchors class_threshold? The draft uses an intra-experiment bar (the same antibody in parental or sensitive cells). Is that admissible, and must effect_size be numeric?
- A trial source that hedge-attributes a null to a condition the model never tested ('it is possible that lower-dose ridaforolimus ... contributed', Rugo 2017): does a hedged attribution demote contra/same to untested, or only a stated one?
- Conference abstracts (SABCS 2013 for NCT01234857, reportedly SABCS 2014 for NCT01605396) are not a listed source tier, yet they make a later model a retrodiction under decision 10. Do they count for 'the clinic already knew the answer', and for rule-6 dating?
- Engagement from patients of other indications (Atzori 2011, mixed solid tumours): the engagement rule admits healthy volunteers; does it admit other-disease patients without imported_from?
- (models check) A registry-only proof-of-biology trial whose pre-specified bar (GFS decrease in >=40% of participants) is the sponsor's decision threshold, not a number the model stated, and whose ER+ 80% CI (13.1-49.2) spans the bar: is the point-estimate miss contra, same + direction_only (direction held in 4/14 and 3/17), or untested with source_calls_it: unresolved (#90) when the source itself makes no call? Also, prediction_level for the GFS track: the signature is a tumour classifier (patient) but its only use in NCT00759785 is a class-level PD readout.

Missed comparisons:

- Ganitumab in breast cancer, same mechanism but absent from this tile's member list (it is a register member on the NSCLC tile): Robertson 2013 Lancet Oncol randomised phase 2 ganitumab + exemestane/fulvestrant, negative (doi 10.1016/S1470-2045(13)70026-3, PMID 23414585); I-SPY 2 neoadjuvant ganitumab + metformin, did not graduate (Yee 2021 npj Breast Cancer, doi 10.1038/s41523-021-00337-2, PMID 34611148, NCT01042379). Both are the human data Fagan 2012 and Becker 2016 retrodict; whether they score is rule question 1.
- IGF-I gene-transcription signature (Creighton 2008 J Clin Oncol, doi 10.1200/JCO.2007.13.4429, PMID 18757322): an in-silico, MCF-7-derived patient-level model used as the primary endpoint of the dalotuzumab presurgical trial NCT00759785 (GFS decrease in 28.6% ER+ / 17.6% TN; results 2017-04-14). It is its own in_silico track (untested: no efficacy comparison), not a footnote on the MCF-7 xenograft track.
