Decision 5 (#51) says the margin does not gate `same` (contra, opposite); decision 16 says a stated margin that held is dose `same` and the harm is counted once. Is the finding scored as dose same only, dose same plus safety contra, or safety same + over_predicted (the decision 5 alternative the drafter used)?
- Track identity for toxicology packages: do different members' repeat-dose intravitreal toxicology packages (aflibercept cynomolgus; abicipar rabbit/dog/cynomolgus) form one `model_id` or one per member or species? Under sticky contra (decision 6) merging lets aflibercept's over-prediction colour abicipar's matched prediction; splitting makes the safety headline green on abicipar's track.
- Partial safety agreement inside one regulator source (mild transient intraocular inflammation predicted and seen; nasal erosion predicted and not seen): does decision 11 (`same` + `source_calls_it: partial`) or decision 5 (`contra`, opposite) govern a safety entry?
- A later class member's model run after the class answer was known in patients (Huang 2012 KH902 in STZ rats, after DA VINCI 2011 showed aflibercept works in DME): is it a prospective prediction for that member (`same`, as drafted) or a class-level retrodiction under decision 10?
- Dating under decision 7: a phase 2 trial whose primary endpoint is the same measure as the phase 3 (DA VINCI BCVA at week 24) makes the comparison public three years before VIVID/VISTA. Confirm that a phase 2 primary endpoint counts for an efficacy `same` and sets `year`.
- models check: Decision 10 vs the dose-cell post-hoc rule (#50/#118, decision 16): a sponsor popPK fitted to the pivotal trial's own plasma PK whose unobserved ocular-compartment output 'explains' the regimen that held (aflibercept 8 mg, 15 vs 8.7 weeks) - `retro` or `untested` with `source_calls_it: scaling`? The dose rule was applied as the more specific and later-decided one.
- models check: Decision 7 dating of an FDA review: its signature/approval year (761355 ClinPharmR signed 2023-04-21, approval 2023-08-18) or its Drugs@FDA posting folder year (nda/2024)? The tile dates dose 4 by approval; the same convention was applied.
- models check: Does `retro` require a non-null `magnitude`? Decision 10 rests on reproduction, decision 11 allows the null bin; engagement 4 (Luu 2020) is retro with null because only the abstract and the EMA's PK-fit sentence are readable.
- models check: Is a `retro` whose stated reproduction is drug concentration in aqueous humour (PK) admissible in the engagement cell, or does it belong in dose with engagement untested until a measured-VEGF comparison is shown?

Missed comparisons:

- Applicant population-PK (in silico) model for aflibercept 8 mg: time to ocular LLOQ 8.7 weeks (2 mg) -> 15 weeks (8 mg), flagged unconfirmed by the reviewer, held in PHOTON q12/q16; no track, yet dose entry 4 borrows its number. Source: https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2024/761355Orig1s000ClinPharmR.pdf (bundle sources/761355/761355Orig1s000ClinPharmR.txt lines 289-290).
- Abicipar mechanistic translational PK/PD model (rabbit -> human, refitted to nAMD/DME patient aqueous VEGF): Luu 2020, doi 10.1124/jpet.119.263178, PMID 32098861. The draft excluded it as a refit; under decision 10 a refit after patient data is a retrodiction (retro or contra) and is tracked, not dropped.
- DA VINCI phase 2 (Do 2011, doi 10.1016/j.ophtha.2011.02.018, PMID 21546089, epub 2011-05-05; 52-week Do 2012, doi 10.1016/j.ophtha.2012.02.010): the first public DME-patient comparison for the rat-leak track (BCVA primary endpoint met, CRT fell) and for the decay-model track (2q8 arm). Not a model, but it redates engagement 2, dose 2 and efficacy 2 to 2011.
- Direct-tier engagement in DME patients: aqueous VEGF after intravitreal aflibercept in treatment-naive DME (doi 10.1089/jop.2020.0038, PMID 32589498, 2020) could test the decay model's VEGF-binding prediction in this context; optional, the rat model never measured VEGF.

## dry-eye-syndrome-anti-tnf-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Retraction after the comparison date: Donnenfeld 2024 was admissible when the RELIEF release paired it (2024-06-10) and retracted 2024-10-01. Decision 7 (score on what was known at the entry's date) and decision 2/#126 (author-withdrawn retraction inadmissible) pull opposite ways; I applied #126 because it was decided on this paper, but the brief does not say which wins when the retraction post-dates the entry.
- Patient-level (human_genetic) prediction tested in a prespecified subgroup: is the governing test the subgroup's own primary-family endpoint, or a carrier-vs-non-carrier interaction? And does a symptom-derived genotype model count as tested when the next trial reads out signs only?
- May the opening untested entry of a track cite an inadmissible (retracted) paper as src purely as the model_first_use_year proxy (#74 says reliance stands, #126 says inadmissible as a source)? If yes, lint should require retracted: true on it.
- Cross-indication PK as a dose source: decision 12 says other-indication comparisons transfer only for safety, yet the dose cell scores Thiel 2013 cataract-surgery patients as healthy volunteers without imported_from. Is 'otherwise healthy' surgical PK a healthy-volunteer study (admissible) or a cross-indication import (safety only)?
- readout_tier for elimination of free target protein in tissue (ocular-surface TNF-alpha): 'direct' (free target) or 'proximal' (target's product)? Decision 15 lists 'free target' under direct; the draft uses proximal.
- models: decision 10's text requires a result that 'post-dates AND is motivated by' the patient result; the drafter brief (line 40) and MODELS_CHECK_TASK use the temporal test alone. Kelagere 2023 post-dates the VELOS-1/2 registry postings by 16+ months but is not motivated by the miss: it calls tanfanercept 'currently in Phase III' and cites only the VELOS-1 ARVO abstract as positive. Temporal test: retrodiction true, contra (as drafted). Conjunctive text: late prospective model, contra with retrodiction false. Status, year and headline are identical either way (decision 6 bars any track that predicted after the patient result from the headline); only the mark and the tally differ. Which test governs, and is a contra retrodiction counted with prospective contras or apart like retro?
- models: dating a retrodiction's comparison entry. Decision 7 takes the earliest of registry-posted year (2022) and epub year, but the comparison cannot exist before the model side (2023); the drafter used 2023. Decision 7 should say a retrodiction's comparison entry is dated no earlier than its model source.
- models: flag-only disagreements (retrodiction, readout_tier) have no adjudication path, because the queue keys on status_draft != status_checked; a confirmed verdict proposing a flag change is never applied.
- models: contra scored on a model whose readouts (goblet cells, tear cytokines, lacrimal foci) were never VELOS endpoints and whose stated claim is confined to 'age-related dry eye'. I hold contra at medium: decision 4 asks whether the trial primary responded as predicted, the model predicted benefit, three placebo-controlled sign/symptom primaries were null, the registry attributes the miss to nothing the model never tested (#35/#72), and the paper itself cites the unselected VELOS-1 population as support, which makes the prediction class-level. Confirm that an age-defined animal model is class-level, not a patient-level population prediction left untested by an unstratified trial.

## endometriosis-gnrhr-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Efficacy from PD-only models: can a model with no disease readout (knock-in mouse uterine weight; monkey E2/menses) score efficacy against a pain primary endpoint, or is that scoring the oestrogen-deprivation hypothesis rather than the model? The draft scores the mouse same and the monkey untested on identical evidence; the rules decide neither.
- Insensitive null as a prediction: the mouse measured bone density and found no change, but its OVX positive control also showed none (4 weeks). Is that a stated prediction of no harm (contra when patients lost BMD) or an inadmissible comparison (untested)?
- Decision 16 gap: the model stated an absolute liver margin <1 at the clinical exposure (EPAR), yet patients were dosed to 200 mg with only slight LFT excess. Is dose same with source_calls_it partial/over_predicted, or contra with magnitude opposite for a misplaced margin in the conservative direction?
- Retrodiction with an unknown run date: the linzagolix rat study is in the MAA primary-PD package (EPAR 2022) and was published in 2023, after the 2018 phase 2b readout; its run date is not public. Default prospective (same) or retro?
- Must every track open in every cell? The rat track appears only in efficacy and the tox track only in dose/safety; lint accepts this, the brief's 'each cell opens at model_first_use_year' is ambiguous.
- Which area spelling is canonical: tiles.json 'endocrine' or the contexts' capitalised 'Endocrine'?

Missed comparisons:

- Patient-derived xenograft ('murine avatar') of human endometrioma lesions in NOD/SCID mice dosed with the GnRH antagonist cetrorelix: low-PR lesions respond more to GnRH antagonism than to MPA (F S Sci 2025, PMID 39393571, doi 10.1016/j.xfss.2024.10.004). A patient-level track for this mechanism with a non-member peptide sibling (product_in_model false, modality outside the tile); no patient comparison yet, so untested, but it is a model compared-in-principle with patients and should be listed.
- Rat autotransplant track has no dose entry although Tezuka 2023 (PMID 37186405) states a model exposure target: 'The effective concentration of linzagolix, calculated as the free form of the last-observed drug concentration, was ~1 umol/L in endometriosis model rats.' Comparable to human free exposure at 200 mg (EPAR gives ~3-fold free-drug margin); at least an untested opening entry, possibly same.
- Knock-in mouse reversibility prediction (Nakata 2014: gonadal function recovered within 14 days of withdrawal) matched by faster menses return on relugolix vs leuprorelin (Osuga 2022, PMID 34895700). A held prediction on the hgnrhr-knockin-mouse track; does not lift the bone contra but belongs in the record.
- Nothing missed on failures: no endometriosis press-release or EMA failure for relugolix or linzagolix (EDELWEISS 2 termination already noted); opigolix/ASP1707 has no preclinical model on PubMed or Europe PMC (16 hits, all clinical or reviews), confirming the drafter's 'no public model'.

## follicular-lymphoma-cd19-directed-depleters

Rule questions:

- Species-silence exemption: v3 safety says silence is a miss on 'a harm the species could have shown' and in the next sentence that 'a mouse cannot show cytokine release'. Mice do show CRS (Cheadle 2014 syngeneic CD19 CAR; Giavridis and Norelli 2018 at high tumour burden). Is the exemption per species or per model-as-run (tumour burden, conditioning)? Does rule 7 (no later knowledge) bar using 2014/2018 demonstrations of species capability to score a 2011 silence? The myeloma BCMA tile used the species reading; this draft needs the same answer.
- Patient-side drug identity: does the academic predecessor construct (NCI FMC63-CD28-CD3zeta CAR, later licensed as KTE-C19) count as the member drug axicabtagene ciloleucel, so that Kochenderfer 2012's three FL patients score the class? If not, engagement/efficacy/safety at 2011 stay untested until ZUMA-5 (2021), which compares with no model.
- readout_tier for a depleter whose PD readout is the disappearance of the target-bearing cell (B-cell counts, hypogammaglobulinemia): direct (free target gone), proximal, or downstream?
- Mitigation trials in the safety cell: does a prophylaxis trial (anakinra, Park 2023) count as a comparison of the model's safety prediction when the prediction is about the harm's mechanism (monocyte IL-1) rather than the class's harm itself? When a two-part prediction (abolishes CRS and neurotoxicity) half-holds, is it same + source_calls_it partial, or contra?
- Member list completeness: tisagenlecleucel (Kymriah FL indication, ELARA, FDA 2022-05-27) and tafasitamab (CD19-directed cytolytic antibody; inMIND, FL approval 2025, verify) are CD19-directed depleters with FL patient data but are not in the tile's members. The drafter rightly did not widen; is the CD19|depleter FL member list complete, and does 'depleter' include CD19 mAbs?

Missed comparisons:

- Syngeneic BALB/c A20 lymphoma with anti-mouse CD19 CD28-CD3zeta CARs (Cheadle 2014, J Immunol, https://doi.org/10.4049/jimmunol.1302148, epub 2014-03-12): T-cell-dose-dependent acute toxicity with raised serum Th1 cytokines in preconditioned mice, absent in C57BL/6 and C3H recipients. A 2014 immunocompetent-mouse cytokine-toxicity retrodiction that bears directly on the C3H model's silence; no track.
- Second 2010 syngeneic system: BALB/c A20 lymphoma with the amCD19z CAR (Cheadle 2010, J Immunol, https://doi.org/10.4049/jimmunol.0901440, epub 2010-01-20): eradicated disseminated A20 after 6 Gy TBI, but CAR T cells failed to persist, B cells returned, no autotoxicity. A prospective persistence/B-cell-aplasia prediction that the 2011-2012 patients contradicted; not relied on by a member, but a compared model with no track (untested or contra).
- Humanized NSG-SGM3 patient-derived ALL xenograft CRS/neuroinflammation model with GM-CSF neutralization (Sterner 2019, Blood, https://doi.org/10.1182/blood-2018-10-881722, epub 2018-11-21, PMC6376281): a retrodiction with a prospective test in ZUMA-19 (NCT04314843, lenzilumab + axi-cel in LBCL; results posted 2024-03-04; phase 2 primary = grade >=2 neurologic events within 28 days), imported from LBCL. No track.
- The Giavridis track's own prospective test: 'IL-1 blockade abates CRS' was tested in Park 2023 (https://doi.org/10.1038/s41591-023-02404-6: all-grade CRS 74%, severe 6.4%, LBCL/MCL) and in https://doi.org/10.1182/bloodadvances.2024015161 (2025: once-daily anakinra insufficient to prevent grade >=2 CRS/NT with axi-cel). Belongs on scid-beige-raji-crs-mouse as a dated entry (same + partial, or contra), not only on the Norelli track.
- The safety cell has no entry for the liso-cel track (raji-nsg-xenograft-fmc63-41bb-car). The Breyanzi SBRA (https://www.fda.gov/media/146242/download) states: 'Animal studies to assess the long-term safety of BREYANZI were limited because BREYANZI does not survive in immunocompetent rodents and induces graft-versus-host disease in immunocompromised mice.' An untested 2015 opener with that quote.
- Not found (reported, not a miss): no press release, SEC filing or EMA document reporting a member failure in FL (ZUMA-5, TRANSCEND FL, RELIANCE all positive); no FL patient-derived organoid or ex-vivo system used to pick CAR-T patients surfaced in Europe PMC. I ran no dedicated WebSearch, so an FL-specific ex-vivo model could still exist.

## follicular-lymphoma-pik3cd-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Multi-organ toxicology package where most organs matched (liver, GI, lung, lymphoid) and two predicted harms (cardiac, pancreatic) never appeared, seen in rats only at AUCs above clinical exposure with no multiple stated: decision 5 (contra, magnitude opposite, 'a conservative model is still a wrong model') and decision 11 (same + source_calls_it partial, 'one readout matched, one did not') give different answers. Which governs, and does a finding confined to supra-clinical exposure count as a stated prediction for patients at the clinical dose?
- Engagement scored on another indication's patients (CLL phase-1 chemokine and pAkt PD) for an FL context with no FL-patient PD readout: safety has imported_from, engagement and dose do not. Should imported_from extend to engagement and dose, or is the FL engagement cell untested until an FL-patient readout exists?
- Dose where the clinical dose was chosen from a tumour-shrinkage plateau and the regulator afterwards checked troughs against an in-vitro EC90 that pre-dated the pivotal trial: is that a reached model target (same) or a post-hoc PK/PD justification (untested, source_calls_it scaling) under decisions 4 and 16?
- A genetic model that predates the drug class (D910A mouse 2002; first PI3Kdelta inhibitor ~2007): does its track open at the model's creation or at the class's first use, given decision 7 says a model introduced mid-cell opens at its first class use?
- outcome_tested when the confirmatory outcome trials were run but terminated with the prespecified analyses never conducted and the indication later withdrawn: null (never run) hides this; a value such as not_conducted or negative_confounded may be needed.
- [models] Decision 10 timing: Eschweiler's mouse (epub 2022-05-04) precedes the tier-1 patient record of zandelisib's intermittent cohort (Pagel, epub 2022-07-11) but follows the regimen decision (TIDAL registered 2018-12-07) and the press/conference result (TIDAL topline 2021-11-30). dose[4] scored retro and dose[5] folded into patient_trials on 'known' meaning any public record or a design fixed before the model; if 'known' means a tier-1 source, dose[4] becomes same. Which reading?

Missed comparisons:

- Mouse PI3Kdelta-inhibitor colitis and intermittent-dosing model: Eschweiler et al. 2022, Nature, https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-022-04685-2 (PMID 35508656). Non-member PI3Kdelta inhibitors (AMG319, PI-3065) depleted colonic ST2 Treg cells and caused colitis in mice; intermittent dosing curbed irAEs. The paper sets this beside AMG319 patients (12/21 discontinued for irAEs), and the regimen prediction was then tested prospectively in FL by zandelisib's intermittent schedule (TIDAL NCT03768505, results 2024-02-01; grade 3-4 colitis 3%, Zelenetz 2024). Needs a dose (regimen) and a safety track, product_in_model false.
- Human PIK3CD loss-of-function genotype (model_kind human_genetic, retrodiction): a patient expressing kinase-dead p110delta, J Allergy Clin Immunol, epub 2018-10-16, https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jaci.2018.10.005 (PMID 30336224); autosomal-recessive PIK3CD deficiency with immunodeficiency, autoimmune thrombocytopenia and enterocolitis, Haematologica 2019, https://doi.org/10.3324/haematol.2018.208397 (PMID 31073077). These reproduce the idelalisib colitis and infection phenotype after the clinic knew it: a retro safety track alongside the D910A mouse.
- Zandelisib's own animal model: 'Absorption, Distribution, and Binding Profile of ME-401 ... in Animal Models', Target Oncol 2019, https://doi.org/10.1007/s11523-019-00668-y (PMID 31506873). A20 syngeneic mouse B-cell lymphoma with tumour concentrations 20-30x plasma persisting at 24 h and ~100x slower dissociation than idelalisib (used as the reference compound); this is the member drug's stated basis for intermittent dosing, with TIDAL as the patient side. No dose track for it.
- Human regulatory T-cell ex-vivo assay with phenotyping of idelalisib-treated CLL patients: Chellappa et al. 2019, J Immunol, https://doi.org/10.4049/jimmunol.1701703 (PMID 30692213). A stated comparison (Treg IC50 0.5 uM vs CD4 Teff 2.0 uM and CD8 Teff 6.5 uM; patient Treg phenotyping 'supported our in vitro findings'). The draft cites it in a judgement but gives it no track; it is a retro ex_vivo safety track distinct from the B-cell system.

## gastric-cancer-anti-erbb2-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Dating (rule 6): do congress abstracts published in journal supplements with a DOI (ESMO 2017 JACOB, ASCO 2018 CP-MGAH22-05, ESMO 2025 KC-WISE LBA) or sponsor press releases set `year`, or only registry posting, online journal publication, label and print? Three entries here move by a year on the answer.
- Dose track attribution: when the programme's exposure target (pertuzumab 20 ug/mL trough) was stated by a different model system (breast xenografts) than the efficacy model on the track (N87 gastric xenograft), does 'reached, failed, abandoned' put contra on a separate track for the model that stated the target, or stay untested on the efficacy model's track (the draft's own treatment of KN026)?
- Patient-level predictions in single-arm combination trials: is an exploratory genotype-by-response tabulation (5 responders, margetuximab plus pembrolizumab, no wild-type-Fc comparator) an admissible comparison for `same` when the model's claim was a differential (158F carriers gain most), or does it need a comparator arm?
- A retrodiction model (Ughetto 2021 PDX) that contradicts the class-level patient result while proposing an untested patient-level selection rule: one track with a class-level contra entry plus a patient-level untested entry, or two prediction levels on one entry?
- Member list: zanidatamab, a biparatopic anti-ERBB2 antibody with a phase 3 in HER2-positive GEA (HERIZON-GEA-01, NCT05152147; reported 2025), is absent from the tile's members while the biparatopic anbenitamab is included - intended split by modality group?

Missed comparisons:

- HER2 'hyper'-amplified (>=8 copies) gastric patient-derived xenografts run as 'preclinical randomized trials' with trastuzumab plus pertuzumab after JACOB had failed: Ughetto et al., Gastric Cancer 2021, doi 10.1007/s10120-021-01165-w (PMID 33755862) - dual blockade gave complete durable responses in 5/8 models and the authors set this against the negative trials. A retrodiction track for pertuzumab (retrodiction: true, contra at class level since it did not reproduce JACOB) carrying an untested patient-level prediction (HER2-hyper-amplified subgroup).
- CD16A-genotype x response analysis in the gastric margetuximab trial: Catenacci et al., ASCO 2018 abstract 4030 (doi 10.1200/JCO.2018.36.15_suppl.4030), carried forward in Catenacci 2020 Lancet Oncol - the comparison the fc-optimised-adcc-cd16a-158f track's patient-level prediction needed (scored above as efficacy #4 corrected).
- Earlier public records of KC-WISE on the CRIB track: ESMO Congress 2025 LBA oral (17 Oct 2025, Alphamab press release, PR Newswire) and the phase II part Zhao et al., Cancer Commun 2025, doi 10.1002/cac2.70080 (PMID 41273757) - change no status, move the year to 2025.
- JACOB primary result first public at ESMO 2017 (abstract 616O; ASCO Post 2017-11-25) - changes no status, moves the 2018 JACOB entries to 2017 if congress abstracts count for dating.

## gastric-cancer-parp1-2-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Dating with tier 5: evidence rule 6 names registry posting, online publication, label or print as the dates that set `year`, but rule 2 says a phase 3 failure known from a press release must be found. When a sponsor press release reports the primary-endpoint miss 18 months (GOLD, 2016-05-18) or 24 months (PARALLEL 303, 2021-06-04) before the tier-1 paper, does `year` follow the press release while the tier-1 paper sets the status, or the first tier 1-3 record? Both corrections above turn on this.
- Cross-indication inference as a track: pamiparib entered gastric cancer with no disease model (rationale = PARPi approvals elsewhere + HR-gene mutation frequency). The brief says 'no disease model relied on -> empty list', yet `model_kind` offers `cross_indication`. Is a cross-indication inference a scoreable track (contra on the PARALLEL 303 miss, as drafted), or should the pamiparib miss be left out of the figure / recorded only as a patient trial? The draft itself invites the reviewer to drop it.
- Dose/efficacy coupling when the model stated no exposure target: the only model is in-vitro viability; the clinical regimen was 100 mg BID with paclitaxel (a third of the monotherapy dose, neutropenia-limited) and the sponsor's 2016 press release attributes the GOLD miss to 'a low dose and in combination with chemotherapy'. Does the coupling row 'never reached because of toxicity; no stated margin -> untested/untested' apply (which would also make efficacy untested), or does efficacy stay contra because the trial was the direct prospective test of the model's ATM prediction and no model source set a target? Decision 13 (data, not the sponsor's reading) and the checker rule on 'conditions the model never tested' pull in opposite directions.

Missed comparisons:

- Olaparib + ramucirumab NCI10066 (Br J Cancer 2023-12-22, doi:10.1038/s41416-023-02534-1, PMID 38135713; NCT03008278, results posted 2025-06-04): the trial paper's own preclinical model (tumour hypoxia induces HRD, sensitising to PARPi) compared with patients, single-arm phase 1/2, ORR 6/43 (14%) at RP2D 300 mg BID, HRD-mutant subgroup non-significant. A member drug's own model with no track.
- Rucaparib, the third class member, is absent from the draft: NCT03995017 (rucaparib + ramucirumab +/- nivolumab, phase 1/2, n=34, completed 2022-10-19, no results posted, status UNKNOWN). Nothing scoreable, but 'finding nothing is a finding' and the note should say so.
- AstraZeneca press release 2016-05-18 'AstraZeneca provides top-line results from Lynparza GOLD trial in advanced gastric cancer' (tier 5): OS miss in overall and ATM-negative populations public 18 months before the Lancet Oncol epub; sponsor attributes it to 'a low dose and in combination with chemotherapy'. https://www.astrazeneca.com/media-centre/press-releases/2016/astrazeneca-provides-top-line-results-from-lynparza-gold-trial-in-advanced-gastric-cancer-1805206.html
- BeiGene press release 2021-06-04 (BusinessWire 20210604005079, ASCO 2021 poster) reporting PARALLEL 303 PFS miss (HR 0.799, p=0.1428) and that the study was launched as a phase 3 (planned ~540) and closed at 136 for slow enrolment and a change in standard of care; tier 5, two years before the 2023 paper.
- MEDIOLA gastric cohort (olaparib + durvalumab, ASCO GI 2019 abstract, doi:10.1200/JCO.2019.37.4_suppl.140, ORR 10%, n=39): conference abstract only, not an admissible tier; listed so the drafter knows it was looked at. Changes no status.
- Searched and not found: any gastric patient-derived organoid or PDX compared with patient response for a PARP inhibitor (Europe PMC title search 2014-2026: 11 hits, all cell-line mechanistic; the 2026 PDO-PDX concordance paper PMID 42674710 is chemotherapy only). The cell-line track is therefore the only gastric model track; the draft is right not to add one.

## gastroesophageal-reflux-disease-atp4a-blockers

Rule questions:

- Efficacy: when the only published disease model (Kim 2019 rat GERD) was submitted after the phase 3 finished and after approval, and its run date is unpublished, is it prospective or `retrodiction: true` (retro rather than same)? The rules key on run date, which no source gives.
- Safety: if the class relied on an unpublished regulatory tox package and the only public animal safety study was run post-approval, should the cell stay `untested` on the unpublished package (model_first_use_year pre-clinic, unknown) or be built on the post-approval study as the draft does? And is that study then a retrodiction of the comparable-AE phase 3 data?
- Dose: does a model that states only an effective oral mg/kg (no exposure or occupancy target) count as stating a dose target, so that 'reached, drug worked' gives `same`, or does it stay `untested` as drafted?

Missed comparisons:

- No unreported failure found: Sebela press release 2025-04-23 (biospace.com/press-releases/sebela-pharmaceuticals-announces-positive-topline-results-from-phase-3-triumph-program-of-tegoprazan-in-gerd) reports all primary endpoints met in TRIUMpH EE and NERD; could be added to patient_trials (tier 5), no status change.
- Fexuprazan phase III EE head-to-head (Jeong 2024, J Clin Med, doi:10.3390/jcm13113262) is RETRACTED; correctly absent, worth a note so it is not re-added.
- Tegoprazan Korean NERD phase 3 and TRIUMpH NERD (GERD patients) are additional efficacy patient_trials; no status change.
- Zastaprazan (JP-1366; Korean approval 2024 for erosive oesophagitis) is a Korean P-CAB the tile does not list; its only model found is a GERD-patient popPK model (doi:10.1002/psp4.13228, 2024) that describes exposure, not acid or healing, so no track needed unless zastaprazan is added to the class.

## hemophilia-a-f8-replacement-gene-therapy

Rule questions:

- Where is a durability failure scored when every primary endpoint and PoC gate was met? Valoctocogene lost ~50% FVIII from year 1 to 2 and giroctocogene fell 42.6% to 25.4%, yet ABR stayed down; decision 4 (efficacy = primary endpoint) cannot hold the dog-track miss, and engagement asks only whether the readout moved. Efficacy #4 turns on this.
- Does the checker-brief bar 'result attributed to conditions the model never tested' extend to a mechanism the species cannot mount — capsid-directed T-cell hepatitis in tolerant hFVIII-transgenic mice or in macaques? If yes, efficacy #7 (BAX 888) and safety #2 (ALT rise) become untested; if no, both contra stand.
- Decision 5: is a model harm that the sponsor and regulator attribute to xenogeneic immunogenicity (monkey anti-drug antibodies, acquired hemophilia, one death on efanesoctocog alfa) a 'predicted harm that never came' (contra, opposite) or not a stated human prediction (same)? Safety #4 turns on this; untested seems wrong either way.
- Is a secondary FVIII-activity endpoint in a phase 1/2 gene-therapy dose-escalation trial whose registry primary is adverse events only (NCT03370172, NCT03003533) a 'pre-specified proof-of-concept gate' by default, or must the sponsor name it as the decision point in a source?
- Decision 16: does a mouse toxicology margin of 1.09x (mortality at 6.53e13 vg/kg against a 6e13 vg/kg clinical dose) count as a stated margin that held (dose #2, margin_stated true)?
- A platform harm (AAV capsid hepatotoxicity with ALT rise) was already shown in hemophilia B AAV2 patients (Manno 2006) and missed by dogs and macaques before any F8 member was dosed: does `other` (same platform) open the gene-therapy safety strip in 2006, or is cross-class evidence set aside under decision 13 and the strip opens at 2017 as drafted?
- Does a retrodiction track need an opening untested entry (engagement #4, same source and year as the retro entry), and if so should it carry retrodiction: true when lint only allows retro/contra on retrodiction entries?
- Is a 3-patient terminated descriptive study reporting registry counts only on a secondary outcome (NCT03520712, 1/3 FVIII >=5 IU/dL at week 26) a patient result for an engagement `same` (engagement #7), given decision 10's 'a non-significant trend in a terminated trial is not a patient result'?

Missed comparisons:

- Mouse unfolded-protein-response / ER-stress prediction for high FVIII expression (Lange 2016, doi:10.1038/mtm.2016.64, PMID 27738645: transient UPR activation at >200% FVIII after AAV in hemophilia A mice) versus patient biopsies in Fong 2022 (doi:10.1038/s41591-022-01751-0: 'no endoplasmic reticulum stress was detected in hepatocytes expressing hFVIII-SQ protein') — a safety comparison on the mouse track that the draft never scores (decision 5 contra/opposite, or same if only BioMarin's own mice are the track).
- Dog on-target safety prediction: no long-term FVIII inhibitor after liver gene transfer (Sabatino 2011, doi:10.1038/mt.2010.240: 'no long-term immune response to FVIII was observed in any of the dogs (1/9 dogs had a transient inhibitor)'; also Finn 2010, doi:10.1182/blood-2010-06-288001) versus no FVIII inhibitors in GENEr8-1 (Roctavian SBRA, https://www.fda.gov/media/170455/download) — a pooled on-target safety entry on the dog track; the drafter noted it as 'uncompared in print' but v3 rule 1 allows the pairing.
- Dog genotoxicity prediction: clonal expansion of AAV-transduced hepatocytes in 5/6 dogs at 10 years (Nguyen 2021, doi:10.1038/s41587-020-0741-7, PMID 33199875) versus NHP integration analysis showing no clonal expansion (Roctavian SBRA nonclinical section) and no hepatic tumours reported in patients to date — the dog track has no safety entry at all.
- Post-clinic nonhuman-primate models of AAV-FVIII expression loss (IL-15 blockade + rapamycin rescue of 'multifactorial loss of factor VIII'), pointed to by the review doi:10.1177/20406207251406537 (Ther Adv Hematol, 2026) — a candidate retrodiction track (decision 10, status retro) that the draft does not carry; primary paper not resolved within budget.
- Peboctocogene camaparvovec (BAY 2599023 / DTX201, Bayer/Ultragenyx, AAVhu37; WHO INN proposed list 124) is a tile member with no entry and no model named; its phase 1/2 (NCT03588299) and preclinical model were not searched by the drafter.
- Regulator action on the durability miss: FDA Complete Response Letter for valoctocogene, August 2020, requesting two-year GENEr8-1 data (BioMarin press release, tier 5), and BioMarin's 2025 withdrawal of Roctavian from the US market (https://www.bleeding.org/news/biomarin-pulls-roctavian-hemophilia-a-gene-therapy-from-us-market) — neither changes a status but both belong in the efficacy note.
- Member-drug program stops with no model comparison, worth a note: Pfizer ceased giroctocogene development despite AFFINE meeting its primary endpoint (Sangamo press release, https://investor.sangamo.com/news-releases/news-release-details/sangamo-therapeutics-regain-full-rights-hemophilia-gene-therapy); Roche withdrew the SPK-8011 phase 3 (https://www.fiercebiotech.com/biotech/roche-mothballs-another-hemophilia-gene-therapy-under-spark-it-plans-debut-new-hematologic) and discontinued SPK-8016.
- Kistner 2024 (doi:10.1038/s41434-023-00423-z, PMID 37833563, epub 2023-10-13): cynomolgus macaques given AAVrh10-hFVIII under prophylactic prednisolone, rapamycin or combinations (Ultragenyx/Penn); prednisolone alone raised hFVIII but 'this was not sustained upon taper'. The drafter set it aside as a sibling capsid asking a regimen question, but decision 8 allows proxies (product_in_model: false) and the dose cell scores regimen predictions, with GENEr8-3 prophylactic corticosteroids (NCT04323098, results 2024-04-04) as the patient test; candidate dose/engagement track, retro if motivated by the GENEr8-1 steroid data.

## hemophilia-b-f9-replacement-gene-therapy

Rule questions:

- Best-model rule vs class members on different models: when one member (BAX 335, mouse model) is contradicted and another (fidanacogene, dog model) matches in the same cell and same year range, the cell shows only the dog's same. Should a different member's contra on a different model of the same kind (AAV8 FIX-Padua, liver) be shown?
- Two dog systems for fidanacogene: Crudele 2015 AAV8-cFIX-Padua (cited by George 2017) vs the AAVRh74var-cFIX-R338L surrogate FDA reviewed. Are these one model_id (same species, transgene, route) or two, and which is 'the model the class relied on' for dose?
- Year of a pairing whose patient side was public first by press release (Dec 2022) and model side only in the SBRA (Apr 2024): rule 6 does not say whether the year is the later of the two dates.

Missed comparisons:

- DTX101 (AAVrh10-hFIX, a class member): Phase 1/2 101HEMB01 missed its primary endpoint (peak FIX >=20 IU/dL at week 6; median 7 and 10 IU/dL) and the program was dropped (Dimension press release Nov 2017; Blood Adv 2025, PMID 40197980, PMC12197983). The paper names no preclinical model; if Dimension's NHP/mouse data predicted >=20%, efficacy and gene-therapy dose take a contra (2017, tier 5 then tier 1). Not entered in the draft at all, not even in patient_trials.
- AskBio009/BAX 335 (Konkle 2021, PMID 33067633): FIX not sustained beyond 5-11 weeks in 7 of 8 patients; first public in Baxalta 2015-2016 releases and ASH abstracts. Only mentioned in judgement/patient_trials; a dated engagement/dose entry against its own preclinical model (Monahan 2015 mice/NHP) would show the miss even if the best-model rule leaves the cell at same.
- verbrinacogene setparvovec (FLT180a, Chowdary 2022 NEJM 10.1056/NEJMoa2119913): needed glucocorticoids +/- tacrolimus, one AV-fistula thrombosis at FIX 260%; development paused 2022 (Freeline). Its preclinical (NHP) model was not read by the drafter or by me.
- AAV5-hFIX (AMT-060, Miesbach 2018 Blood 10.1182/blood-2017-09-804419): FIX 4.4-6.9 IU/dL; no comparison with its NHP predictions was looked for (dose cell, gene therapy).
- Fidanacogene predecessor (AAVRh74var-hFIX-Padua) GLP toxicology in healthy mice: 6 test-article-related unscheduled deaths (brain/skin pathology, read as exacerbated tissue/vascular injury in animals with endogenous FIX) at doses 2-10x below the human dose; no such harm in patients. Safety track absent from the draft (FDA SBRA Beqvez, https://www.fda.gov/media/178688/download, nonclinical section).

## hepatitis-c-virus-infection-hcv-ns3-4a-protease-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Dose table 'reached, drug worked': does a phase 1 viral-load response count as 'worked', so the entry is dated at phase 1, or must the efficacy primary endpoint have read out first? Also, can a dose entry be 'same' when the source never reports exposure against the model's numeric target (EC99)?
- Q80K: the replicon fold change was measured on baseline clinical isolates at the same time as the phase 3 trials. Is this a prospective model prediction or a retrodiction (retro)?
- Hepatotoxicity seen only under a drug interaction (sovaprevir with atazanavir/r in healthy volunteers; paritaprevir with ethinyl estradiol): no rule says whether a DDI-driven harm the animals were never dosed under counts as the model's silence being contradicted or as untested.
- Models check: a routine preclinical model (HCV chimpanzee monotherapy) whose run date is unstated and whose first record (2010) post-dates a public patient result (AASLD 2008), but which was not motivated by it. Rule 10 requires both 'post-dates' and 'motivated by'; scored retro here on the record date.

Missed comparisons:

- Efficacy: vaniprevir phase II (Manns 2012 Hepatology, doi:10.1002/hep.25743, epub 2012-07-17): primary endpoint RVR 68.8-83.3% vs 5.6% placebo (P<0.001). An earlier member 'same' (paired with vaniprevir replicon data), so the efficacy cell turns 'same' in 2012, not 2013.
- Dose: glecaprevir 6-month rat NOAEL gives a 70x exposure margin at 300 mg (209394 PharmR: 'This provides an exposure margin of 70X'), which held in patients. This is a margin_stated 'same' (2017) and could carry the dose cell if dose entry 2 is not accepted.
- Safety (no status change expected): FDA clinical hold on sovaprevir, June 2013, for grade 3-4 ALT elevations in a healthy-volunteer DDI study with atazanavir/ritonavir (Achillion 10-K, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1070336/000119312514089075/d645073d10k.htm; https://www.fiercepharma.com/regulatory/fda-slaps-a-clinical-hold-on-achillion-hep-c-drug). The harm came from conditions the model never tested, so 'untested'; the draft's judgement should mention it.

## hepatitis-c-virus-infection-hcv-ns5a-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Dose cell timing: when the model's exposure target is reached and a 14-day monotherapy viral-load decline is near-maximal (Nettles 2011), is that 'reached, drug worked' -> same in 2011, or must dose `same` wait for the SVR primary endpoint (Lok 2012)? The draft's judgement uses Lok 2012 and ALLY-3, which rule 7 forbids; the 2011 source alone supports only PD, not efficacy.
- Retrodiction with a split result: one retrodiction paper (Shi 2013) reproduced the known human result in genotype 1b (retro) and predicted failure in genotype 2, where patients were cured under a different duration and NS5B partner (4 weeks + NNI in mice; 24 weeks + sofosbuvir in Sulkowski 2014). Is the GT2 comparison `untested` (conditions the model never tested), `contra` on the mouse track (a retrodiction that failed to reproduce human data, overriding retro by the 'one contra beats same' rule), or a fresh prospective entry under decision 10 if the GT2 human data were not yet public in January 2013?
- Safety 'predicted harm': an adaptive, reversible animal finding (adrenal cortical hypertrophy with raised corticosterone in rat and monkey at ~0.8-2.3x the human AUC) that the regulator treated as a monitoring item and cleared on cortisol. Does any target-organ finding near clinical exposure count as a predicted harm (contra, magnitude opposite, as drafted), or only one the model source or sponsor stated as a human risk (the lenient 'same' the draft mentions)? The answer flips the safety track.
- Track identity for toxicology: the draft pools every member drug's GLP repeat-dose package into one model_id (ns5a-animal-tox), so daclatasvir's adrenal contra (2015) overrides ledipasvir's same (2014) via 'one drug's contra beats another drug's same on the same track'. Is species-matched repeat-dose toxicology one system across drugs, or does each drug's package form its own track?
- Dating by the earliest regulator: the daclatasvir adrenal/cortisol comparison was presumably in the EMA Daklinza EPAR (CHMP opinion mid-2014) before the FDA summary review (July 2015); rule 6 would date the contra to 2014. I could not verify (EPAR PDF download truncated twice); not retried.
- In-silico regimen-level models (CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models): the viral-kinetic model estimates whole-regimen effectiveness and would make the same per-patient duration call for any DAA regimen. Does a prospective prediction about an NS5A-containing combination (Etzion 2020: SOF/VEL, ELB/GRZ, SOF/LED, GLE/PIB) count as a class prediction because a member drug is in every regimen, as the tile's trial entries already assume, or must the model's prediction be attributable to the NS5A component (only the Guedj 2013 daclatasvir-monotherapy fit qualifies)?
- Track identity for humanized-liver mice (CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models): is host strain the system (TK-NOG, CIEA/Osaka vs cDNA-uPA/SCID, PhoenixBio/Hiroshima: different transgene, injury mechanism and immunodeficiency, same readout), or is 'human hepatocyte chimeric mouse' one platform? If merged, Kan 2015, Doi 2019 and Osawa 2019 join the uPA/SCID track; retro entries never set the headline, so only the 2015/2017 Kan prediction could change a headline.
- Per-patient miss inside a prospective patient-level prediction (CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models): Etzion 2020 cured 9 of 10 model-shortened patients and the primary SVR rate held (28/29). Decision 11 covers one readout matched and one not, not 9 of 10 subjects; is this same with source_calls_it: partial and a magnitude bin, or does a patient-level call carry the miss?

Missed comparisons:

- Humanized-mouse track, second prediction: Shi 2013 (doi:10.1136/gutjnl-2012-302600) also reports NS5A+NS3 and NS5A+NS5B combinations 'failed to eradicate the virus in mice infected with HCV genotypes 2a or 2b'; the draft says this was never tested with a member drug, but daclatasvir + sofosbuvir 24 weeks cured 92% (24/26) of genotype 2 patients (Sulkowski 2014, NEJM 370:211, doi:10.1056/NEJMoa1306218, NCT01359644; GT2/3 data presented 2012). Conditions differ (4 weeks + non-nucleoside BMS-821095 in mice vs 24 weeks + nucleotide sofosbuvir), so admissibility is rule question 2; either way it belongs on the track.
- Earlier regulator patient-level comparison on the replicon track: FDA Harvoni label Oct 2014 section 12.4 (http://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/label/2014/205834s000lbl.pdf; bundle sources/205834/205834s000lbl.txt) reports treatment-experienced GT1 subjects with baseline NS5A resistance-associated polymorphisms relapsed 22% (5/23) vs 2% (2/85) after 12 weeks and 0% after 24 weeks. Same status (same, patient-level) nine months before the Daklinza 2015 entry; rule 6 dates the first regulator use of the replicon RAS map to 2014.
- Humanized-mouse track, later member-drug retrodictions not in the draft (title/abstract level only, not read in full): 'Efficacy of glecaprevir and pibrentasvir treatment for genotype 1b HCV drug resistance-associated variants in humanized mice' (J Gen Virol 2019, doi:10.1099/jgv.0.001268, PMID 31199224) and 'Combinations of two drugs ... effective for HCV with NS5A-P32 deletion in humanized-liver mice' (J Gastroenterol 2019, doi:10.1007/s00535-018-01541-x, PMID 30684016); both model patient-derived NS5A-failure variants against clinical outcomes.
- In-silico track not drawn: the viral-kinetic model fitted to the Gao 2010 daclatasvir patient kinetics (Guedj et al., PNAS 2013, 'Modeling shows that the NS5A inhibitor daclatasvir has two modes of action and yields a shorter estimate of the hepatitis C virus half-life', doi:10.1073/pnas.1203110110) is a model_kind in_silico retrodiction compared with patient data for a member drug. Citation from memory, not re-opened in this check; verify before use.
- TK-NOG track, prospective patient-level prediction before the Doi 2019 retrodiction: Kan 2015 (J Gastroenterol 50:1145, doi:10.1007/s00535-015-1108-6, PMID 26208695, epub 2015-07-25; Osaka, TK-NOG) inoculated TK-NOG mice with DCV/ASV-failure serum (NS3 D168V, NS5A L31V+Y93H) and found ledipasvir + GS-558093 failed to achieve EOT or SVR while telaprevir + GS-558093 cured, when LDV/SOF retreatment of DCV/ASV failures was untested in patients. Patient tests followed in 2017 (PMIDs 28315983 J Gastroenterol, 28815329 J Gastroenterol, 28884930 and 28332272 Hepatol Res: LDV/SOF +/- RBV in DCV/ASV failures stratified by NS5A RAS); SVR numbers not read, so same vs contra is open. This moves the TK-NOG opener to 2015 (CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models).

## hepatitis-c-virus-infection-hcv-ns5b-non-nucleoside-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Sub-year ordering: on one track the filibuvir contra (registry 2014-01-27) precedes the dasabuvir same (pairable only from the Kati epub 2014-12-22), but lint orders by year and the draft lists same first. Should the schema carry a month/date, or should a December pairing be dated by print year 2015?
- A replicon-predicted antiviral that improved on-treatment response but failed the SVR primary through post-treatment relapse after a 24-week response-guided stop: contra (primary-endpoint rule) or untested (result attributed to a condition the model cannot test)?
- Is a viral-kinetic PK/PD model run in 2012 on already-published monotherapy results a model-system track at all; if so, is it retro under decision 10 even though its inputs (replicon EC50,PS and measured Cmin) existed before each trial?
- Which cell scores a resistance-pathway prediction (replicon-selected substitution later found in treated patients): efficacy, engagement, or none?
- Deleobuvir (BI 207127), a thumb-I NNI whose Phase 3 HCVerso programme was stopped and which Reddy 2012 names, is absent from the member list. In or out?
- (models) Does a table row quoted cell-by-cell (verbatim cell contents joined with '|') satisfy the quote rule's 'sentence ... verbatim', or does the absence of a prose sentence force untested? Chimpanzee engagement 2012 rests on Reddy 2012 Table 1.
- (models) Restates open question 4 for the chimpanzee track: a resistance-pathway prediction (sibling-selected NS5B substitutions recurring in dasabuvir-treated failures, no primary endpoint compared) scores in efficacy, engagement, or neither?
- (models) For model_first_use_year under #112 (proxy allowed), does a non-member molecule of the class from another sponsor count as first class use (HCV-796 in the chimeric mouse, Kneteman 2009), as A-837093 does for the chimpanzee, or must the proxy be a member's own sibling?
- (models) When one paper reports the model and the patient series together without stating which ran first (Teraoka 2018), does decision 10 default to prospective on the authors' framing or to retrodiction until the order is shown?

Missed comparisons:

- Chimpanzee (HCV-infected) dosed with A-837093, Abbott's palm-I benzothiadiazine sibling of dasabuvir: Long 2007 AAC doi:10.1128/AAC.00723-07 — 1.4/2.5 log10 decline (GT1a/1b) with rebound and C316Y/Y448H/G554D selection on 14-day monotherapy. Patient side: ABT-333/ABT-072 3-day monotherapy ~1.0-1.1 log10 (Reddy 2012 Table 1) and dasabuvir RAVs S556G/C316Y/M414T (Kati 2015; Krishnan 2015 AAC doi:10.1128/AAC.00998-15). No track in the draft; product_in_model false.
- Human-hepatocyte chimeric (uPA-SCID) mouse with an NNI (BMS-821095, sibling, product_in_model false): Shi 2013 Gut doi:10.1136/gutjnl-2012-302600 — NNI monotherapy broke through with resistant variants; NS5A+NS5B combination eradicated GT1b. Patient side: beclabuvir inside DCV-TRIO (UNITY-2, Muir 2015 JAMA doi:10.1001/jama.2015.3868). No track in the draft.
- Replicon resistance-pathway prediction compared in patients, never scored: filibuvir M423 selected in dosed patients (Wagner 2011 Hepatology doi:10.1002/hep.24342; Troke 2012 AAC doi:10.1128/AAC.05611-11), matching Shi 2009's M423T; dasabuvir S556G/C316Y/M414T in AVIATOR failures (Krishnan 2015 doi:10.1128/AAC.00998-15), matching Kati 2015; lomibuvir+telaprevir DUAL arm stopped for breakthrough (Di Bisceglie 2014 doi:10.1097/MEG.0000000000000084). A 2011 same on the replicon track would precede both 2014 entries.
- Beclabuvir is a second approved member: Japan, Ximency (daclatasvir/asunaprevir/beclabuvir), PMDA review https://www.pmda.go.jp/files/000226231.pdf (tier 2, approved Dec 2016); UNITY-2 Muir 2015 JAMA doi:10.1001/jama.2015.3868 (SVR12 in compensated cirrhosis); model side Lemm 2014 AAC doi:10.1128/AAC.02495-13 (replicon EC50 3-6 nM GT1a/1b, P495 resistance, liver-to-plasma 1.6-60x). The context note's 'only dasabuvir … reached approval' is wrong; a same entry strengthens the replicon track.
- Tegobuvir safety, tier 5 patient side only: Gilead PR 2007-11-04 ('In the second multiple dose cohort, a possible but not confirmed QT elongation was observed'; dedicated QT study started) and Gilead PR 2011-09-04 (GS 9190 dosing halted in two four-drug PEG/RBV+PI trials after two serious adverse events, in consultation with FDA; nature undisclosed). No tier 1-3 model source for a tegobuvir hERG/cardiac prediction found (Shih 2011 AAC doi:10.1128/AAC.00307-11 abstract silent; a 2018 SAR paper doi:10.1016/j.bmc.2018.04.029 says analogues improved hERG 'over tegobuvir'). Needs a model_src before it can score; the 2011 halt is a class failure the draft does not show.
- Registry checked for the unscored failures: setrobuvir NCT01903954 (SVR24 primary) and NCT00978497, tegobuvir NCT00743795 (24/48 wk + PEG/RBV) have no posted results, so the draft's 'not scored' for those members stands.

## hepatitis-c-virus-infection-hcv-ns5b-nucleotide-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Dating a silence-based safety contra: the harm was public in Aug 2012 (press release, tier 5) and Dec 2013 (FDA MedR, tier 2) but no source stated that the animals missed it until Gill 2016. Does a drafter pairing of a pre-trial model paper that is silent on the harm (Vernachio 2011: rats and cynos dosed, no mito toxicity in cells) with the regulator's quotation of the sponsor's 'unexpected' make the comparison public at 2012/2013, or must the model side explicitly state the toxicology prediction?
- Decision 5 #51 wording: 'A 25-50x margin below clinical exposure does not gate same; record it in magnitude.' For sofosbuvir, rat myocardial degeneration at 14x AUC (7-day, GS-9851) with a clean 6-month NOAEL at 9x and the FDA requesting a follow-up rat study: is this same + off_by_>5x (prediction 'no harm at clinical exposure' held) or contra/opposite (predicted harm that never appeared)?
- Retrodiction dating when the patient paper (Ahmad, PubMed 2014-09-25) cites unpublished model data ('consistent with some results of recent primate studies', data not shown): does that tier-1 statement set the retro entry's year at low confidence, or does the model paper (Gill 2016) govern?
- Track identity for IND toxicology across members: the draft runs one track for sofosbuvir (rat/dog/mouse), BMS-986094 (rat/cynomolgus) and would for IDX-184 (mouse/rat/cynomolgus); is 'standard repeat-dose toxicology' one system per decision 13 (#48), or one track per species set? And is Gill's cynomolgus study with echocardiography a 'newer model' (second track, decision 6) or the same monkey model re-run (retro on the IND track)?
- Second matched retrodiction on one track (Yanagida 2021 after Millard 2016): #113/#127 permit a new entry only on a status change or a second miss; may strengthens: true carry a second match, or does it fold into the note?
- Decision 5 for a terminated member: IDX-184's animals predicted heart/kidney/muscle harm; patients dosed up to 12 weeks before the class hold showed none. Is a predicted harm absent from a short, truncated patient dataset contra/opposite, or untested because the dataset could not have shown it?

Missed comparisons:

- In-vitro mitochondrial-toxicity assays (POLRMT incorporation; HepG2/CEM/PC-3 mitochondrial protein synthesis and respiration) have no track. Prospective: Vernachio 2011 AAC (doi 10.1128/AAC.01335-10, epub 2011-02-28) 'No evidence of mitochondrial toxicity was observed after 14 days of INX-08189 exposure in both HepG2 and CEM human cell lines', then the 2012 cardiotoxicity (contra). Retrodiction: Feng 2016 AAC (doi 10.1128/AAC.01922-15, epub 2015-11-23) BMS-986094 'primary effect on mitochondrial function at submicromolar concentrations' while sofosbuvir's active form was a poor PolRMT substrate (retro); Jin 2017 AAC (doi 10.1128/AAC.00492-17) extends it.
- IDX-184 on the IND-toxicology track: Luo 2015 Hum Exp Toxicol (doi 10.1177/0960327115592939, epub 2015-07-02) reports 13/26/32-week mouse/rat/cynomolgus studies naming heart, liver, kidney, skeletal muscle and lower GI as targets, with echocardiography in the monkey. Patients: healthy-subject SAD (Zhou 2010, doi 10.1128/AAC.01101-10) and 3-day monotherapy (Lalezari 2012, doi 10.1128/AAC.01521-12: 'adverse events ... similar' to placebo) showed none, and the programme was stopped on the BMS-986094 class concern. A decision-5 / #51 comparison the draft left unscored; the 12-week Phase 2b safety record (Idenix) was not found this session.
- IDX-184 on the replicon track: 3-day monotherapy fell only -0.5 to -0.7 log10 at 25-100 mg (Lalezari 2012) and Luo 2015 records termination for 'suboptimal efficacy'; candidate neg under decision 13 if its replicon EC50 sat below the class bar (not computed here).
- GS-0938 (PSI-352938) member safety failure sits in the bundle: Sovaldi MedR 'Due to ALT elevations associated with GS-0938' (QUANTUM, Dec 2011); no animal-side source found, so it stays unscored but should be named in the safety judgement with its FDA citation. ALS-2200/VX-135 2013 liver-enzyme hold not verified (press-release tier).
- [models check] CYP3A4 metabolite-formation assay (Good 2020, PLoS One, doi 10.1371/journal.pone.0227104, PMC6949113): a distinct in-vitro system from the mitochondrial panels. Retrodicts the PSI-938 / PSI-661 hepatotoxicity ('likely due to the formation of mutagenic O6-alkylated nucleobase metabolites'; PSI-938 formed 1.57 uM O6-ethylguanine with rhCYP3A4) and made a prospective clean prediction for AT-527 (no O6-alkylguanine formed) matched by the 8-week Phase 2 record (NCT05904470). Sponsor-authored and the PSI-938 attribution is hedged ('likely'), so candidate retro 2020 + same, low confidence; no track.

## hepatocellular-carcinoma-anti-cd274-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Combination arm with a non-member partner: HIMALAYA's primary endpoint was OS for STRIDE (durvalumab plus tremelimumab, an anti-CTLA-4 outside the class) vs sorafenib; the member-only arm met only a pre-specified non-inferiority secondary (HR 0.86). Decision 12 (#70) limits combination-arm contra to attributed events but nothing decides whether a combination-arm primary success scores same for the member class, or whether a member-only non-inferiority secondary can carry same without endpoint_role poc_gate. The draft's efficacy same, and hence dose same, rest on this.
- outcome_tested scope: the scored efficacy comparison (HIMALAYA) was itself an OS endpoint, matched. A later member trial in the same disease (EMERALD-1, embolisation-eligible setting) met a surrogate primary (PFS) and then missed its key-secondary OS (HR 1.10). Does that set outcome_tested negative on the cell, stay in patient_trials, or does the different stage/setting keep it apart? Decision 9 covers only the case where the model predicted the surrogate.
- Class membership: atezolizumab is an anti-CD274 antibody with the largest HCC patient record (IMbrave150 positive OS; IMbrave050 adjuvant RFS later lost significance) and the only stated model-vs-patient comparison for anti-PD-(L)1 in HCC (Pfister 2021 Nature, NASH mice plus trial meta-analysis), yet the tile's member list is benmelstobart, durvalumab, sugemalimab. If the reviewed class table is right the draft is correct to exclude it; if the table is wrong, both tracks and missing change materially. Please confirm the table.
- Opening-year consistency across cells of one model_id: the monkey track opens at 2012 (proxy) in safety but 2017 in dose with model_first_use_year 2012 on both. Rule 7 implies every cell opens at first use; a lint check for year-of-first-entry equal to model_first_use_year per cell would catch this.
- Confirmation entries: rule 7 writes a new entry only on a status change and reserves strengthens true for a second miss that removes the first miss's excuse. The draft's safety entry 5 uses strengthens true for a confirmation of same in the context's own disease. Is a confirming entry allowed, and if so should it carry a distinct flag so matrix counts do not treat it as a status change?
- Models check (indices 4 and 6): the same HIMALAYA pairing is confirmed at efficacy index 2 and corrected to untested at indices 4 and 6 under decision 4 (#64/#144). One adjudication should cover all three: either a pre-specified, alpha-controlled member-only non-inferiority key secondary may carry endpoint_role poc_gate (same stands, relabelled), or a combination-arm primary with a non-member cannot carry a member-class same (untested).

Missed comparisons:

- Efficacy track for the models the class actually relied on (rule 8, checker step 4): durvalumab's filed cross-indication efficacy systems, the human-T-cell co-implant A375/HPAC xenografts dosed with MEDI4736 and CT26 mice dosed with the anti-mouse PD-L1 surrogate (Stewart 2015, doi 10.1158/2326-6066.CIR-14-0191; FDA PharmR BLA 761069 studies ONC4736-0019, TGI up to 74%/95%). The draft opens efficacy on an academic transgenic HCC model the sponsor never used; the filed models need their own model_id with model_kind cross_indication, model_filed true, paired with HIMALAYA.
- EMERALD-1 final overall survival, not in the draft: Sangro et al., Annals of Oncology 2026 abstract 183O (ESMO GI 2026; PII S0923-7534(26)00410-2), data cut-off 4 Jul 2025: D+B+TACE vs TACE median OS 29.9 vs 33.3 months, HR 1.10 (95% CI 0.87-1.39, p=0.470); D+TACE vs TACE 33.6 vs 33.3 months, HR 0.93 (0.74-1.19, p=0.666); immune-mediated AEs 65.5%/38.4%/34.5%. Key secondary, so status stays with the met PFS primary, but it belongs in efficacy patient_trials with a quote (conference abstract, confidence low) and bears on outcome_tested (rule question 2).
- EMERALD-1 primary figures the drafter recalled, now verified from the Lancet abstract (PMID 39798579, epub 2025-01-08): PFS 15.0 vs 8.2 months, HR 0.77 (0.61-0.98), p=0.032 for durvalumab+bevacizumab+TACE vs TACE (primary, met); 10.0 months, HR 0.94 (0.75-1.19), p=0.64 for durvalumab+TACE vs TACE (key secondary, not met). The patient_trials quote for NCT03778957 should carry these numbers rather than the aims sentence.
- HIMALAYA long-term OS updates for the durvalumab-alone arm, patient_trials facts: four-year update (Ann Oncol 2024, doi 10.1016/j.annonc.2024.02.005) and five-year update (J Hepatol 2025, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.03.033).
- Benmelstobart HCC patient data beyond the draft's phase 1b: ALTER-H006 adjuvant benmelstobart plus anlotinib phase II (Nat Commun 2025, doi 10.1038/s41467-025-66342-2); DEB-TACE plus transarterial TQB2450 plus anlotinib phase II (Front Immunol 2025, doi 10.3389/fimmu.2025.1667785); adjuvant HAIC plus anlotinib plus TQB2450 (Innovation 2025, doi 10.1016/j.xinn.2025.100910). All single-arm with a non-member TKI and no model comparison; note-only, no status change.
- Safety fact for the HCC note: EMERALD-1 reported three study-treatment-related deaths on durvalumab+TACE (arterial haemorrhage, liver injury, multiple organ dysfunction) vs three on placebo (Lancet abstract), and Lancet correspondence asked whether unexplained early deaths were liver failure (doi 10.1016/s0140-6736(25)01666-6; authors' reply 10.1016/s0140-6736(25)01667-8). Not attributed to durvalumab, so no status change, but the draft's 'no unpredicted harm appeared in HCC' should cite it.
- Searched and not found (Europe PMC, title-restricted and free-text, 2026-11-05): no patient-derived HCC organoid or ex-vivo system compared with patients for a member drug; no humanized-mouse HCC study of durvalumab, sugemalimab or benmelstobart; no sugemalimab HCC preclinical model (only the BLU-554 + CS1001 phase Ib/II, NCT04194801, results 2023-02-06). The draft's 'no model comparison' for benmelstobart and sugemalimab stands.

## hepatocellular-carcinoma-anti-pdcd1-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Combination trials: when the model dosed the antibody alone and the only HCC patient result is antibody plus an active TKI vs sorafenib with no antibody-alone arm (APOLLO), is that agreement 'in direction' for the antibody, or a condition the model never tested (untested)? Affects efficacy 4 and dose 2; CARES-310 is unaffected because Yang 2021 modelled the combination.
- Single-arm trials without a pre-specified null (Qin 2020, co-primary ORR and 6-month OS): can efficacy score 'same', or does 'primary endpoint responded as predicted' require a tested hypothesis or a comparator?
- Tox studies with lost exposure: when 93% of 13-week monkeys were ADA-positive and the FDA says this may explain the limited findings, is the model's silence on immune-mediated harm still a scorable 'contra', or is the study inadmissible as a prediction?
- model_id grain for syngeneic tumour models: do different cell lines, hosts and sites (Chen 2015 orthotopic HCA-1/RIL-175 vs Yang 2021 unnamed syngeneic HCC) under the same surrogate antibody share one model_id, as the MC38 pooling precedent implies, or are they separate tracks?
- Dose 'magnitude' when a stated margin held: the drafter recorded the NOAEL-to-clinical-dose ratio (about 20x) as off_by_>5x; should a safety-margin ratio be recorded as magnitude at all, or left null?
- Retrodictive model on an untested opener: Kauffman 2021's introduction says it was run as anti-PD-1 TB case reports accumulated, but lint forbids retrodiction: true unless the status is retro or contra, so the flag is recorded false and the fact lives only in judgement text. Should an untested entry carry retrodiction: true?
- Combination isolation in the safety cell: every member TB datum (penpulimab AK105-304 SAE, Lin 2023 camrelizumab case) is under chemotherapy co-administration. Applying the tile's efficacy-4 combination rule leaves infection-exacerbation tracks permanently untested; is a case report under member plus chemotherapy admissible for safety when the harm is the model's stated mechanism?
- Pooled class cohorts on a prediction_level: class track: the Shenzhen anti-PD-1 cohort (44/889 active TB) includes camrelizumab and serplulimab without per-drug counts. Can a pooled anti-PD-1 cohort containing members score a class-level safety track, or must a member be isolated?

Missed comparisons:

- Regulatory failure not shown: FDA complete response letters for camrelizumab plus rivoceranib in unresectable HCC (2024-05-16, 2025, 2026-07-10), cGMP/inspection-based, not efficacy; changes no model comparison. Source (tier 5): Elevar Therapeutics press release, https://www.biospace.com/press-releases/elevar-therapeutics-receives-fda-complete-response-letter-for-combination-of-rivoceranib-and-camrelizumab-as-a-first-line-treatment-for-unresectable-or-metastatic-hepatocellular-carcinoma
- Patient-level ex-vivo HCC system exists for the mechanism but not for a member: patient-derived organotypic tumour spheroids predicting lenvatinib plus tislelizumab response (Wang 2026, Front Oncol, doi:10.3389/fonc.2026.1744326, PMID 42311270). Tislelizumab is not a tile member, so it does not score; the draft's claim that no member has an HCC ex-vivo predictor stands.
- Model-stated safety prediction with no patient comparison in the draft: the penpulimab review (bundle p.40) cites Mtb-infected PD-1 knockout mice and anti-PD-1 in Mtb-infected monkeys (Kauffman 2021) as predicting infection exacerbation, carried into label section 13.2; an untested knockout/monkey harm the safety cell could record.
- Searched and found nothing: no public in-vivo model for camrelizumab (SHR-1210), serplulimab (HLX10; only the structural paper PMID 34972111) or spartalizumab (PDR001) on PubMed, so no missed member-own animal track.
- Member-drug TB case the Mtb tracks' judgements say does not exist: Lin 2023, Front Immunol 14:1162190 (PMID 37304292, PMC10248429), camrelizumab plus chemotherapy, lung squamous cell carcinoma, pulmonary TB after one cycle, tabulated in the 2026 review PMC13581663. Chemotherapy-combined, so it does not isolate the antibody and untested stands on both Mtb tracks; the judgement text should cite it instead of claiming no camrelizumab case exists (abstract does not name the drug, which is why tiab searches miss it).
- Member-drug cohort data on the Mtb prediction, neither isolating a member and no status change: Zhongshan ICI cohort (PMID 42733647, PMC13571672, 2026), camrelizumab n=3 and penpulimab n=1 among 118 LTBI/TB-history lung-cancer patients, none developing active TB; Shenzhen real-world cohort (PMID 42751276, PMC13579480, 2026), camrelizumab and serplulimab pooled among 841 anti-PD-1 patients with 44 active TB and no per-drug counts.

## hepatocellular-carcinoma-kdr-vegfr-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Decision 3: does PK/PD measured in patients of another indication (tivozanib solid-tumour FIH; RCC dose 1.34 mg/day) count for an HCC dose or engagement cell the way healthy-volunteer data do, when the HCC RP2D (1 mg) was set lower for tolerability and never exposure-matched?
- Decision 4 dose table: the model's exposure target was reached (stated by a regulator) but the same drug's efficacy in the context is indeterminate (tivozanib phase 1b/2 stopped without a primary-endpoint verdict, #53 says stopping is not failure). No row covers 'reached, efficacy indeterminate': same on the floor, or untested?
- Decision 4 (#32): once the randomised axitinib trial is untested (missed OS primary, significant PFS/TTP secondaries), does the single-arm McNamara 2015 phase 2, whose primary (16-week tumour control 42.3%) was met, score efficacy same for 2015? The draft treats single-arm response primaries as not status-setting.
- Decision 16: hypertension was predicted by the class package (mice/rats/dogs; telemetered monkey only at 3 mg/kg, HED 58 mg/day) and appeared at the effective clinical exposure without closing the window (patients dosed to target, hypertension managed). Is that the dose ceiling contra with margin misplaced, or safety same direction_only as drafted? Where is it counted once?
- Decision 8 (track identity): is one safety model_id right for the GLP rodent-plus-non-rodent package when members used different species (mouse/rat/dog for axitinib, rat/monkey for tivozanib) and apatinib's own package is unpublished?
- Engagement readouts: is a class-standard circulating PD marker (plasma sVEGFR-2 fall, Fountzilas 2020) 'the PD readout the model used' when the model measured tumour-endothelial pVEGFR2 and MVD? The draft kept it untested per a sibling tile; decision 15 defines tiers but not substitutability.
- Decision 10 (models check): when a model manuscript was received before the trial's first public readout but revised and published after it (Yang 2020: received 2019-12-28, revised 2020-05-22, published 2020-06-12; AHELP ASCO abstract 2020-05-20), does the submission date or the publication date decide retrodiction? Scored prospective here by submission date, consistent with adjudicated efficacy 3.
- Decision 8 (models check): does an in-silico drug-sensitivity imputation (oncoPredict trained on GDSC) that names a member drug among 198 screened compounds, with nothing dosed, count as 'a model used for the class' and open an untested track, or does an in-silico screen need a threshold before it enters the atlas?

Missed comparisons:

- Engagement, HCC patients, member drug: McNamara 2015 (doi:10.1002/cncr.29227, full text) reports DCE-US tumour blood volume decreased at 2 weeks in 8 of 12 axitinib patients and predicted better OS; the draft lists the trial but never scores it against the model's DCE-MRI readout (used for the engagement correction).
- Engagement, product in model, other-indication patients: tivozanib first-in-human study AV-951-03-B01 showed dose-dependent decreases in serum sVEGFR-2 and dose-dependent reduction in tumour perfusion (FDA NDA 212904 Multidiscipline Review, sources/212904/212904Orig1s000MultidisciplineR.txt lines 1417-1420; published as Eskens et al. 2011 Clin Cancer Res, DOI not verified here). Scorable only if decision 3 extends healthy-volunteer admissibility to other-indication patients (rule question 1).
- Dose, comparison stated: FDA NDA 212904 Multidiscipline Review (intro, ~lines 1121-1126) pairs the rat-effective exposure at 0.2 mg/kg (Cmax 70-108 ng/mL, AUC 1640 ng*h/mL; simulated Css 69.5 ng/mL, p.179) with the clinical exposure at 1.34 mg/day (Cmax 86.9 ng/mL, AUC0-24 1510 ng*h/mL) in solid-tumour patients; the draft states no such comparison exists.
- Safety, product in model: Lu et al. 2019, Exp Mol Med (doi:10.1038/s12276-019-0274-7, PMID 31285418) HCC xenograft mice on apatinib developed hypertension and mild liver/kidney damage, a product-in-model safety prediction published before AHELP read out; safety entry 4 could cite it as model_src with product_in_model true.
- Patient-level model, no comparison: Journal of Translational Medicine 2024 (doi:10.1186/s12967-024-05870-9, PMID 39558364) patient-derived liver-cancer stem-cell lines; a transcriptomic C2 subtype is predicted sensitive to axitinib, no patient was dosed. Untested entry on the patient-derived track (hcc-patient-derived-primary-cell-lines or its own model_id).
- No additional member-drug failure (press release, SEC filing, EMA/AdCom document) for HCC monotherapy was found in the sources reached; title/abstract-restricted Europe PMC searches found no HCC organoid or PDX study of apatinib, axitinib or tivozanib beyond the two above.
- Attribution note (models check, CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models): the 'Lu 2019' safety item above is Liang Q 2019, Exp Mol Med (doi:10.1038/s12276-019-0274-7, PMID 31285418), on the existing hcc-human-xenograft-immunodeficient-rodent track; the models merge added no safety entry for it, so safety entry 4 still carries product_in_model false.

## hepatocellular-carcinoma-vegfr-multikinase-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Member list: the class table gives catequentinib, donafenib, lenvatinib, regorafenib only. Brivanib (Huynh 2008 CCR doi:10.1158/1078-0432.CCR-08-0509, 5/6 HCC PDX inhibited; BRISK-FL and BRISK-PS failed), sunitinib (SUN1170 failed) and linifanib (LiGHT failed) are VEGFR multikinase inhibitors with model-vs-patient comparisons in HCC. Under the no-widening rule they score nothing, so the context shows two matches and no failure; is that the intended record for this mechanism?
- A filed pre-trial experiment whose results are first published after the trial (Eisai study W-20130793 in Matsuki 2018, written after REFLECT): the draft scores it 'same' because the model was run before the clinic knew the answer. Decision 10 speaks of models built or run after; does a post-trial publication of a pre-trial result count as prospective, and should the date be the filing or the publication?
- Engagement readout concordance: the model's PD readout was tumour pFRS2/pErk (FGFR blockade); patients showed serum FGF19/FGF23 rise (Finn 2021), a different quantity on the same axis. Does 'the PD readout the model used' admit a downstream readout_tier entry for a pathway-concordant but different analyte, or must the analyte itself match?
- A model that predicts superiority over an active comparator (lenvatinib > sorafenib in Matsuki 2018) when the trial meets non-inferiority only on the primary endpoint and superiority on secondaries: is that 'same' + over_predicted, or 'same' + partial?
- Models pass: Yang 2024 (efficacy 9) is a second untested entry on the organoid track after Cell Oncol 2022 with strengthens: true, but it is not a 'second miss' (#127); decision 7 would fold it into the 2022 entry's note or patient_trials unless the full text turns it into same or contra for lenvatinib.

Missed comparisons:

- Tai WT et al. 2014 Clin Cancer Res, doi:10.1158/1078-0432.CCR-14-0725 (epub 2014-09-23): regorafenib 20 and 40 mg/kg/d inhibited PLC5 (PLC/PRF/5) HCC xenografts in mice, published before RESORCE. A tier-1 prospective prediction on the hcc-human-xenograft-immunodeficient-mouse track that pairs with Bruix 2017 as 'same' (2016) for regorafenib; the draft's xenograft track has only the Eisai title (2015) and the Kissel retrodiction (2017). Its 20-40 mg/kg dose is 2-4x the exposure-matched 10 mg/kg, relevant to the dose cell.
- Patient-derived liver-cancer organoid biobank compared with patient response, lenvatinib among the seven drugs: Cancer Cell 2024, doi:10.1016/j.ccell.2024.03.004 (399 organoids from 144 patients; 'reliable for drug sensitivity screening, as evidenced by both in vivo models and patient response'; lenvatinib-response signatures). Also Ji et al. 2023 Sci Transl Med, doi:10.1126/scitranslmed.adg3358 (LICOB; lenvatinib + temsirolimus validated in organoids and PDX). A patient-level ex_vivo track with no entry; abstract-level reading, the direct organoid-vs-patient lenvatinib comparison needs the full text.
- FGF19-driven HCC as a patient-level prediction of lenvatinib benefit: Matsuki 2018 (Hep3B2.1-7 FGF19 xenograft, selective) and Myojin et al. 2021 Clin Cancer Res, doi:10.1158/1078-0432.CCR-20-3382 (mouse model with diverse drivers: lenvatinib selectively eliminated FGF19-expressing tumours; serum/tumour FGF19 and ST6GAL1 associated with OS in lenvatinib-treated patients). The draft has no prediction_level 'patient' entry for this.
- Finn RS et al. 2021 Clin Cancer Res, doi:10.1158/1078-0432.CCR-20-4219 (REFLECT PD biomarkers): lenvatinib, not sorafenib, raised serum FGF19 and FGF23 in patients at all time points, responders more than non-responders. The draft cites REFLECT biomarker work in a judgement but gives no source; it is the patient side of an FGFR-axis engagement comparison (downstream readout) that the engagement cell leaves untested.
- Not checked for budget: the FDA regorafenib HCC sNDA review (2017) and lenvatinib HCC sNDA review (2018) and the EMA Lenvima HCC variation report, which may state the model-to-clinic comparison at tier 2 earlier than Kissel/Matsuki.

## hiv-infectious-disease-hiv-integrase-insti-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- When the first public patient signal is a tier 1 letter with no abstract (Zash 2018) and a tier 2 regulator communication exists but could not be retrieved, should the contra be dated to the first public signal (2018) or the first quotable source (2019)?
- For dose 'reached, drug worked', is the entry dated when both halves are public (HV PK reaching target 2009 + first efficacy 2011) via pairing, or does it need a source that states trough vs target and response together (FDA review 2013)?
- Class-wide harms not tested against tox packages (INSTI weight gain, DTG creatinine/OCT2, neuropsychiatric AEs): does rodent silence on weight gain count as a miss the species could have shown?

Missed comparisons:

- Min 2010 AAC 10.1128/AAC.00842-09 (epub 2009-11-02): 'C(tau) with a 50-mg dose was 1.6 microg/ml, approximately 25-fold higher than the protein-adjusted 90% inhibitory concentration (0.064 microg/ml)': healthy-volunteer dose comparison the draft omits; moves dose dating earlier.
- Zash et al. AIDS 2025, 10.1097/QAD.0000000000004326: earliest peer-reviewed report of the Tsepamo NTD signal attenuating (0.10% vs 0.11%).
- Zash 2018 NEJM letter 10.1056/NEJMc1807653 (epub 2018-07-24) and the May 2018 FDA/EMA safety communications: first public NTD signal, a year before the scored entry.
- Zebrafish embryo DTG developmental-toxicity model (Cabrera et al. AIDS 2019, 10.1097/QAD.0000000000002289; Cabrera 2026 DMM 10.1242/dmm.052632): folate-receptor antagonism and NTD-type defects set against the Tsepamo signal; no track.
- Second mouse NTD model, SWV/Fnn and other strains dosed with DTG (Tukeman et al. AIDS 2024, 10.1097/QAD.0000000000003639; Gelineau-van Waes 2023 Front Cell Dev Biol 10.3389/fcell.2023.1175917): DTG-induced NTDs, folate-responsive; a separate model system from the C57BL/6J therapeutic-dose track.

## hiv-infectious-disease-hiv-maturation-gag-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- readout_tier for on-treatment resistance selection at the drug's binding site (A364V at CA/SP1): 'direct', 'proximal', or not an engagement readout at all when the model's own PD readout (viral replication = viral load, listed as proximal) is available earlier?
- Safety 'contra' for a predicted animal harm absent in patients: what minimum sample size and duration makes 'never appeared' a comparison rather than 'untested' (here 14 on-drug paired gastric biopsies at 24 weeks, planned Week-96 biopsies never done), and does an animal finding with no public exposure margin count as a stated prediction of harm at clinical exposure (decision 16 interaction)?
- Dose table: when the exposure target was reached and the Week-24 primary was met but the regimen was abandoned for emergent NRTI-backbone resistance and GI intolerability (GSK3532795 Phase 2b, NCT02415595), does the 'reached, failed, abandoned' row apply, or does dose stay 'same' because efficacy is judged on the primary endpoint only?
- Does a cross-species ADME/PK projection (bioavailability, metabolite profile, excretion route) compared with human PK count as a model-system track, or is only the exposure-target model (serum-shifted EC90) scored under dose?
- Patient-level prediction on an existing track (baseline Gag V362I/V370A predicted and observed reduced response): does it require its own entry with prediction_level 'patient', or is one entry per status change per track regardless of prediction_level?
- May a track that only a non-member first-generation drug (bevirimat) ever used in vivo (SCID-hu Thy/Liv mice) appear as an 'untested' track opened by proxy, or does a track need at least one member run in it to exist?

Missed comparisons:

- Humanized mouse efficacy model, no track: HIV-1-infected SCID-hu Thy/Liv mice, oral bevirimat cut viral RNA >2 log10 at plasma concentrations 'achievable in humans' with dose-dependent CA-SP1 cleavage inhibition in thymocytes and in-vivo confirmation of the SP1/A1V resistance phenotype (Stoddart 2007, PLoS One, doi 10.1371/journal.pone.0001251, PMID 18043758; cited as ref 52 in Nowicka-Sans 2016). No member was reported in it, so it is a separate track opening 'untested' (proxy, product_in_model false), and the draft's claim that no animal efficacy model exists must be withdrawn.
- Chronic animal toxicology as the track's species: Johnson 2018 (doi 10.1002/prp2.408) names 6-month rat and 9-month dog studies for GSK2838232 and an FDA clinical hold (Studies 200912/200207 terminated; resumed as Study 204953) that the draft's safety track does not record; not a new track but a dated event on mi-nonclinical-tox.
