- Candidate, pending rule question 4: cross-species ADME/PK prediction compared with human PK for GSK2838232 (oral bioavailability 6-40% across species, preclinical metabolite profile 'qualitatively similar' to human plasma, negligible renal excretion predicted and used to skip urine collection), Johnson 2018 doi 10.1002/prp2.408; the GSK3640254 discovery paper (Regueiro-Ren 2022, J Med Chem, doi 10.1021/acs.jmedchem.2c00879, abstract only) describes the preclinical PK profile that preceded the Phase 1 (Pene Dumitrescu 2020, doi 10.1002/prp2.671).

## hiv-infectious-disease-hiv-rt-nnrti-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Does a tier-5 sponsor press release set `year` (and `model_first_use_year`) for a comparison whose status is set by a later tier-1 paper? Rule 6 lists registry posting, online publication, label or print only. Here Idenix's Sep 2008 release reported the fosdevirine 7-day monotherapy result four years before Zala 2012, and the seizure clinical hold was public Feb 2011 vs Castellino Dec 2012; if yes, engagement/dose/efficacy `same` move to 2008 and safety `contra` to 2011, closing the 2009-2012 gap the strip draws.
- `model_kind` has no in-vitro value: a cell-culture replication assay with laboratory HIV-1 strains in MT-4/MT4-GFP cells is not ex_vivo (patient cells). Which value should antiviral cell-culture models carry, or should the enum gain `in_vitro`?
- When the engagement readout (viral load, proximal) is also the trial's primary endpoint, do engagement and efficacy both score `same` from the same 7-day monotherapy study, or should efficacy wait for a treatment trial with a clinical virologic endpoint (<50 copies/mL at week 48)?
- Is a registration by a regulator outside the tier-2 list (elsulfavirine, Russian Ministry of Health, 2017) a tier-2 source, or inadmissible so the member stays without a tier 1-3 patient source?
- dor-cyp3a-induction-pk-model joins two in-silico methods under one model_id: nonparametric superposition (Khalilieh 2018, predicts doravirine trough) and a PBPK model (Yee 2021, predicts M9) built later for the same dose adjustment; the 2022 label row tests only the superposition projection, the PBPK's M9 prediction is tested only in the Yee abstract. Decision 8 does not say whether successive in-silico methods for one dosing question are one system or two tracks. [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models]

Missed comparisons:

- Patient-level use of the same cell-culture track has no entry: Pifeltro label section 12.4 (http://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/label/2018/210806s000lbl.pdf, bundle txt lines 736-750) sets cell-culture-selected V106A/M, F227C, Y318F beside the substitutions in DRIVE-FORWARD/DRIVE-AHEAD virologic failures (7 of 11 with >97-fold phenotypic resistance), tier 2, comparison stated, 2018; the draft notes it in a confidence_note but enters no prediction_level: patient entry.
- Tier-5 patient-side sources the draft did not cite: Idenix press release, Sep 2008 (https://www.biospace.com/idenix-pharmaceuticals-inc-announces-completion-of-proof-of-concept-study-for-idx899-in-treatment-naive-hiv-infected-patients) reporting 1.78/1.78/1.84/1.87 log10 at 800/400/200/100 mg after 7 days, i.e. the Zala 2012 comparison four years earlier; and Feb 2011 press coverage of the FDA clinical hold after four seizure reports (https://newprod1.poz.com/article/hiv-gsk761-idenix-19885-4233), 22 months before Castellino. Status unchanged by either; dates depend on rule question 1.
- No separate model system found: WebSearch for doravirine/elsulfavirine/VM-1500 in humanized mice or RT-SHIV macaques returned only generic model papers, and PubMed (elsulfavirine OR VM-1500 OR fosdevirine OR GSK2248761 OR IDX899) has no animal-efficacy or elsulfavirine patient-trial paper. Elsulfavirine's only regulator record is a 2017 Russian MoH registration, outside the tier-2 list (rule question 4).
- Tier-2 observed record of the dor-cyp3a-induction-pk-model confirmatory trial, not cited by the dose index-4 entry: Pifeltro label s007 (01/2022, https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/label/2022/210806s007lbl.pdf) Table 9, rifabutin 300 mg QD with doravirine 100 mg BID, N=15, AUC 1.03 (0.94-1.14), Cmax 0.97 (0.87-1.08), C24 0.98 (0.88-1.10); footnote 'Doravirine 100 mg BID resulted in similar pharmacokinetic values when compared to 100 mg QD without rifabutin'. Absent from the 10/2019 s003 label. Supplies the magnitude (observed C24 ratio 0.98 vs projected restoration to the QD trough) and subjects the entry leaves null; year unchanged. [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models]
- Candidate in-silico dose model with no track: population-PK dose bridge for adolescents 35-<45 kg, Pifeltro label 12.3 (s007 01/2022; current 07/2026 revision: 'the population pharmacokinetic model-predicted mean C24 of doravirine was comparable to that achieved in adults, whereas mean AUC0-24 and Cmax were 25% and 36% higher', IMPAACT 2014 n=54). A post-hoc exposure bridge built on the observed pediatric data, so it would score dose untested with source_calls_it: scaling (decision 4) rather than same; listed for the drafter to decide, not a missed comparison. [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models]

## hiv-infectious-disease-hiv-rt-nrti-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Is a China NMPA package insert (说明书) a tier-2 regulator document? Decision 2 names FDA, EMA, Health Canada and AdCom documents only; the azvudine result exists nowhere else.
- When one proximal readout (plasma HIV-1 RNA) is both the engagement readout and the efficacy primary endpoint, a success scores in both cells but decision 4 routes a genotype-stratified failure to efficacy only, so the same track reads engagement `same` (censavudine 2013) and efficacy `contra` (GS-9131 2021). Is that asymmetry intended?
- Does one drug-related SAE in 198 patients (thrombocytopenia, Gupta 2016) count as a predicted low-frequency animal harm appearing in patients (`same`), or is there an n or incidence floor below which the comparison is `untested`?
- When the registered primary outcome was never populated (randomized cohort not enrolled) and the sponsor's stated go/no-go bar sat on a registered secondary outcome in sentinel cohorts, does the trial score on that bar as endpoint_role poc_gate (my proposal) even though the sponsor itself calls it the primary endpoint in the limitations field?
- Dating: does a registry status change to Terminated with an efficacy reason (GS-9131, 2020 per Adis) make the comparison public under decision 7, or only the results posting (2021-01-05)?

Missed comparisons:

- Azvudine NMPA package insert (阿兹夫定片说明书; conditional approval 2021-07; copy at https://file.wuxuwang.com/zhuce/ssypfiles/78da81a9010d86837acf314780c4798c%E8%AF%B4%E6%98%8E%E4%B9%A6.pdf, holder 河南真实生物): pivotal Phase 2, 172 ART-naive HIV-1 patients, azvudine 2/3/4 mg + EFV + TDF vs 3TC + EFV + TDF, primary endpoint HIV-1 RNA <50 copies/mL at week 48, PPS 97.2% / 100% / 100% vs 97.5%; HIV-1 RNA fell 1.5-3 log10 by weeks 4-12 and -3.2 to -3.5 log10 at week 48. A regulator document reporting the azvudine patient result the draft calls unpublished: supports efficacy `same` and engagement `same` for azvudine (2021, product_in_model true, model side Wang 2014 10.1371/journal.pone.0105617); the cell headline does not change because the GS-9131 contra (2021-01-05) beats it under decision 6. The label's adverse-reaction section (dizziness 38-60%, ALT/GGT/AST rises; warnings on neutrophil count, bilirubin, AST, glucose) is also an uncompared patient safety record for the cell-culture package. Tier depends on rule question 1.
- In-vitro resistance selection vs patient resistance for azvudine, on the cell-culture track: Wang 2014 (10.1371/journal.pone.0105617) selected M184I as the key azvudine mutation in culture; an AIDS 2023 letter (PMID 37930315, 10.1097/QAD.0000000000003564) reports M184I after short-term azvudine given for COVID-19 to people living with HIV. A patient-level resistance comparison with exposure in another indication (imported_from COVID-19); only the title was available to me, full text not read.
- Earlier public date for the GS-9131 failure: Adis Insight gives the Phase 2 termination as 2020-04-28 and a Fierce Biotech piece reported GS-9131 quietly dropped from Gilead's HIV pipeline in favour of GS-6207; neither is an admissible tier, the CT.gov version history and the Fierce page both returned 403, and the 2020 10-Q search surfaced nothing, so 2021-01-05 stands unless a 2020 registry status change or Gilead filing (tier 5) is found.

## hyperlipidemia-cetp-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Decision 16 margin_stated: does a margin count as 'stated before the trial' when the model data were generated pre-IND but first published (Cao 2011) after the human MAD that tested it was run, and before that MAD was published (2014)?
- The same rat BP / exposure-multiple datum scores both dose (margin_stated same) and safety (same). 'Count the harm once' covers a harm that occurred; is double scoring allowed when no harm occurred?
- Best-model rule vs member-specific dose models: should a different model's dose miss for another member (rocacetrapib PK/PD allometry, Choi 2019) appear as its own entry, and is a model published after phase 1 data a retrodiction even if it set the FIH dose?

Missed comparisons:

- Lilly press release 2015-10-12 announcing discontinuation of evacetrapib after ACCELERATE futility (tier 5, investor.lilly.com; page returned 403, not opened) dates the outcome failure to 2015, earlier than the NEJM 2017 / registry 2018 dates in patient_trials.
- Rocacetrapib (CKD-519): Choi 2019 (https://doi.org/10.3390/pharmaceutics11070336, abstract) animal allometric PK/PD model predicted a 25 mg efficacious dose with a 'huge difference' from observed phase 1 bioavailability; published after phase 1 (Kim 2016, https://doi.org/10.2147/DDDT.S120387), so likely retrodiction/contra on a different model of a member drug. Drafter noted it in judgement but scored nothing; best-model rule keeps cell same.
- Teramoto 2014 (PMID 24786356, Europe PMC abstract) gives patient CETP-activity engagement data in April 2014, ahead of Suico (June 2014).
- DRL-17822 patient_trials quote is a paraphrase of the NCT01388816 results table, not a verbatim sentence; numbers (3.2/84.2/122.1/160.6%) verified.
- Prospective pairing for the human-genetics track: Brown 1989 (https://doi.org/10.1038/342448a0) / Inazu 1990 (https://doi.org/10.1056/NEJM199011013231803) CETP deficiency raises HDL-C, confirmed in patients by de Grooth 2002 JTT-705/dalcetrapib phase 2 (https://doi.org/10.1161/01.CIR.0000015857.31889.7B): first class-level same, 2002.

## hyperlipidemia-hcar2-agonists

Rule questions:

- Co-primary split / weak-but-real response: MK-1903 (LDL-C co-primary null, HDL-C co-primary +4.6% P=0.013, TG secondary -10.3% P=0.02) and SCH 900271 (primary LDL-C met at 5 mg P<0.01, not at 10/15 mg, no dose-response); the sponsor abandoned both and calls them misses against niacin. Is this `contra`, or `same` + partial + off_by_>5x? The dose table has no 'reached, worked weakly, abandoned' row.
- Dose row when exposure was reached but the model's PD target was lost to tachyphylaxis (Lauring: chronic FFA suppression not sustainable; NCT00903617 trough NEFA rose vs placebo): 'target never reached' (efficacy untested) or 'reached-failed-abandoned' (efficacy contra)? Both give dose contra; the efficacy knock-on differs on all three tracks.
- Is a second entry with the same status on one track (efficacy 3: contra after contra, a tier-1 comparison-stated source after a tier-3 registry) allowed under 'one entry per change of status'? Lint accepts it.
- Dating when the earliest public comparison sits in an unreadable or unquotable source (Lauring 2012 full text for MK-1903 flushing; MK-1903 registry 2010 SOC rows 'vascular disorders' 42/116 vs 8/75 without the term 'flushing'): does rule 6 (earliest date) or rule 3 (must quote) set the year?
- Track identity: Lauring 2012's stated comparison is between its own humanized LDLR-/- +/- GPR109A mice and patients; the draft makes those mice a separate retro track yet marks Lauring comparison_stated on the Tunaru knockout track. Should a stated comparison transfer between tracks of the same knockout on different backgrounds?
- Two-part safety prediction (dog: 'no overt flushing to 10x the FFA ED50' failed at 10-15 mg; 'improved window vs nicotinic acid' held): same + partial, or contra because the absolute margin was misplaced? Decision 16's margin logic is written for dose, not safety.
- Area casing: tiles.json gives 'metabolic', the draft writes 'Metabolic', the two house-style contexts are capitalised, and the other v3 drafts split (50 lowercase 'oncology' vs 25 'Oncology'). Which is canonical for the figure key?

Missed comparisons:

- Rat plasma FFA/TG model used by Merck and Schering for the member drugs: MK-1903 lowered plasma FFA 90% in rats (Boatman 2012, https://doi.org/10.1021/jm2010964, full text paywalled) and SCH 900271 suppressed rat FFA 70% and TG 49% at 1 h (Palani 2012 full text); both compared with the phase 1 human FFA decrease (Boatman 2012 abstract; Lauring 2012). Pointer: Tuteja & Duffy 2016, Curr Atheroscler Rep, https://doi.org/10.1007/s11883-016-0570-9. The draft has a rat track only for GSK256073.
- Mouse dermal blood-flow (laser-Doppler) flush model, Merck: MK-1903 increased dermal blood flow 30% in mice (Boatman 2012 via Tuteja 2016), and in the phase 1 urinary PGD-M rose dose-linearly while MK-1903 flushed patients (Lauring 2012; Offermanns 2012 Focus, https://doi.org/10.1126/scitranslmed.3004445). An MK-1903 safety/engagement track with no entry; only niacin-in-knockout stands for the mouse.
- Lauring 2012 SCH900271 phase 1B (10 mg/day vs 2 g ER-niacin vs placebo, 4 weeks, dyslipidaemic patients): HDL-C +10% vs placebo (P<0.05) and no niacin-type skin symptoms per Tuteja 2016 — an in-trial niacin comparator for both the dog flush window (safety 4) and the efficacy magnitude (efficacy 6). Not in the draft; supplement/full text not readable (science.org 403; supplement download denied).
- Lauring 2012 Fig. S1 ('summary of preclinical pharmacology and pharmacokinetics' of SCH900271) and Table S2 (MK-1903 phase 2A adverse events): unchecked; could convert the dog track to comparison_stated and date the mouse flush 'same' to 2012.

## insomnia-hcrtr1-2-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Suvorexant next-day impairment was the harm behind the FDA dose cut, and the animal package never tested it. The drafter left it unscored under dose. Decision 16 puts an unpredicted harm in safety, so does model silence on next-day or residual sedation count as a harm the species could have shown (safety contra)? Or is it human-PK carryover (half-life), outside what the model could show?
- A dose cell mixes an efficacy-exposure target model (suvorexant C4h) with a decision 16 tolerability-margin model (daridorexant dog NOAEL). Should model_change: true be set when the cell was not contra, given the rule defines model_change only for turning a contra back?
- Dose entry 2: the effective-exposure target is published only in the FDA review that also holds the patient comparison, and Winrow 2011 is cited as model_src. Is this comparison_stated true (regulator states both sides) or false (a pairing)?
- Safety 5-6: the lemborexant mouse cataplexy study (W-20140712, mid-2014) ran when suvorexant's cataplexy-like events in patients were already known (2013 AdCom, 2014 label). At prediction_level class, is a member's model run after a sibling's patient result a retrodiction, or prospective because its own drug's patients were untested?

Missed comparisons:

- Vornorexant Phase 3 (Japan) met its primary sSL endpoint with no narcolepsy-related signs (Sleep 2025, PMID 41001841, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41001841/): this agrees with the draft and changes no status. It could be added to efficacy patient_trials.
- Filorexant Phase 2 insomnia was positive at all doses (Connor 2016, doi:10.1093/ijnp/pyw022), and Merck dropped it without an insomnia failure. The draft names this only in a judgement, which is correct; no failure was missed.
- Lemborexant FDA multidiscipline review 2019 (Figure 1: lemborexant cataplexy in wild-type vs orexin-KO mice) is a separate member-drug safety model with a patient comparison. It agrees, so no status change, but it could be a dated safety entry.
- Fazamorexant Phase 3 (NCT05525637) is reported positive only on secondary aggregators. No failure was found for any member in a press release or SEC filing.
- Lemborexant's and daridorexant's own nonclinical abuse-liability studies vs their human abuse potential trials (FDA 212028 multidiscipline review 2019: 'Nonclinical findings were less suggestive of abuse liability'; daridorexant NDA 214985 review 2022, HAP vs zolpidem): further entries in the rhesus-rat-abuse-liability-battery track, both C-IV.

## interstitial-cystitis-anti-ngf-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Dose table row 'reached, failed, abandoned -> dose contra, derived_from efficacy' (and 'reached, worked -> same'): does it apply when no model ever stated an exposure target for any member drug? The draft keeps dose untested while efficacy goes same then contra; the rules do not say whether 'target reached' can be scored without a stated target.
- Engagement: can a model's functional readout (cystometric voiding frequency in CYP cystitis) count as a downstream PD readout when the human trial measured its counterpart (micturition frequency, unchanged in Evans 2011)? If yes, engagement would be contra 2011 at readout_tier downstream; the draft scored it as a symptom endpoint.
- Dating of efficacy entry 3: could not open the ClinicalTrials.gov history for NCT00999518 (API returned non-JSON). If the termination-for-interim-analysis text was public before Feb 2012, the year should be earlier; the rules do not say whether a registry termination reason without results counts as a public comparison.

Missed comparisons:

- Wang H et al. 2017, BMC Urol 17:2, doi:10.1186/s12894-016-0193-z (PMID 28056917, epub 2017-01-05): fulranumab 9 mg SC in IC/BPS (NCT01060254, n=31), primary endpoint LS mean difference -0.2 [-1.52, 1.10], not significant; no joint-related SAE. The draft says this trial ended 'with no results'; it belongs in efficacy entry 3's patient_trials (no status change: underpowered, already contra) and the note should be corrected.
- ASP6294 is a PEGylated Fab' (MAbs 2023, PMID 36458900, doi:10.1080/19420862.2022.2149055); the EU CTR record 2016-004138-12 (first posted 2020-02-08, women only, P=0.591, -0.2 [90% CI -0.84, 0.43]) confirms the draft's patient_trials quote and shows the female subgroup Nickel 2016 flagged was not confirmed prospectively.

## malignant-pancreatic-neoplasm-map2k1-2-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Add-on trials: when the model tested the member drug alone (Walters 2013: trametinib single agent and with lapatinib) and the trial tested drug + gemcitabine vs gemcitabine, does the 'conditions the model never tested' clause make the pairing untested, or does a single-agent growth-inhibition prediction count against an add-on null? (For pimasertib, Vena 2015 tested the combination, so the track keeps a clean contra either way.)
- Retrodiction dating: when the only public dates are publication dates and the paper's own framing shows the trials were still running when the experiments were designed ('several are currently in clinical trials'), does retrodiction follow the epub date (retro, as drafted) or the experiment date (same)? Junttila 2015's received date could not be retrieved.
- The 80% biomarker bar: does 'biomarker is >= 80% of the trial population' mean the fraction measured positive by the trial's assays (64% KRAS-mutant in Infante 2014, cfDNA/archival tissue) or the known prevalence in the disease (~90% of PDAC)? The population label 'Unselected' vs 'KRAS-mutant' turns on this.
- Multi-component regimens: when the member drug is one of three differing components (SBRT + pembrolizumab + trametinib vs SBRT + gemcitabine, NCT02704156, positive OS primary endpoint), is the result ever admissible as same for the class, or always untested with the trial recorded in patient_trials?
- Track openers: the GEMM track has no untested opener at its model_first_use_year. Does a non-member MEK inhibitor in the same KRAS GEMM (Collisson 2012 Cancer Discov with PD325901; Alagesan 2015 with selumetinib, neither re-opened in this run) set model_first_use_year under 'proxy allowed', which would open the track in 2012 rather than 2014?
- [models] model_kind for an immortalised pan-tumour cancer cell-line panel (Gilmartin 2011): the enum offers ex_vivo (patient ex-vivo cells) but no in_vitro; is ex_vivo the intended bin, or does the enum need in_vitro?
- [models] Patient-level prediction when the class result is null in everyone: Infante 2014 and Van Cutsem 2018 show no benefit in any arm and 'KRAS status did not influence' outcomes. Is the genotype-selection prediction contra (the predicted responders did not respond) or untested (no responders in whom selection could show)? Only Van Laethem 2017 (KRAS-wild-type did better) is a true reversal; the track's latest status is contra either way.
- [models] source_calls_it for Van Laethem 2017: the source calls the wild-type advantage 'a trend ... that deserves future investigation' and never names a cell-line panel; is 'miss' admissible as source-stated, or should an un-framed subgroup reversal be 'unresolved'?

Missed comparisons:

- NCT02704156 (results posted 2021-02-16): randomised phase 2, n=170, KRAS-mutant PD-L1-positive locally recurrent PDAC after resection, SBRT + pembrolizumab + trametinib 2 mg vs SBRT + gemcitabine, primary endpoint OS; HR 0.69 (95% CI 0.51-0.95), p=0.021 in the republished report Zhu 2022 Lancet Oncol 10.1016/S1470-2045(22)00066-3 (the 2021 report 10.1016/S1470-2045(21)00286-2 was retracted and republished; notice 10.1016/S1470-2045(22)00064-X). A member-drug randomised primary-endpoint readout in this disease that the draft does not show. The arms differ in three components, so it probably scores untested on the class question, but it belongs in patient_trials, and any preclinical basis pairing MEK inhibition with PD-1 blockade and radiation in a KRAS GEMM would be a track entry.
- Vena 2015 Clin Cancer Res 10.1158/1078-0432.CCR-15-0485 (epub 2015-07-30): pimasertib + gemcitabine 'caused significant tumor growth delays in an orthotopic pancreatic cancer model'. This is the model side the draft left null for Van Cutsem 2018, and the only member-drug model in the context that tested the add-on-to-gemcitabine design the trials used; it predates the 2017 results posting, so it is a prospective pairing, not a retrodiction.
- Lapatinib + trametinib phase I, Cancer Chemother Pharmacol 2020 10.1007/s00280-020-04066-4: the Walters 2013 PDX combination prediction did reach patients (34 enrolled, 3 pancreatic); dose-limiting toxicities set the RP2R at trametinib 1 mg continuous, below the 2 mg single-agent dose. A dose-cell comparison for the xenograft track (dose table row 'never reached because of toxicity; no stated margin' -> untested) that the draft's judgement says never reached patients.
- GSK2256098 (FAK inhibitor) + trametinib 0.5 mg phase II in advanced PDAC, J Gastrointest Oncol 2022 10.21037/jgo-22-86: primary endpoint clinical benefit, 1 of 11 evaluable, 'not active in unselected advanced PDAC'. A member-drug pancreatic trial the draft does not show; MEK inhibition is not isolated and the trametinib dose was sub-therapeutic; its model side (KPC FAK/MEK work) was not identified in this run.
- Trametinib 2 mg + gemcitabine phase I/Ib in Japanese patients, Invest New Drugs 2015 10.1007/s10637-015-0270-2 (NCT01324258): interstitial lung disease in 3 of 5 pancreatic patients, one fatal. A harm in this disease on the context's regimen; whether the rat/dog lung findings in NDA 204114 predicted it was not checked here. Candidate safety entry for the tox-species track.
- Checked and excluded: patient-derived organoids (Front Immunol 2026 10.3389/fimmu.2026.1760379) screened trametinib combinations in PDOs and matched PDXs, but compared organoids with patients only for gemcitabine/paclitaxel, so there is no MEK-class patient comparison and no missing PDO track.
- [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models] Tox-species-rat-dog track, safety: tier-2 stated comparison in NDA 204114 PharmR lines 400-405 (analyses/model-system-atlas/sources/204114/204114Orig1s000PharmR.txt): 13-week dog study, 'the lymph nodes and lungs were target organs'; lung histopathology 'minimal hemorrhage, mononuclear infiltration, pleural fibrosis, and macrophage accumulation. All findings in the lung were classified as minimal to mild; however, clinically, treatment with trametinib has been associated with cases of interstitial lung disease and pneumonitis.' A regulator's reconciliation counts as stated (safety rule, #79); rat lungs are not named. Disease-specific patient side: trametinib + gemcitabine Japanese phase I/Ib, Invest New Drugs 2015 10.1007/s10637-015-0270-2 (ILD in 3 of 5 pancreatic patients, one fatal). Candidate safety entry (dog lung signal -> clinical ILD, year 2013) on the same track as the 2013 ocular contra.
- [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models] PDX track (pdac-xenograft-pdx), efficacy: a member-drug combination comparison the sweep parked under considered_not_added. Model: Kinsey 2019 Nat Med 10.1038/s41591-019-0367-9 (trametinib + chloroquine regressed patient-derived PDA xenografts; one patient responded to trametinib + hydroxychloroquine). Patients: Witte 2024 J Cancer Res Clin Oncol 10.1007/s00432-024-05954-5 (AIO retrospective, trametinib + hydroxychloroquine, no responses) and Tang 2023 BMC Cancer 10.1186/s12885-023-11464-3 (real-world third-/later-line, trametinib + hydroxychloroquine, no responses); THREAD NCT03825289 terminated without results. Same PDX system, model tested the regimen patients received, model precedes the patient series: a candidate prospective contra entry on the existing track (tier 1 both sides), not a new track.

## melanoma-anti-pdcd1-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Decision 16: may a tolerability margin stated by a different system (monkey NOAEL, 13-23x AUC) set margin_stated: true on the in-vitro T-cell saturation track, or must the margin come from the track's own model, which would open the monkey (tori-cynomolgus-monkey) as its own dose track?
- Safety: when a tox package is affirmatively negative at saturated occupancy (hormone panels run), is an on-target immune-mediated harm (thyroid irAE) a harm 'the species could have shown' -> contra, or expected class pharmacology outside the tox model's remit -> untested? Also: does 'class-known' harm (Tang 2019: no novel signal vs approved anti-PD-1) alter scoring under rule 7?
- Retrodiction comparison_stated: does a sponsor-authored model paper that cites the clinical result as known background, without putting model and patient numbers side by side, count as comparison_stated: true or as a pairing (false, model_src = the paper, src = the trial)?
- Efficacy same vs untested: the checker brief flips same to untested when the trial source attributes 'the result' to conditions the model never tested. Does attribution of only the attenuated magnitude (acral/mucosal subtype) to an unmodelled condition, with direction matched, keep same + direction_only?
- Single-arm ORR primary with no stated success bar in the source abstract: must 'primary met' be documented from a source (protocol/registry/regulator) for same, or does direction agreement suffice?
- Do filed-but-never-compared systems in the same review (PBMC-humanised NSG mouse) require their own untested track, or may the drafter fold them into a note?
- [models check] Decision 8 granularity: is one model_id the animal system (strain) or the preparation (strain + tumour + readout)? The draft splits tori-hpd1-knockin-mc38 from prolgolimab-hpd1-knockin-ct26 and opens the S180 immuno-PET mouse separately (preparation level), yet pbmc-humanised-nsg-mouse lumps PBMC-engrafted (T-cell expansion; HCC827 xenograft) and CTL-engrafted (MC38) NSG mice (system level). Both cannot be right.
- [models check] Efficacy same from an unquantified model result: may a reviewer's adjective ('minor antitumor activity', no TGI, no statistics, figure only) stand as the model's prediction for same + direction_only, or does same require a model-stated number or direction, leaving such an arm untested?

Missed comparisons:

- PBMC-humanised NSG mouse (Studies 34885_IVHU and TR003: toripalimab increased splenic/circulating T cells; MC38 + allogeneic anti-624Mel CD8 CTLs with 'minor antitumor activity') is a filed toripalimab model with no track; source: FDA 761240 MultidisciplineR, nonclinical pharmacology section (bundle txt lines ~4376-4442). Candidate untested track(s) or an explicit note on why not opened.
- Fu 2017 monkey PK/ADA model (ADA-driven clearance at 10 mg/kg) vs patient ADA/trough in Tang 2019 (serum ADA measured) is an unscored dose-side comparison on the monkey track; source: doi:10.1038/aps.2016.161 abstract + PMC6332582 PK/ADA paragraph.
- Searched, nothing found (agrees with drafter): no published preclinical model for pucotenlimab (phase I PMC8182631 says only 'Preclinical data suggests'; Drugs 2022 First Approval summary) or for prolgolimab before Gordeev 2024 (Europe PMC TITLE:BCD-100/prolgolimab 2016-2024 = 5 hits, all clinical); no toripalimab melanoma organoid/PDX/humanised-mouse comparison with patients in Europe PMC.
- [models check] Pucotenlimab HuGEMM arm (Zhang 2020 mAbs, PMC7153830: 'HuGEMM with human PD-1 gene knock-in syngeneic MC38-bearing mice'; TGI vs pembrolizumab) is the same preparation as tori-hpd1-knockin-mc38-mouse (human PD-1 knock-in C57BL/6 + MC38; vendor construct differs). Its pairing with Cui 2023 (NCT04749485, ORR 20.17%, lower 95% CI 13.37% > 10% bar, primary met) belongs on that track as a 2023 same/strengthens entry with model_src doi:10.1080/19420862.2020.1724751. The draft's efficacy[2] judgement sentence 'no published pre-clinic model' for pucotenlimab, and this file's earlier missing item 3, are wrong on that point.
- [models check] Pucotenlimab cynomolgus single-dose PK (Zhang 2020, PMC7153830): monkey ADA accelerated terminal elimination, set beside the phase 1a CTR20180125 PK in the same paper ('the safety and favorable pharmacokinetics demonstrated in human clinical trials'), a stated monkey-overpredicts-immunogenicity comparison: an unscored dose-side entry for a second member on the cynomolgus track, parallel to the Fu 2017 / Tang 2019 item above.

## melanoma-braf-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Safety 2/4 (rat-dog track): the FDA review's executive summary hedges that one high-dose dog's undiagnosed paw growth 'may have been similar' to clinical cuSCC while its own correlation table leaves the dog cell blank. Does a hedged, n=1, unexamined finding count as a regulator's retrospective reconciliation (#79) or a sponsor-dismissed signal patients confirmed (#23, same), or does the blank table govern (contra)?
- Safety 2: Bollag's stated 'no histological changes in the skin' was contradicted by a harm (keratoacanthoma-type cuSCC) whose mechanism needs RAS-primed, sun-damaged keratinocytes that rats and dogs lack. Decision 4's 'measurable in the model species' carve-out is written for silence; does it also soften a stated negative prediction, or does a stated prediction always count both ways?
- Safety 7: the patient side is a member (dabrafenib) plus a non-member (trametinib) arm, and the prediction tested is a mitigation (MEK co-inhibition cuts cuSCC). Decision 12 only says combination-arm events anchor a contra when attributed to the class drug; is a combination arm admissible for a same on a safety track?
- Safety 7 dating: Flaherty 2012 (epub 2012-09-29) had cuSCC incidence as a pre-specified PRIMARY end point and found 7% vs 19%, P=0.09. Decision 7 dates to the earliest public comparison; decision 10 discounts a non-significant trend only in a terminated trial. Does an underpowered, non-significant primary in a completed trial date a same entry (2012) or not (2014)?
- Safety 6 dating: Oberholzer 2012 (epub 2011-11-07) showed RAS enrichment (21.1% vs 3.2%) in cuSCC from 19 patients on vemurafenib or sorafenib pooled, with no member-only split. Is a pooled member/non-member patient series admissible as the earliest public comparison for the class, or set aside as the drafter did?
- Missing S1320: a schedule prediction (intermittent vs continuous) from vemurafenib-resistant xenografts, tested with dabrafenib+trametinib. Which cell takes it: dose (regimen prediction, #91) or efficacy (PFS primary)? And is the resistant-xenograft system a new model_id or the V600E xenograft track with product_in_model false?
- Missing encorafenib rat: a second member's own package predicted a milder harm on the same hyperproliferative spectrum (hyperkeratosis) but not cuSCC. Does that score same on the class rat-dog track with magnitude/partial, or is hyperkeratosis a different harm from cuSCC so the entry is untested for cuSCC and same only for hyperkeratosis?
- Models check, efficacy 5-7 (intermittent-schedule track): decision 4 puts a regimen prediction under dose (#91) and gives case 3 a derived dose contra that is hatched and never a second failure; the drafter scored the schedule failure in efficacy alone, citing double counting. Should the track also carry a derived dose contra (derived_from: efficacy), or does a schedule-efficacy prediction answered by a PFS primary live in efficacy only?
- Models check, efficacy 5-7: in all three trials the intermittent arm withheld a non-member MEK inhibitor together with the BRAF inhibitor, and the sources attribute the test to the BRAF-inhibitor drug-addiction hypothesis. #70 is written for combination-arm events anchoring a contra; does it also admit an efficacy contra on a schedule that was only ever tested in combination (product_in_model true only for Gonzalez-Cao)?
- Models check, efficacy 7: INTERIM is a third miss entered with strengthens: true. #127 admits a second miss that removes the first miss's excuse; #113 sends trial facts that change nothing to patient_trials. Does a third randomised miss with no new excuse removed qualify as its own entry, or belong in the prior entry's patient_trials?
- Models check, efficacy 6-7: a reversed point estimate that is not significant (INTERIM PFS HR 1.39, CI 0.79-2.45; Gonzalez-Cao 16.2 vs 6.9 months, p = 0.079) in a completed trial. Is magnitude: opposite the bin, or direction_only, when the predicted improvement is absent and the direction only trends against?
- Models check, efficacy 9: the patient's trial arm is undisclosed and the three PDX arms did not differ, so the stated match is 'patient responded, PDX responded' with no discrimination. Does a non-discriminating stated comparison count as retro (source_calls_it: match), or is it untested for want of a prediction?
- Models check, efficacy 10: decision 11 scores partial agreement 'on the pre-specified readout', written for prospective entries. In a retrodiction (Krepler 2017: initial 70.6% PR not reproduced, early relapse reproduced), who fixes the readout: the source's framing (early relapse, retro + partial) or the patient's full course (PR missed, contra under decision 10)?

Missed comparisons:

- Encorafenib's own rat toxicology, in the bundle (sources/210496/210496Orig1s000MultidisciplineR.txt, review pp.35 and 50, FDA NDA 210496, 2018): 13-week rat skin abnormalities (scabbed/dry/flaking skin, paw swelling, 'hyper keratosis of the skin') at all doses including 6 mg/kg (~11x clinical exposure), reconciled by the reviewer: 'Skin toxicity is the most common adverse event noted clinically with encorafenib treatment.' A stated regulator comparison on the rat-dog-toxicology track: same for encorafenib (rat predicted hyperkeratosis; 57% on COLUMBUS monotherapy), year 2018; the track stays contra because vemurafenib's contra beats it. The drafter said this package was unread; it is in the bundle.
- Vemurafenib-resistant xenograft / drug-addiction model (Das Thakur 2013, Nature, doi:10.1038/nature11814: intermittent dosing forestalled resistance in xenografts) tested prospectively in SWOG S1320 (NCT02196181, first posted 2014-07-21, no results posted; Algazi 2020, Nat Med, doi:10.1038/s41591-020-1060-8): intermittent dabrafenib+trametinib did not improve PFS over continuous dosing. A prospective regimen prediction (#91) contradicted for a member drug; no track in the draft (own model_id; dose-regimen or efficacy cell, see rule_questions).
- Patient-derived xenografts of melanoma compared with the donor patients' BRAF/MEK-inhibitor responses: Krepler 2017, Cell Reports, doi:10.1016/j.celrep.2017.10.021 ('A Comprehensive Patient-Derived Xenograft Collection Representing the Heterogeneity of Melanoma') and Br J Cancer 2020, doi:10.1038/s41416-019-0696-y ('Drug responses are conserved across patient-derived xenograft models of melanoma'). A patient-level PDX track (retro or same) is absent. Titles verified on Crossref; abstracts not read because NCBI returned 429.
- Not a new model but a missed fact on the rat-dog track: FDA pharmacology review NDA 202429 executive summary: 'one high dose male dog developed a wart-like growth on the front left paw which was not fully examined. This growth may have been similar to the cutaneous skin cell carcinomas observed clinically.' The draft's 'rat and dog columns blank' note should carry it.
- Candidate, unverified (models check): Gao 2015, Nat Med, doi:10.1038/nm.3954 (PMID 26479923; Novartis ~1,000-PDX '1x1x1' clinical-trial simulation, 62 treatments, six indications). Abstract names no drug and the body is paywalled, but its reference list cites Sosman 2012 (vemurafenib), BREAK-2 (dabrafenib) and Flaherty 2012 (BRAF+MEK) beside Einarsdottir 2014, consistent with a stated PCT-vs-clinic comparison for BRAF inhibitors in melanoma PDX; encorafenib/LGX818 is Novartis's own drug. A class-level 'member drug's own model' track under decision 8 if the full text states the comparison; not read here.

## metabolic-dysfunction-associated-steatohepatitis-fgf21-analogue-fgfr1c-klb-agonists

Rule questions:

- Is a congress abstract (Hepatology 2016;64 Suppl 1:749A, no DOI/PMID), reached only through a trial paper's citing sentence, an admissible model side? Rule 2 lists no such tier. If not, the nash-fibrosis-mouse track has no model source and the class's fibrosis endpoint (FALCON 1/2 failed, HARMONY and ENLIVEN met) has no track at all.
- When the model stated no exposure target or tolerability margin, does the dose table's 'reached, drug worked' / 'reached, failed' row still apply (as the draft does for dose 2, 4 and 6), or is dose 'untested' because there was no prediction to reach?
- One stated comparison that half matches (triglycerides, adiponectin) and half contradicts (glucose, insulin, weight) on the model's own readouts, with the source framing it as a contrast: v3 says partial -> same + source_calls_it partial. Confirm that is intended when the quoted sentence states the contradiction.
- A member-drug toxicology study run before but published after the human result (pegbelfermin 1-year monkey bone, 2021 vs BMD known 2018-2019): retro or same? Decision 10 keys on when the model was 'built or run', which a publication date does not show.
- Non-member drugs of the same mechanism (PF-05231023, LY2405319) carry tier-1 monkey-to-human comparisons including a BP/heart-rate harm. Are they set aside entirely, or does safety's 'other' (same platform) admit them?
- Dating across comparison kinds on one track: can a pairing (comparison_stated false, Kaufman 2020) set the year of a track's 'same' earlier than a later stated comparison (Rosenstock 2023), or does the stated one govern the date?
- Independent human-genetic model (Frayling 2018) published after a sponsor-internal, unpublished patient result (Amgen AKR-001 MAD NCT01856881, 2013-2015, no results posted; published Kaufman 2020): decision 10 needs 'post-dates and is motivated by', so scored same, not retro. Confirm that unpublished sponsor data do not make a later independent model a retrodiction.

Missed comparisons:

- Member-drug monkey bone model with a stated margin: pegbelfermin 1-year skeletally-mature cynomolgus study (Toxicol Appl Pharmacol 2021, doi 10.1016/j.taap.2021.115673, PMID 34364948) at 0.7-2.2x human AUC (20 mg QW) found no bone effects and states 'low potential for negative effects on bone quality in adult humans'. A second bone track (margin_stated true); retro vs same depends on when the study was run (rule question 4).
- Member-drug rat BP/sympathetic model: efruxifermin 4/26-week Sprague Dawley tox (Br J Pharmacol 2022, doi 10.1111/bph.15725, PMID 34773249) states 'absence of an increase in sympathetic tone in rats mirrors the unchanged salivary cortisol and systemic blood pressure following efruxifermin treatment in humans' (Kaufman 2020 PK-PD). Stated comparison, published after the human data: retro. Rats also ate more, matching the increased-appetite AE.
- Human-genetic model on blood pressure: Kaufman 2020 (doi 10.1016/j.xcrm.2020.100057) compares the absence of a BP effect of efruxifermin with FGF21 loss-of-function variant carriers having higher BP (its ref 28); model_kind human_genetic, no track.
- Monkey-track safety silence: tremor (Kaufman 2020: product-related withdrawals for tremor at 140 mg QW efruxifermin) and GI AEs (diarrhoea/nausea, the class's commonest harm) are harms a cynomolgus monkey could show; Stanislaus 2017 / Rosenstock 2023 say nothing. No safety entry exists on the obese-diabetic-cyno-monkey track; the pegbelfermin 1-year monkey paper (clinical observations, food intake) could settle whether monkeys predicted GI effects.
- Later mouse bone model that reversed Wei 2012: Kaufman 2020 ref 27, a DIO-mouse rhFGF21 study finding no bone loss (likely Li et al., J Bone Miner Res 2017, Amgen); a separate track or a retrodiction on the bone question, absent from the draft.
- Pegozafermin phase 1b/2a in NASH patients (Loomba, Lancet Gastroenterol Hepatol 2022, doi 10.1016/s2468-1253(22)00347-8, NCT04048135, epub 2022-12-12): MASH-patient adiponectin/liver-fat PD for the monkey track one year before ENLIVEN; dates the subjects=patients engagement datum earlier than the healthy-volunteer SAD.
- Non-member FGF21 analogues with tier-1 stated monkey-to-human comparisons: PF-05231023 (Talukdar 2016, doi 10.1016/j.cmet.2016.02.001; raised BP/heart rate in one human study) and LY2405319 (Gaich 2013, doi 10.1016/j.cmet.2013.08.005). Outside the member list, so not scored; see rule question 5.
- Could not verify: no phase 3 topline for SYNCHRONY Histology (efruxifermin) or ENLIGHTEN-Fibrosis (pegozafermin; 89bio guides 1H 2027) was found by web search as of 2026-11-05; no BMS press release on pegbelfermin discontinuation was checked beyond the registry (no phase 3 registered).

## metabolic-dysfunction-associated-steatohepatitis-nr1h4-agonists

Rule questions:

- Safety, model-side disclaimer: when the model paper itself says its readout cannot predict the human effect (Hernandez on STAM cholesterol, after OCA's FLINT LDL rise was known), is the later patient opposite result contra or untested? Decision 4 covers trials disclaimed as efficacy tests (#57) but not model papers disclaiming their own readout.
- Safety, askable silence on pruritus: every member had dose-limiting pruritus (tropifexor, EDP-305 50.9%, cilofexor, vonafexor). Mice can show scratching, but no member's rodent studies measured it. Is 'measurable in the model species at the time' satisfied by the behaviour being measurable, or does it require an FXR-itch mouse readout to have existed? If the former, safety should carry a contra on pruritus.
- Dose tie without a stated exposure target: cilofexor efficacy is contra at 30 mg (rat effective dose 10-30 mg/kg, no human exposure mapping). Does row 3 ('reached, failed, abandoned' -> dose contra derived_from efficacy) apply when the model never stated a target, or does dose stay untested as drafted?
- Is a conference abstract with a DOI in a journal supplement (Reiberger 2016) an admissible tier-1 model-side source for dating and for model_first_use_year?

Missed comparisons:

- Cilofexor rat model first public as Reiberger et al. 2016 Z Gastroenterol abstract 'The non-steroidal FXR agonists PX20606 and GS-9674 improve liver fibrosis and portal hypertension in rodent models...' (doi:10.1055/s-0036-1583977); sets model_first_use_year and rules out retrodiction for efficacy entry 2.
- EDP-305 ARGON-2 (NCT04378010, histology phase 2b) stopped after the 12-week interim; Enanta press release 2021-10-04 (businesswire.com/news/home/20211004005192) calls it a strategic decision to prioritize combinations, not safety. Tier 5; belongs in patient_trials, changes no status (development stop is not 'failed', decision 4).
- Cilofexor phase 2 in non-cirrhotic NASH (NCT02854605, results 2019-01-29; Patel 2020 doi:10.1002/hep.31205): primary endpoint safety only; liver-fat fall was secondary. Could be a patient_trials row; does not change status.
- PXB humanized-mouse track, tropifexor healthy volunteers: Papazyan 2018 (doi:10.1194/jlr.M081935) cites Badman 2016 LJN452 first-in-human abstract (Hepatology 64:16A) as reportedly lipid-neutral over <=2 weeks; the mouse LDL rise contradicts that known HV result (retrodiction, contra 2018) before FLIGHT-FXR's patient LDL rise.
- PXB track, cilofexor in NASH patients: Patel 2020 phase 2 (doi:10.1002/hep.31205, NCT02854605, 24 weeks) reportedly no LDL/HDL change vs placebo; would give a patient-population contra in 2020 on top of the HV contra (full text paywalled; registry results posted 2019-01-29 could confirm lipid tables).

## migraine-disorder-anti-calca-calcb-cgrp-ligand-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Pediatric-population miss: fremanezumab failed its primary endpoint in pediatric chronic migraine (NCT04464707, 2025) after matching in adults. Under 'patients of the context's disease only' and 'one drug's contra still beats another drug's same', does a pediatric placebo-controlled miss flip an Unselected (adult-derived) efficacy track to contra, or is age a population split the context does not cover?
- Efficacy cell on a PD-only system: when the only prospective preclinical system is a target-engagement model (evoked vasodilation) whose authors nonetheless made an explicit efficacy prediction (Zeller 2008), does efficacy score same on that track, or is the cell the empty list because no disease model was relied on? The draft's context note says 'not on a disease model' yet populates the cell.
- readout_tier for in-vivo blockade of an evoked stimulus (capsaicin-induced dermal blood flow): the rule lists ex-vivo blockade of a stimulus as direct; in-vivo physiology (blood flow) could be downstream. Undecided.
- magnitude for a predicted-null harm that then appeared: 'opposite' is defined for a predicted harm that never appeared; the reverse case (null predicted, hypertension reported) has no stated value.
- Dating a class-wide pharmacovigilance harm: a tier-1 prospective cohort pooling a sibling-class drug (erenumab) with a member (fremanezumab) showed BP rising in 2022; does a pooled cohort where the member is the minority arm set the member's comparison date, or only the member-specific label (2025)?
- margin_stated on a PD-model track: the tolerability margin comes from the FDA-filed monkey tox NOAEL (a separate study on the same species), not from the CIDBF PK/PD model that is the track's model_id. Does a tox NOAEL count as 'the model stated the margin' for decision 16?
- Christensen 2019 runs two paradigms (mouse GTN-provoked allodynia; selectively bred rat spontaneous trigeminal allodynia) with one result under one model_id rodent-gtn-sta-pain. Decision 8 tracks each system; does one paper/one result justify one track, or should it be split?

Missed comparisons:

- Fremanezumab pediatric chronic migraine Phase 3 NCT04464707 (6-17 y): primary endpoint NOT met, LS mean difference -0.1 MMD (95% CI -1.48 to 1.22), p=0.8484, placebo -3.7 vs fremanezumab -3.8; CT.gov results posted 2025-12-19 (tier 3); AAN 2026 abstract Hershey et al. (index.mirasmart.com/AAN2026/PDFfiles/AAN2026-001129.html) states 'no significant difference was observed between treatment groups in the CM study (-3.8 vs -3.7; p=0.8484)'. A member-drug primary-endpoint miss in the context disease with no entry on the efficacy track; companion EM study NCT04458857 was positive (-2.5 vs -1.4, p=0.0210; label s031 adds pediatric EM). Whether it scores contra is rule question 1.
- Human ex-vivo artery model: Ohlsson et al. 2018, 'Fremanezumab blocks CGRP induced dilatation in human cerebral, middle meningeal and abdominal arteries', J Headache Pain, doi 10.1186/s10194-018-0905-8 (PMID 30109438, 2018-08-14): model_kind ex_vivo, product_in_model true, retrodiction after Phase 3; no track in the draft.
- Mouse CGRP-evoked light-aversion / spontaneous-pain models blocked by ALD405 (eptinezumab's murine sibling, Alder): Mason et al. 2017 J Neurosci doi 10.1523/JNEUROSCI.2967-16.2016 (PMID 28053042) and Rea et al. 2018 Pain doi 10.1097/j.pain.0000000000001337 (PMID 29994995): retrodiction track, product_in_model false; no track in the draft.
- Rodent GTN / migraine-like pain models with ALD405 and olcegepant: Christensen et al. 2019 Cephalalgia doi 10.1177/0333102419861726 (PMID 31288556, 2019-07-09): retrodiction, product_in_model false; no track in the draft.
- Rat post-concussion headache model (Bree & Levy, Cephalalgia, doi 10.1177/0333102416681571, epub 2016-12-07) is filed in the 761089 PharmR as a paradigm distinct from the MOH model; the draft folds it into rat-headache-behaviour without an entry or source of its own. Acceptable as one track, but the PharmR citation should be recorded.
- For the record, not scoring: CHALLENGE-MIG NCT05127486 (galcanezumab vs rimegepant, Lilly 2024 release) missed superiority on >=50% responder, an active-comparator endpoint no model predicted; galcanezumab chronic cluster headache and fremanezumab ENFORCE cluster headache (futility, 2018-2019) are another disease (MONDO cluster headache), outside this context.

## migraine-disorder-cgrp-receptor-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Retrodiction vs prospective on the same model: DILIsym retro-modeled MK-3207 (published 2020) in the same paper that reports the prospective ubrogepant match (2019 FDA, 2020 paper). Should the cell carry a separate retro entry for the MK-3207 member, or does the prospective same subsume it? Decision 10 does not say how a retro and a same of the same date and model order on the strip.
- Dose-table row 'reached, failed, a higher dose later worked': does the higher dose have to be shown superior to the predicted dose? For MK-3207, 200 mg alone was significant, but CIs overlapped from 10 mg up, and the same trial met its primary endpoint on a pooled dose-response trend. The table also implies efficacy contra-then-same, while the draft keeps efficacy same because the primary was met in the same trial. Which applies?
- Should a dose model built in human healthy volunteers (human CIDV calibrated on a non-member's phase 3 data) count as the same model_id/system as the rhesus CIDV used for engagement and efficacy, given that the brief scopes 'the model' to the preclinical system the class relied on?
- MK-3207 GLP toxicology: Monroe 2020 reads three species as silent on liver, but a 1-month dog study showed slight periportal vacuolation and <4X ALT/AST at 17X, attributed to body-weight loss. Is that a sponsor-dismissed animal signal that patients confirmed (same, #23) or askable silence contradicted (contra)? The paper's own reading is silence.

Missed comparisons:

- Smith 2020 (doi 10.1093/toxsci/kfaa093, PMC8312697): 'DILIsym successfully modeled liver toxicity for telcagepant and MK-3207 at the dosing regimens used in clinical trials'. This is a 2020 retrodiction for member MK-3207. The draft mentions it only in a judgement, with no retro entry (see rule question).
- BI 44370 discontinuation: secondary reviews (e.g. PMC4392770) attribute it to liver toxicity and/or oral formulation difficulty. No primary source (press release or registry) was found. If confirmed, it is a second member liver signal for the safety cell. At minimum the safety note should mention it.
- Ubrogepant dose: Voss 2016 phase 2b (doi 10.1177/0333102416653233) found 1 and 10 mg ineffective and 25/50/100 mg effective. Moore 2020 gives human CIDV EC50 2.6 nM. If a CIDV-based ubrogepant dose prediction exists (e.g. the exposure-response paper 'Making Better Dose Decisions...'), it is a second member's dose comparison. The FDA ClinPharmR bundle has no CIDV dose rationale.
- MK-3207 GLP toxicology, 2020 contra on model_id mk3207-glp-tox: Monroe 2020 (doi 10.1093/toxsci/kfaa088, PMC7553701, Table 1 and text) states MK-3207 'was dosed for 9 months in NHP at ... exposures exceeding 4-fold', rat and mouse 6-month margins 'exceeded 30-fold and 14-fold', 'No liver safety signals of concern were noted', and concludes such molecules 'present no evidence of liver safety concerns in 3 species of conventional animal toxicology studies ... and still provoke liver signals' in the clinic (phase 2 ALT>3x ULN 42% at >500 mg, Hy's law case). Comparison stated (comparison_stated true, tier 1); prospective model (run before the clinical signal), so contra, year 2020. Caveat: a 1-month dog study at 17X showed slight periportal vacuolation with <4X ALT/AST, attributed to excessive body-weight loss (see rule question).

## myelodysplastic-syndrome-hdac-pan-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Engagement: the model predicted an exposure-dependent readout (sustained, dose-dependent AcH3 in mice) and patients showed the readout moving without dose dependence (Abaza 2017). Decision 4 reads 'moved' = same, decision 11 makes it partial; the drafter read the lost dose-dependence as contra. Which is decided?
- Dose: the protocol dose (60 mg RP2D) was delivered and the trial failed, but the sponsor attributes failure to toxicity-driven early discontinuation (sub-optimal cumulative exposure) and the model stated no margin (efficacy at the mouse MTD). Decision 4 case 3 (derived contra) or decision 16 (untested, no stated margin)?
- Dose on an ex vivo cell track that stated no exposure or occupancy target: is 'reached' definable, so case 3 derived contra applies, or is the cell untested?
- Model identity: a human PK prediction from mouse/rat/dog allometry plus Simcyp (Jayaraman 2011) sits on the mouse-xenograft model_id. Decision 8 says any system the class relied on counts, labelled; is multi-species PK scaling the same track as the xenograft efficacy model or its own?
- Safety: animal findings the label says were 'not reported (or reported with low incidence)' in patients, where the AE tables do list them at low incidence. Decision 11 (right hazard, wrong incidence, same) rather than decision 5 (never came, contra)? And one entry same+partial, or one entry per harm, when a single package matches one harm and over-predicts another?
- Efficacy: a randomized phase 2b whose primary endpoint (composite CR 27.5% vs 14.3%) was numerically higher with overlapping CIs, no OS/TTP difference, and the sponsor abandoned the regimen. Does it add a same+partial entry on a track already contra, or change nothing?
- Dating: the earliest patient comparison (Giles 2006) predates the drafter's chosen model paper (Fiskus 2009) but follows the system's first use (George 2004). Rule 6/decision 7 is read here as opening the track at 2004 and dating the comparison 2006; confirm that the opener follows the system, not the paper the drafter picked.
- In-silico PK prediction whose run date is not public (Jayaraman 2011): the published predicted-vs-observed comparison (epub 2011-08) postdates the phase 1 PK reports (2011-02/03) and the 2007 FIH, but IND-enabling allometry/Simcyp ordinarily precedes FIH. Decision 10 requires 'post-dates and is motivated by the patient result'; scored retro here for consistency with famitinib-pbpk-rat-monkey-human-pk. Does an unverifiable run date default to retro, or does an IND-enabling presumption give same?

Missed comparisons:

- Engagement, pracinostat-mouse-xenograft track: Mol Cancer Ther 2011 tissue-acH3 PD paper (doi 10.1158/1535-7163.mct-11-0044, PMID 21586629, epub 2011-05-17) is a stated comparison the draft lacks: acH3 tracked SB939 dose in HCT-116 xenograft and HL-60 orthotopic SCID tissues (r2 0.76-0.94), then rose dose-dependently (10-80 mg) in PBMCs of 30 solid-tumour phase 1 patients (r=0.97 with Cmax/AUC); the abstract states the PK/PD 'are translated from preclinical models to patients'. A 2011 same, comparison_stated true, subjects patients (solid tumour; decision 3 admits any subjects for engagement), ahead of the adjudicated 2017 Abaza same; whether the 2017 same+partial entry remains a status change under decision 7 is the adjudicator's call.
- Engagement, panobinostat-leukemia-cells track: Int J Oncol 2014 (doi 10.3892/ijo.2014.2555, PMID 25051119, epub 2014-07-22) compares HL-60 cells treated with azacitidine plus panobinostat (caspase-3, Nor-1, NUR77, p15, p21 up; Bcl-xL down) with PBMCs from the Tan 2014 phase Ib/II: NUR77 and p21 rose after cycle 1 and more in responders than non-responders. Makes the 2014 Tan entry a stated comparison (comparison_stated true, this paper as src or model_src) instead of a drafter pairing; the checker's Giles 2006 re-dating still sets the track's first comparison.

## non-small-cell-lung-carcinoma-alk-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Within-class head-to-head: when the model ranked one member above another (Ba/F3 panel: brigatinib > alectinib) and the trial found no difference (ALTA-3) while the class still works, does the ranking miss score contra on that track, or is it outside class-level efficacy?
- Patient-level predictions and endpoint_role: when the test is the primary endpoint (ORR) analysed by predicted genotype (Shaw 2019), is endpoint_role 'primary' or null? The rules only define primary/poc_gate for class-level calls.
- model_kind has no value for engineered cell lines (Ba/F3); 'ex_vivo' implies patient material. Is one slug covering engineered Ba/F3 mutants plus patient-derived resistant lines one system or two?
- Engineered-mutant xenografts (Ba/F3 or NIH3T3 EML4-ALK G1202R in mice, the source of lorlatinib's Ceff) sit between the NSCLC-cell-line xenograft track and the mutant-cell track; which model_id owns them?
- Engagement by response rate: when no human PD readout (tumour pALK) exists, may mutant-bearing patients' ORR stand in as a 'downstream' engagement readout, or does engagement stay untested?
- A single paper that reproduces a known clinical result (retro) and also makes a new prediction tested in the same paper by resistance-biopsy enrichment (G1202R): one entry or two, and does an in-paper resistance correlation count as an efficacy comparison at all?
- PBPK DDI models in the dose cell (alk-pbpk-ddi): decision 4 scores whether the model's exposure target was reached and sufficient; a PBPK DDI model predicts a co-medication exposure ratio and is verified against DDI studies already in the NDA, so the track can only ever be retro or untested. Does it belong in dose, and is prediction_level 'class' right for a drug-level PK prediction (neither 'does the class work' nor 'which patients respond')?
- One organoid system, two readouts (alk-nsclc-pdo-codrp, Lee 2023): the conventional AUC mis-called the only non-responder while the paper's post-hoc regression-fitted CODRP index (which includes clinical stage) matched 5/5. Is the track scored on the pre-specified readout (#110, contra) or on the index the paper proposes (retro), or are these two tracks under decision 8?
- PDX-derived cell lines (Recondo 2019 MR57-S, lorlatinib-sensitive at IC50 50 nM while patient MR57 progressed): owned by the PDX track (alk-lorlatinib-resistant-pdx) or by the patient-derived resistant-lines track (alk-mutant-cells)?

Missed comparisons:

- ALTA-3 head-to-head (NCT03596866, results posted 2025-02-19; Yang 2023 J Thorac Oncol doi 10.1016/j.jtho.2023.08.010, PMID 37574132): brigatinib not superior to alectinib after crizotinib, PFS HR 0.97, futility met. The Ba/F3 mutant panel (brigatinib FDA review Table 4, bundle 208772) ranked brigatinib above alectinib across mutants; no entry records this within-class miss, and the context note's 'every track matched' overstates.
- Patient-derived organoid track, ALK-positive NSCLC (J Exp Clin Cancer Res 2023, doi 10.1186/s13046-023-02899-4, 8 patients): the conventional AUC readout did not separate responders to crizotinib/alectinib/brigatinib; a growth-rate-weighted index (CODRP) matched clinical responses. A retrodictive patient-level comparison with no model_id in the draft (retro or contra by readout).
- Low confidence: a CKB entry (https://ckb.jax.org/profileResponse/show/25891) reports alectinib failing in an ALK I1171N/F1174I PDX; the primary paper was not resolved within budget, so an alectinib PDX comparison may exist.

## non-small-cell-lung-carcinoma-anti-ctla4-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Biomarker-defined co-primary populations (TMB>=10, PD-L1>=1%, PD-L1>=50%) in an Unselected context: the brief decides only the reverse case (unselected trial counts for a biomarker context at >=80%). If they count, CheckMate 227's first same is 2018 (Hellmann 2018) and KEYNOTE-598's contra is 2020; if not, the first same is 9LA 2020 and the first contra S1400I 2021.
- When the model only compared the combination with each monotherapy (Curran 2010: 'more than twice as effective as either alone'), does a combination-versus-chemotherapy win (CheckMate 227, 9LA) count as same, or is combination-versus-anti-PD-1 (KEYNOTE-598, S1400I) the only admissible test of the anti-CTLA-4 contribution?
- Dose when the model stated no exposure, occupancy or regimen target at all (dose entries 3-5): does the efficacy-tied table still score same/contra as drafted, or is the cell untested because nothing was carried into patients?
- Track identity: wild-type mice with a hamster surrogate (Leach 1996) versus hCTLA-4 transgenic mice dosed with ipilimumab (BLA 125377) on the same tumour lines, one model_id or two? The brief says a different system gets a new slug but does not say whether the antibody or the host genotype defines the system.
- A failed multi-component regimen (CheckMate 73L: concurrent nivolumab with chemoradiation plus ipilimumab consolidation vs durvalumab consolidation): does it score contra for the anti-CTLA-4 class when the class's contribution cannot be isolated from the design?
- Dating a paired (comparison_stated false) safety comparison: the year of the patient-side source the drafter chose (FDA review 2011) or the earliest tier-1 patient source showing the harm (melanoma irAE reports from 2003), given rule 6 says the earliest date the comparison was public?

Missed comparisons:

- KEYNOTE-598 (NCT03302234): Phase 3, ipilimumab 1 mg/kg q6w added to pembrolizumab in PD-L1 TPS>=50% NSCLC, co-primary OS/PFS, stopped for futility (OS HR 1.08, PFS HR 1.06; grade 3-5 AE deaths 13.1% vs 7.5%). Merck halt announced 2020-11-10 (tier 5; onclive.com/view/keynote-598-trial-examining-pembrolizumab-ipilimumab-in-nsclc-stopped-for-futility); Boyer 2021 JCO 10.1200/JCO.20.03579 (PMID 33513313, epub 2021-01-29); registry results 2021-09-10. A direct test of the dual-blockade mice's combination-beats-monotherapy prediction: contra on syngeneic-mouse-dual-blockade (efficacy, and dose derived_from efficacy).
- Lung-MAP S1400I (NCT02785952): Phase 3, nivolumab +/- ipilimumab 1 mg/kg q6w in previously treated squamous NSCLC (unselected), primary OS, closed for futility, OS not significantly different. Gettinger 2021 JAMA Oncol 10.1001/jamaoncol.2021.2209 (PMID 34264316, online 2021-07-16); registry results 2022-01-11. Second contra on the dual-blockade track, in an unselected population.
- CheckMate 73L (NCT04026412): Phase 3, nivolumab + chemoradiation then nivolumab + ipilimumab vs chemoradiation then durvalumab in unresectable stage III NSCLC; primary PFS (Arm A vs C) not met. BMS press release 2024-05-10 (tier 5; businesswire.com/news/home/20240510499555/en); registry results 2025-07-24 (tier 3). Confounded regimen (concurrent nivolumab plus ipilimumab consolidation): a missed failure whose scoring is rule question 5.
- Du 2018 Cell Res 10.1038/s41422-018-0012-z (PMID 29463898): CTLA4-humanized knock-in mice in which ipilimumab itself induced severe irAEs, worse with anti-PD-1, reducing Treg/Teff among autoreactive T cells. A safety retrodiction with product_in_model true (retro, 2018, imported from melanoma); no track in the draft.
- Selby 2016 PLoS One 10.1371/journal.pone.0161779 (PMID 27610613): cynomolgus combination toxicology with dose-dependent immune-related GI inflammation absent from single-agent studies (a 2016 entry on cynomolgus-toxicology; contra track stays contra), plus human-PBL superantigen/MLR cytokine assays, an ex_vivo system with no track. Cited only inside judgements.
- FDA PharmR BLA 125377 p.2 (http://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2011/125377Orig1s000PharmR.pdf): SA1/N, MC-38 and CT-26 tumours in hCTLA-4 transgenic mice dosed with ipilimumab itself (product_in_model true), the system BMS filed, folded into the wild-type-mouse surrogate track with product_in_model false; arguably its own model_id (rule question 4).
- Ji 2019 Clin Cancer Res 10.1158/1078-0432.CCR-18-4083 (PMID 31085720, online 2019-05-13): cynomolgus monkeys dosed nivolumab 20 mg/kg plus ipilimumab 15 mg/kg weekly developed myocarditis with colon, kidney, liver and endocrine infiltrates, built as an ICI-myocarditis model after the patient syndrome was known. A 2019 retro entry on the existing cynomolgus-toxicology track (same system as Selby 2016), not a new model_id; no entry in the draft. [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models]
- Axelrod 2022 Nature 10.1038/s41586-022-05432-3 (PMID 36385524, online 2022-11-16): the same Pdcd1-/- Ctla4+/- mice (ctla4-het-pdcd1-null-mouse track) show clonal CD8 T cells specific for alpha-myosin, with alpha-myosin-reactive T cells then found in patients with ICI myocarditis. A 2022 retro entry on the added track, status unchanged; no entry in the models file. [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models]

## non-small-cell-lung-carcinoma-anti-igf1r-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Efficacy pairing across partner drugs: the A549 model tested h7C10 + vinorelbine or + anti-EGFR antibody 225; the trials ran R1507 + erlotinib (TKI), cixutumumab + pemetrexed/cisplatin and + carboplatin/paclitaxel/bevacizumab. Does 'conditions the model never tested' cover a different combination partner of the same mechanism (EGFR blockade, cytotoxic chemo), or only population/biomarker conditions? Ramalingam attributes the failure to lack of a predictive biomarker, which the unselected A549 model also lacked, so I kept contra.
- Opening (untested) entries at model_first_use_year: may they cite a later patient-side source (dose and safety cells cite 2012 and 2016 papers in 2005 entries), or must the opening entry cite the model source dated to that year?
- When two prospective models of member drugs disagree before the trial (h7C10/A549 predicting near-complete inhibition; R1507 22-line panel predicting low unselected sensitivity), which is 'the best model in the context' for scoring the R1507 trial?
- Safety contra whose patient harm is documented for the same drug only in another indication (TED) while NSCLC trials of a different member (cixutumumab) show the same on-target harm: imported_from the TED id, or a direct NSCLC comparison via the pooled on-target rule?

Missed comparisons:

- safety: contra entry, model = cynomolgus monkey repeat-dose toxicology of R1507/teprotumumab (Roche reports Jul-Aug 2005, up to 39 weeks; product_in_model true), source FDA BLA 761143 Pharmacology/Toxicology review (http://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2021/761143Orig1s000PharmR.pdf, tier 2, comparison_stated true). Quote (11.4): 'the monkey model predicted the clinical reduced weight gain/weight loss and decreased serum ALP, but not the clinical infusion reactions, exacerbation of inflammatory bowel disease, or hyperglycemia.' Executive summary: 'The monkey toxicology studies did not predict these effects.' Patient harm is in TED patients, so imported_from the thyroid eye disease MONDO id; hyperglycemia is on-target (IGF1R/IR) and also seen with cixutumumab in NSCLC (Novello 2017 9.4% vs 1.2%; E3508). Year = review posting (approval Jan 2020; file under nda/2021). Monkeys can show hyperglycemia, so this is a scored miss, not silence a species could not show.
- efficacy (candidate competing model): Gong et al. 2009 PLoS One, doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0007273 (PMID 19806209), a member-drug (R1507) NSCLC model: in 22 NSCLC lines only 5 were moderately sensitive, linked to high total IGF-1R, i.e. it predicted little benefit in unselected NSCLC. Published Oct 2009, before the Ramalingam result (Oct 2011). Not the model the draft scored; recorded so the adjudicator can decide whether it, not A549/h7C10, is the 'best model' for the R1507 trial.
- No press release, SEC filing or EMA document found reporting an additional member-drug NSCLC failure; figitumumab's NSCLC phase 3 failures are outside the member list (figitumumab is not a member) and correctly absent.
- efficacy (parallel track r1507-nsclc-cell-line-panel): likely 2011 contra, patient side Habben et al. J Clin Oncol 29:15_suppl 7584 (doi:10.1200/jco.2011.29.15_suppl.7584, NO21160 R1507+erlotinib): search-engine text of the abstract reads 'No significant impact on treatment was observed when the PFS rates of patient subgroups defined by other biomarkers, including IGF1R level, were compared'; abstract body 403 on ascopubs and absent from Europe PMC/Crossref, so it needs a verbatim read before entry.

## non-small-cell-lung-carcinoma-anti-pdcd1-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Is prior anti-PD-(L)1 progression (COSTAR Lung, dostarlimab + docetaxel, OS HR 0.96) inside this context's 'Unselected' population, or a condition the MC38 model never tested? The draft keeps efficacy `same` and lists it in patient_trials; if it is in-population, the one-contra-beats-same rule would turn the mc38 track contra in 2025/2026.
- May a track carry a second entry with the same status that only adds `margin_stated: true` (dose 3, decision 16) or `product_in_model: true` (efficacy 4), given 'one entry per change of status' and `patient_trials` for non-status-changing trials? Lint accepts them.
- Are wild-type MC38 with surrogate RMP1-14 (dostarlimab) and MC38 in human PD-1 knock-in mice with the product (toripalimab) one system (`mc38-mouse-pd1-blockade`) or two tracks? The draft merges them; the T-cell-engrafted NSG MC38 and camrelizumab's hPD-1-transgenic MC38 would stretch the same slug further.
- Which `model_kind` fits an in-vitro receptor-proteome screen on cultured cells (safety 3)? No in_vitro enum; the draft used ex_vivo.
- Should a dose entry whose patient-side PK/AUC is from another indication (dose 3: NPC programme, same 240 mg q3w regimen as CHOICE-01) carry `imported_from` as safety entries do?
- What tier is a sponsor protocol document posted on ClinicalTrials.gov (the only public source for camrelizumab's own mouse data), and can it serve as `model_src` (tiers 1-3 required)?
- When a member's own model under-called a drug that later worked (camrelizumab 20% TGI, not significant, then CameL positive), is that `same` direction-only, `contra` (magnitude opposite), or set aside? The `neg` rule covers only the failed-drug direction.

Missed comparisons:

- Camrelizumab's own in-vivo model, which the draft says does not exist: C57 human PD-1 transgenic mice bearing MC38(PD-L1) tumours, SHR-1210 3 mg/kg i.p. Q2Dx7 gave 20.40% tumour inhibition (not significant vs HIgG control); combination with apatinib 63.07%. Source: sponsor protocol for NCT03092895, section 1.5.5, https://cdn.clinicaltrials.gov/large-docs/95/NCT03092895/Prot_000.pdf (tier uncertain: sponsor document on the registry). Would be the member's own model side for CameL/CameL-sq with product_in_model true, and the weak monotherapy number is an under-call of a drug that worked.
- Toripalimab's second and third in-vivo systems in its own package with no track: NSG mice bearing MC38 engrafted with allogeneic CD8+ CTLs, where toripalimab 10 mg i.p. twice weekly had only 'minor antitumor activity' (Study TR003, Figure 8), and human-PBMC-engrafted mice where toripalimab expanded effector-memory T cells (PD readout). Source: FDA BLA 761240 multidisciplinary review pp.43 and 49, https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2023/761240Orig1s000MultidisciplineR.pdf.
- Not found (one WebSearch each): any NSCLC patient-derived organoid or PBMC co-culture, humanized PDX, or other patient-level model compared with response to camrelizumab, toripalimab, dostarlimab or spartalizumab; and any member-drug Phase 3 failure in NSCLC other than COSTAR Lung (already listed). Spartalizumab's own preclinical package was not searched (budget); its NSCLC data are Phase 1b combinations only, as the draft says.

## non-small-cell-lung-carcinoma-egfr-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- The cell shows the BEST model's status. When a different member drug's own model (mobocertinib exon 20 Ba/F3/PDX; naquotinib xenografts) fails in its Phase 3, must that model get its own scored entries in the same cell, or only a note? The rules say 'one drug's contra beats another's same on the same model' but are silent on other models within one class.
- Population when the tile gives none and members span first-line sensitising, T790M-acquired and exon 20 insertion settings: one context (EGFR-mutant) or one per biomarker? The drafter's T790M-or-exon20 choice excludes FLAURA and SOLAR.
- EXCLAIM-2 / SOLAR failed against an active comparator while the drug's activity (ORR ~30%) matched the model's prediction of regression. Is failing to beat an active comparator, which no model tested, contra or untested ('conditions the model never tested')?
- Rociletinib: regulatory rejection because unconfirmed responses overstated ORR, with no failed pre-specified primary endpoint. Is that an efficacy contra, a partial same, or unscored?
- Brain-metastasis pairing: a parenchymal brain-metastasis mouse model paired with a leptomeningeal-only trial at twice the label dose (BLOOM). Is LM disease 'a condition the model never tested' (untested) or the same CNS-activity question (same)?
- Organoid studies whose enrolment keeps only patients with response evaluations 'consistent with' the organoid test: is a selection-filtered cohort admissible as same, or should it score at most low-confidence same / untested?

Missed comparisons:

- Naquotinib (ASP8273, class member): Phase 3 SOLAR (NCT02588261, first-line EGFR-activating-mutation NSCLC vs erlotinib/gefitinib, primary PFS by IRR) terminated May 2017 on IDMC futility/benefit-risk; registry results posted 2019-02-12; Astellas press release May 2017 (e.g. fiercebiotech.com/biotech/astellas-scraps-nsclc-program-after-look-at-phase-3-data). Its own xenograft model (Hirano et al. 2015, Oncotarget; DOI not verified) predicted activity. This is outside the draft's stated population (T790M or exon 20) only because the draft narrowed it.
- Mobocertinib: an efficacy entry on its own exon 20 model (Gonzalvez 2021 Cancer Discov, Ba/F3/PDX) paired with EXCLAIM-2 (PMID 39879577, 2025; HR 1.04, crossed futility) is only noted in patient_trials, not scored; NCT04129502 has no posted results (completed 2026-09-22). Takeda withdrew the drug in 2023 (press release, tier 5).
- Osimertinib FLAURA (first-line sensitising EGFR, NEJM 2018, 10.1056/NEJMoa1713137) is the class's main efficacy datum for the same xenograft system (PC9 Ex19del models in Cross 2014) and is absent because the population excludes first line.
- Rociletinib: FDA ODAC April 2016 and complete response letter June 2016 after confirmed ORR fell to about 30% from the 59% reported earlier; hyperglycaemia in patients. Not scored; at minimum belongs in patient_trials or a note.
- Olmutinib: Boehringer Ingelheim returned rights in 2016 after fatal toxic epidermal necrolysis/SJS; a safety datum for another member on its own tox package, not recorded.
- Brain-metastasis model track: AURA3 CNS analysis (Wu et al., J Clin Oncol 2018, osimertinib vs platinum-pemetrexed in T790M NSCLC with CNS metastases; CNS ORR 70% vs 31%; DOI 10.1200/JCO.2018.77.9363 not verified here) is the randomised CNS comparison for the PC9 brain-metastasis model; secondary/subgroup endpoint, so at most a note unless marked poc_gate.
- Brain-metastasis dose track: BLOOM osimertinib LM cohort first reported at ASCO 2016 (Yang et al., JCO 34:15_suppl 9002), before Ballard 2016 epub; meeting abstract, not an admissible tier, recorded for dating only.
- Organoid track: Hu et al. 2021 Nat Commun (PMID 33972544, doi 10.1038/s41467-021-22676-1) compared lung-cancer-organoid TKI responses with patient outcomes, but for gefitinib/afatinib (outside this tile's third-gen/exon 20 class); not a track here, noted so the organoid system is not mistaken as first compared in 2023 across EGFR TKIs.

## non-small-cell-lung-carcinoma-hsp90aa1-hsp90ab1-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Dating (decision 7): when a tier-5 press release reports a trial failure nine months before the tier-3 registry posting (GALAXY-2: 2015-10-20 vs 2016-07-01), does the press release set `year` while the higher-tier record sets the status, or is `year` limited to the sources that can set status?
- Decision 10 boundary: a model whose published report (Jorge 2018, epub 2018-08-28) post-dates the registry results of the trial it motivated (NCT01854034, started 2013, results 2018-04-10). Not 'motivated by the patient result', so not a retrodiction, but no public model source pre-dates the trial. Prospective `same`, and is `model_first_use_year` the trial start (2013, proxy) or the model's epub (2018)?
- `model_kind` has no value for cell-line or Ba/F3-only models; the draft used `animal` for the exon-20 track. Which value is intended?
- Safety (decision 4): does an efficacy paper's incidental claim ('no evidence of cardiac or liver toxicity', Ying 2012) constitute a safety model whose silence on GI toxicity is 'askable', or is only a toxicology study's silence scored? The safety-2 correction rests on the first reading.
- Decision 12 for safety models dosed only with non-member drugs of the same mechanism (Kanamaru 2014 dog retina, Chugai HSP90 inhibitors): a sibling-drug track with `product_in_model: false`, or outside the class?
- Decision 4 (#58) for efficacy: the decided text requires only that 'the model made the null a prediction'; the #58 recommendation also asks that the null be discriminating. The GEMM (De Raedt 2011) meets both, so efficacy 7 is same; confirm that a sibling-drug (IPI-504) monotherapy null transfers to a member (ganetespib) on the same terms as a positive sibling prediction (product_in_model false).
- Decision 10 wording: the rule says 'post-dates and is motivated by the patient result'; the models-check task says 'run after the patient result was known'. For efficacy 7 the KRAS interim (Synta 8-K 2011-06-04) preceded De Raedt's print (2011-09) but the paper never names the drug or trial. Scored under the decided (motivated-by) text; if the stricter gloss is adopted, the entry needs the experiment run date, which is not public.

Missed comparisons:
