- 9. [models check] Does the opening untested entry of a model first used after the patient result was known (InflammaSkin engagement, 2020, eleven years after NCT00875277) carry retrodiction: true, or does the flag attach only to entries that make a comparison (as drafted, false)?
- 10. [models check] Is intertriginous/anogenital/facial psoriasis (Hashim 2020, n=21) inside the tile's population 'Unselected (mild-to-moderate plaque)'? If yes it is a patient_trials row on the contra track under decision 6; if no it is out of scope and goes in the note only. Same 2% BID regimen as NCT01300052, so the dose table's 'higher dose later worked' clause does not apply.

Missed comparisons:

- Benzoxaborole (crisaborole, compd3) Th17-cytokine models from Anacor after the psoriasis trials: cell-culture IL-23/IL-17/IL-22 release and topical compd3 suppressing PMA-induced IL-17F/IL-22/IL-23 transcription in mouse skin, with the paper itself stating the class 'has demonstrated therapeutic benefit for psoriasis ... in phase 2' (comparison_stated). A retrodiction entry (retro vs the bilateral trials, contra vs NCT01300052) on the phorbol-ester or PBMC track is owed. Dong et al. 2016 J Pharmacol Exp Ther, doi 10.1124/jpet.116.232819, PMID 27353073, online 2016-06-27; abstract level; product_in_model true in vitro, compd3 in vivo.
- Crisaborole in a mouse psoriasis-like dermatitis model (dampened myeloid cells and itch), a later animal model with the member drug run after the 2011 psoriasis result, abstract asserting crisaborole 'has shown efficacy in patients with psoriasis'. Retrodiction track with no entry; adjudicator to decide retro (bilateral trials) vs contra (NCT01300052). Exp Dermatol 2023;32:e14722, doi 10.1111/exd.14722, PMID 36461082, online 2022-12-19; abstract level, inducer not stated so it may share model_id pde4-mouse-imiquimod-psoriasiform.
- Not missing, checked: ClinPharmR lists '9 studies in subjects with psoriasis' (crisaborole systemic PK); a topical with no model exposure target gives nothing to compare, so no dose entry is owed. LEO 29102 skin-explant cAMP (Pharm Res 2020) and crisaborole AD biomarkers (JACI 2019) are atopic dermatitis and do not import.
- [models check] Youn 2023 inducer confirmed from the PMC10066830 full text (NCBI efetch): '70 ul (62.5 mg) of imiquimod (5% Taro Pharmaceuticals) was applied to both sides of the mouse ear daily for four consecutive days', 2% crisaborole ointment 4 h after each application. So Youn 2023 (doi 10.1111/exd.14722, PMID 36461082, online 2022-12-19) is a retrodiction entry owed on the existing pde4-mouse-imiquimod-psoriasiform track, not a new model_id: year 2022, comparison_stated true ('has shown efficacy in patients with psoriasis', its reference 14 = Hashim 2020), result 'crisaborole treatment significantly reduced ear thickness and epidermal thickness compared to vehicle treatment', product_in_model true, retro vs the cited Hashim 2020 result; whether NCT01300052 makes it contra is the adjudicator's call. Resolves the inducer question in the earlier missing item.
- [models check] Agree with the model sweep that Dong 2016 JPET (doi 10.1124/jpet.116.232819, PMID 27353073, online 2016-06-27; abstract via Europe PMC, full text 403) adds no model_id: its human monocyte/T-cell TNF-alpha/IL-23/IL-17/IL-22 release belongs on pde4-human-pbmc-cytokine-release (product_in_model true) and its topical compd3 vs PMA-induced mouse-skin cytokine transcription on pde4-mouse-phorbol-ear-edema (product_in_model false), each as a 2016 retrodiction entry with comparison_stated true ('a new topical class that has demonstrated therapeutic benefit for psoriasis and atopic dermatitis in phase 2 or phase 3 studies').
- [models check] Patient trial absent from the draft's crisaborole efficacy track (no 'Hashim', '31279028' or 'intertriginous' anywhere in the draft): Hashim 2020 J Am Acad Dermatol 82:360-365 (doi 10.1016/j.jaad.2019.06.1288, PMID 31279028, online 2019-07-03; abstract read via Europe PMC), crisaborole 2% ointment BID vs vehicle 2:1 in 21 participants with intertriginous, anogenital or facial psoriasis, TLSS improvement 66% vs 9% at 4 weeks, P=.0011, single centre, not registered under crisaborole on ClinicalTrials.gov. Owed as a patient_trials row on pde4-mouse-phorbol-ear-edema (decision 7: a trial fact that changes nothing goes in patient_trials; decision 6: the 2011 contra stands), population noted as site-specific; rule question 10.

## psoriasis-rorc-inverse-agonists

Rule questions:

- When the topical drug fails the engagement readout in the same biopsied field, does the efficacy cell become untested (target not reached, decision 4 row 1) even though the engagement model, not a PK model, set the target? The dose table is written for exposure. The draft scored engagement and efficacy both contra, so one failure counted twice.
- Engagement entry 3: the 'model' (human whole-blood ex vivo IL-17 assay) and the human readout (the same assay on dosed subjects' blood) are the same measurement. Does a human ex-vivo assay whose own readout is repeated in dosed humans count as a model prediction that can turn back a contra, or is it close to tautological?

Missed comparisons:

- Engagement, VTP-43742: a peer-reviewed healthy-volunteer PK/PD paper ('Safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of VTP-43742, a RORγt inhibitor, in normal healthy volunteers', ResearchGate 366800762, c. 2022-23) is a tier-1 source for the whole-blood IL-17 readout; it does not predate the 2021 registry date, so it adds no status change. Not opened.
- Efficacy patient_trials: NCT03835481 (BI 730357 long-term extension) TERMINATED 'Due to company decision', results posted 2022-11-18. This was a program stop, not an efficacy failure under decision 4 ('Failed' ≠ development stopped).
- Safety: NCT03339999 registry gives whyStopped 'safety reason' (verified). The draft says so, but no source says what the safety signal was.

## rheumatoid-arthritis-anti-csf2-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Decision 4 #32 vs the primary-endpoint rule: BAROQUE missed DAS28-CRP remission at all five doses (best p=0.053) but had significant secondaries (DAS28-CRP change, EULAR response, SDAI/CDAI, pain at 90 mg) and the source's conclusion calls it neither match nor miss. Does 'missed primary + significant secondary is untested' cover a dose-ranging trial that missed at every dose, or only a single-dose miss? This is the hinge for efficacy 3.
- Decision 4 case 4 ('reached, failed, a higher dose later worked: efficacy contra then same') vs decision 6 (contra sticky unless patient_signal_reversed) and lint: when the corrected weekly regimen then met ACR20 in contRAst 1 and 2, can efficacy return to same without patient_signal_reversed? The draft could not apply case 4 as written; under the proposed correction (BAROQUE untested) the conflict disappears, but the rule text still contradicts itself.
- Dating by tier 5: decision 7 names 'the earliest admissible source that makes the comparison', and a sponsor press release (GSK 2022-10-27) preceded the registry (2023-07-17) and paper (2023-09-11) records of the contRAst 3 miss by a year. Does the press release set year, and if so should the entry carry the tier-5 src with trial_unreported, or keep the tier-1 src with the earlier year?
- Decision 5 'first patient dataset that could have shown the harm': for a slowly developing harm such as PAP, what exposure qualifies: a 4-week IV course with SP-D and PFT monitoring (MOR103, 2014), 12 weeks with KL-6/SP-D (NEXUS, 2018 registry), or about a year (BAROQUE, 2020)? The proposed 2014 date rests on the trial having monitored the harm's own surrogate.
- model_kind for an acquired human loss-of-function state: anti-GM-CSF-autoantibody PAP is the closest human analogue of the class's pharmacology but is autoimmune, not genetic; human_genetic is the nearest enum. Should it be its own track, and does a model that predicts harm only above a neutralisation threshold score same when partial pharmacological neutralisation produced no harm?
- Engagement readout compartment: the 22E9 model's PD readout was joint-washout TNF and IL-1beta; patients were sampled only in serum (Genovese 2020: no change in 110 of 112 serum biomarkers). Is a serum measurement of the same analyte admissible (contra on the 22E9 engagement track) or inadmissible (untested, as drafted)?
- Model-side admissibility of a registry-hosted trial protocol: decision 2 tier 3 is 'registry results record with posted outcome data' and #125 makes a registry protocol narrative with no results a sponsor statement; the contRAst 3 protocol (IND 121958, IRB-approved 2020-01-21, uploaded 2023-01-31 to a record with results) is the only public carrier of otilimab's unpublished GLP monkey NOEL. Is it tier 3 on the model side, or a sponsor statement like tier 5 ('never for the model side'), which would delete the cyno-tox-otilimab-26wk track? If admitted, does its year follow approval (2020) or upload (2023)?
- A toxic ceiling that held: decision 16 scores the stated NOEL margin in dose (same) and counts a window-closing harm once, but says nothing about a correctly placed margin with no harm. #58 (null-null is same when the model made the null a prediction) would also score the cyno safety cell same, flipping the safety headline from the knockout's contra; the draft leaves it untested. Is a held ceiling counted once (dose only) like a closing harm?

Missed comparisons:

- Human anti-GM-CSF-autoantibody PAP as the human loss-of-function analogue the trials themselves relied on for lung monitoring (Behrens 2015 PMC4431325: 'Because high levels of GM-CSF autoantibodies have been associated with idiopathic pulmonary alveolar proteinosis ... serum surfactant D levels were measured'; Kitamura 1999 doi:10.1084/jem.190.6.875; Uchida 2004 doi:10.1182/blood-2003-05-1565). A separate track: it predicts PAP only above a neutralising-autoantibody threshold, so no PAP at partial pharmacological neutralisation may be same for it while contra for the knockout.
- MOR103 Phase 1b/2a (Behrens 2015, doi:10.1136/annrheumdis-2013-204816, PMID 24534756, epub 2014-02-17, NCT01023256 results 2014-05-08, n=96) and NEXUS (Taylor 2019 full text, PMC6471864: 'biomarkers with the potential for early detection of pulmonary alveolar proteinosis (carcinoembryonic antigen, KL-6, and SP-D), there were no trends associated with namilumab treatment') as earlier patient datasets on the knockout PAP track; the draft said the abstracts lacked a quotable sentence but the OA full texts have one.
- GSK press release 2022-10-27 'GSK provides update on contRAst phase III programme for otilimab' (gsk.com; tier 5): contRAst 3 'did not demonstrate statistical significance on the primary endpoint of ACR20', contRAst 1 and 2 met theirs, 'GSK has decided not to progress with regulatory submissions'. The draft's note says 'dropped in 2022' but no entry cites it; it is the earliest public record of the miss.
- Importable safety datasets for members with no RA safety data in the draft: lenzilumab LIVE-AIR COVID-19 Phase 3 (Temesgen 2022, doi:10.1016/S2213-2600(21)00494-X, PMID 34863332) and namilumab CATALYST (Fisher 2022, doi:10.1016/S2213-2600(21)00460-4, PMID 34922649), transferable with imported_from per decision 12; strengthens-only, no status change.

## rheumatoid-arthritis-btk-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Safety silence and species capability: does a dog toxicology study that measured platelet aggregation as 'unremarkable' count as a model silent on a harm the species could have shown, when human BTK-inhibitor bleeding is mediated by GPVI platelet signalling whose BTK dependence differs across species (Tec compensation in rodents)? The rules do not say who decides species capability or on what evidence.
- Dose for a failed drug whose dose basis is unpublished (BMS-986142, poseltinib): the dose table assumes we know whether the model's exposure target was reached. When no source states the dose basis, is the dose entry untested (draft's implicit choice) or does efficacy contra still stand without the dose check (draft scores efficacy contra on BMS-986142 regardless)?

Missed comparisons:

- Safety, imported_from mantle cell lymphoma (acalabrutinib is a member): FDA NDA 210259 Multidiscipline Review (2017, https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2017/210259Orig1s000MultidisciplineR.pdf, in the source bundle) reports the 91-day dog study 'Platelet Aggregation: Unremarkable' and nonclinical platelet assays showing 'fewer effects than ibrutinib', while 39/124 (31.5%) of pivotal MCL patients had hemorrhage (1 major). A paired (comparison_stated false) on-target-harm entry, likely contra (model silent on bleeding) dated 2017 if the dog is judged able to show BTK-dependent platelet dysfunction; TAS5315 bleeding in RA (Takeuchi 2023) is the in-disease echo. See rule question.
- Efficacy patient_trials: NCT02387762 (acalabrutinib + MTX, Phase 2) registered primary endpoint was DAS28-CRP at week 4; registry results 2019-03-26 show 5.40 vs placebo 5.05 (no analysis posted). It is the earliest member primary-endpoint miss, not exploratory; same year as the cell's contra, so no status or year change.
- Efficacy/dose: spebrutinib NCT01975610 registered primary was ACR20 at week 4 (not met, 41.7% vs 21.7%, P=0.25, Schafer 2019 epub 2019-11-13) with median patient BTK occupancy 83%: a member that reached the model's occupancy target and missed its primary endpoint in 2019, independent of the BMS-986142 dose question.

## rheumatoid-arthritis-syk-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- What tier is a peer-reviewed-society meeting abstract with no DOI or PMID (ACR 2014, Arthritis Rheumatol suppl)? Decision 2 tier 1 needs a DOI/PMID; tier 5 is never allowed for the model side, yet here the ONLY model source for MK-8457 (rat AIA/CIA, 69% PK-PD target) is such an abstract. The whole rat track rests on it.
- Decision 16: does a sponsor's healthy-volunteer MAD tolerability statement (200 mg BID x10 d) count as a 'stated margin', given it is clinical data, not a model? If yes, MK-8457's 24-week serious infections at 100 mg BID are toxicity at the effective exposure (dose contra, blocked_by toxicity) rather than a safety miss; the 'counted once' clause points to safety because nothing predicted infection, but then margin_stated=true on a dose same entry is contradictory.
- A trade-press report of a sponsor's exchange disclosure (Korea Biomedical Review quoting Oscotec) is not a press release or SEC filing: admissible as tier 5? And when a tier-3 registry record of the same trial later posts (2024-07-31), does the entry keep the tier-5 src and 2021 year (decision 7, earliest public date) or move to the registry src, 2024 and trial_unreported=false?
- A development model published only after the trial failed (Cho 2023, received 2022-09, trial topline 2021-01), with no public pre-trial record and no mention of the trial: prospective (retrodiction=false) or retrodiction? Status is contra either way, but the mark differs.
- Does the safety cell accept a healthy-volunteer comparison (lanraplenib cynomolgus bleeding time vs Phase 1 humans, Clarke 2018)? Decision 3 names engagement and dose only.
- A member drug whose only published model is another indication's (lanraplenib, NZB/W lupus) and whose RA trial failed: decision 12 says cross-indication transfers only for safety, so the failure has no efficacy track and is invisible on the tile. Should a class failure with no published model be surfaced some other way than inside another drug's patient_trials?

Missed comparisons:

- Engagement entry on the cynomolgus-bleeding-time-lanraplenib track: Clarke 2018 (10.1016/j.thromres.2018.08.012) reports ex vivo GPVI-stimulated platelet aggregation inhibited in both GS-9876-dosed monkeys and humans, a direct readout in healthy volunteers; the draft uses the paper only for safety.
- Lanraplenib RA Phase 2 conference record: Kivitz et al., ACR 2018 abstract 2518 (https://acrabstracts.org/abstract/gs-9876-a-novel-highly-selective-syk-inhibitor-in-patients-with-active-rheumatoid-arthritis-safety-tolerability-and-efficacy-results-of-a-phase-2-study/), 'Clinical efficacy of GS-9876 in RA was not observed'; also reports patient PK comparable to HV and ex vivo whole-blood PD differences vs placebo. No published arthritis model to score it on (Blomgren 2020 ACS Med Chem Lett abstract and BMC Rheumatol 2021 name only NZB/W lupus; Blomgren full text paywalled, not checked), so no status change unless its full text carries an arthritis model.
- Sponsor-side record for the cevidoplenib RA miss: Oscotec's own 2021-01-07 disclosure (KRX/DART filing) was not located; the draft's tier-5 source is a trade-press report of it. The ITP PRNewswire release (2023) is another indication and not relevant.
- Not missing but noted: entospletinib in K/BxN (Kaposztas 2023, 10.3389/fimmu.2023.1279155) is a non-member sibling; correctly left unscored.

## schizophrenia-chrna7-agonists

Rule questions:

- Does a tier-5 press release or SEC filing set an entry's `year` when a later tier-1 paper on the same trial sets its status? Rule 6 says 'earliest date the comparison was public' but lists only registry posting, online publication, label and print; rule 2 says tier 5 must be used. Five entries here move 1-2 years earlier if yes.
- A retrodiction built against a secondary-endpoint signal (Lieberman 2013 SANS) that a larger primary-endpoint trial had already overturned in public (press release 2013-12-16) before the model paper (2014-07-24): is it `retro` (it reproduced the signal its authors targeted) or `contra` (it failed to reproduce the primary-endpoint result)? Proposed contra.
- Dose 'reached' by mg/kg arithmetic alone: when the model stated no exposure or occupancy target and no human PK was published (AZD0328, TC-5619), is bracketing the animal mg/kg range enough for `same` / `contra derived_from efficacy`, or should the dose cell stay `untested`? The v3 dose table has no row for 'unknown whether reached'.

Missed comparisons:

- Targacept press release 2013-12-16 (Business Wire; copy at https://www.biospace.com/targacept-inc-announces-negative-top-line-results-from-phase-2b-clinical-trial-of-tc-5619-in-schizophrenia-553811): 'TC-5619 did not meet the primary outcome measure, change from baseline on the Scale for the Assessment of Negative Symptoms (SANS) after 24 weeks versus placebo.' Tier-5 record of NCT01488929, 18 months before Walling 2016; it dates efficacy 6 / dose 5 and makes Pedersen 2014 a failed retrodiction.
- Targacept 8-K EX-99.1, 2011-01-19 (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1124105/000119312511009860/dex991.htm): 'TC-5619 met the protocol criteria for a positive result on the primary efficacy outcome measure, the Groton Maze Learning Task (GMLT) ... and was well tolerated' (one-sided p<0.10, adjusted p=0.054). Tier-5 record of NCT01003379, 23 months before Lieberman 2013; dates efficacy 4 / dose 4 / safety 3.
- Mazurov 2012, J Med Chem 10.1021/jm301048a (PMID 23126648, epub 2012-11-13): a tier-1 comparison_stated source that puts the animal models and the phase 2 PoC side by side five weeks before Lieberman; the drafter cites it only in a confidence_note. The 24-word animal-model sentence plus the trial sentence can be quoted in two entries.
- ABT-126 healthy-volunteer 'Pharmacokinetic and Exploratory Biomarker Study' NCT00912431 (2009) and PK in stable schizophrenia NCT01043458 (2009): no results posted, no publication found; a possible engagement datum that cannot be verified, so no track can be opened from it.
- Checked and not missing: Hayward 2017 low-attentive rat 5C-CPT (10.1016/j.euroneuro.2017.01.013) dosed encenicline only; DBA/2 gating and human P50 data are DMXB-A only; PubMed has no ABT-126 preclinical efficacy paper (abuse liability 2017, MPTP monkey 2015 only); AbbVie posted no registry results for NCT01095562/NCT01655680/NCT01678755. TC-5619 ADHD and ABT-126 Alzheimer's trials are other indications and transfer only to safety via imported_from, which the in-disease safety entries make unnecessary.

## schizophrenia-pde10a-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Does a peer-reviewed congress abstract with a DOI (journal supplement poster) count as tier 1 for dating, or only as 'data not shown' with low confidence? It decides whether efficacy contra here is 2012 or 2016.
- When a cell's first sourced entry (2014) comes after model_first_use_year (2008), should the opening untested entry be backdated to model_first_use_year with an earlier source, or does lint accept the gap?
- A conclusion that a model 'refutes the hypothesis' in a trial paper that names the preclinical rationale: is that comparison_stated true, or a pairing under evidence rule 1?
- The TAK-063 healthy-volunteer ketamine challenge (run 2013-14) was prospective for TAK-063 but run after a class member (PF-02545920) had failed in patients (2012), while prediction_level is 'class'. Is a class-level model run after another member's failure a retrodiction? Here status is contra either way.

Missed comparisons:

- DeMartinis et al. 2012, 'Results of a phase 2A proof-of-concept trial with a PDE10A inhibitor in the treatment of acute exacerbation of schizophrenia', SIRS Poster #212, Schizophrenia Research (April 2012), doi 10.1016/s0920-9964(12)70783-1: PF-02545920 monotherapy not superior to placebo. This dates efficacy contra to 2012, and reportedly says PK suggested striatal engagement like that in the preclinical models (possibly dose contra 2012 as well). I saw only the title/Crossref record and a search snippet.
- OMS824 (member): Omeros suspended its PDE10 trials in Oct 2015 after a rat high-dose toxicology finding (Omeros press release 2015, https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/omeros-provides-update-on-pde10-inhibitor-program-322137646.html; 10-K FY2015). This is not a patient comparison, so it does not score, but the draft does not mention it.
- Lu AF11167 NCT03793712 (stopped for futility, completion 2020-08-20, no results posted): the draft lists it with registry_date null. A Lundbeck disclosure could date it, but it does not change the status.
- Roche RG7203 (PDE10A inhibitor) reward-processing fMRI/MID model in healthy volunteers, then tested in chronic schizophrenia patients where reward-processing improvement was absent: Schizophr Bull 2018 suppl abstract S43, https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC5888501/ (search snippet only; also summarised in Frontiers Neurosci 2020, doi 10.3389/fnins.2020.600178). A human-model parallel track for the efficacy (negative symptoms) question, likely contra; RG7203 membership in the class needs confirming.

## schizophrenia-slc6a9-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Dating vs tiers: when a tier-5 press release (Roche, 21 Jan 2014) makes a phase 3 failure public two years before the tier-1 record that sets the status, does decision 7 ('earliest date the comparison was public') move the entry's year to the press-release date, and is `trial_unreported: true` still carried once the trial is later reported?
- Dose from healthy-volunteer PK alone: decision 3 lets HV PK/PD count for dose, but the cell definition reads 'was the target reached, and was it enough'. Is a dose `same` dated when the PD target was hit in volunteers (Rosenbrock 2018) or when efficacy first showed it was enough (Fleischhacker 2021)?
- Track granularity under decision 8: are rodent NMDA-antagonist hyperlocomotion (mouse, amphetamine/L-687,414) and rodent MK-801 cognition/EEG (T-maze, ASSR) one system (same challenge paradigm) or two (different readouts, different predicted symptom domain)? The draft splits them; the checker brief warns against splitting one system.
- A model that correctly predicted no effect (PF-03463275 did not attenuate ketamine effects; the drug then failed): `neg` requires `class_threshold` as a bar the class's own successful drugs cleared, but this class has no successful drug. Is a correct negative prediction `same`, `neg` with a null threshold, or unscorable?
- Can a non-member drug of the given mechanism (Org 25935, GlyT1 inhibitor) open a track as a sibling-drug proxy with `product_in_model: false`, or does the reviewed member list exclude it entirely?
- Retrodiction by publication order: Rosenbrock 2022 cites the positive phase 2 in its introduction but does not state when the experiments ran. Is publication after the clinical result sufficient for `retrodiction: true`, or must the source show the work was run afterwards?

Missed comparisons:

- Human visual-evoked-potential LTP (neuroplasticity) in schizophrenia patients with PF-03463275 (D'Souza 2018, Biol Psychiatry, 10.1016/j.biopsych.2017.12.019, epub 2018-01-31, model_kind human_challenge): LTP peaked at 40 mg bid (~75% occupancy) and chose the 40-60 mg doses of Surti 2023 (10.1016/j.schres.2023.04.010, NCT01911676 results 2023-02-27), which failed on MCCB. A member drug's own model with a prospective dose/efficacy contra; the draft mentions it in judgement but gives it no track.
- Human ketamine-challenge fMRI working-memory model in healthy volunteers with PF-03463275 (same D'Souza 2018: 'PF-03463275 did not attenuate any ketamine-induced effects'): a model that predicted no effect for a drug that then failed (Surti 2023). No track; how to score it is a rule question.
- Roche media release, Basel 21 January 2014 (tier 5): 'two phase III studies of ... bitopertin ... failed to meet their primary endpoints' (FlashLyte NN25310, DayLyte WN25309, PANSS negative factor at 24 weeks); https://www.fiercebiotech.com/biotech/roche-provides-update-on-first-two-of-six-phase-iii-studies-of-bitopertin-schizophrenia-0 . Dates the efficacy and dose contras to 2014.
- Boehringer Ingelheim press release, January 2025 (tier 5): CONNEX topline 'primary and key secondary endpoints were not met'; https://www.boehringer-ingelheim.com/us/connex-clinical-program-topline-results-announcement . Same year as the draft's 2025 entries; no change, but it should be cited as the earliest public date.
- Org 25935 human ketamine challenge in healthy males (D'Souza 2012, Neuropsychopharmacology, 10.1038/npp.2011.295, epub 2011-11-23): a human_challenge system for the GlyT1 mechanism, but Org 25935 is not on the member list, so it is listed here for the rule question, not as a scored track.

## secondary-hyperparathyroidism-casr-positive-modulators

Rule questions:

- Dose with no stated exposure or occupancy target: can a dose entry be 'same' on regimen alone (oral once-daily rat dosing -> oral once-daily titration reached the PTH target, evocalcet) or does it stay 'untested' until an exposure comparison exists? The drafter applied the stricter reading to the peptide's exposure target and the looser one to the small molecule.
- Decision 16 when the only general-toxicology margin is below 1 (FDA 0.7x rat, 0.15x dog; EMA 'no safety margins'), the predicted on-target harm occurred at the effective exposure in 64% of patients, yet titration still reached target: is dose 'same' without margin_stated, 'same' with margin_stated on a reproductive NOAEL, or 'contra' blocked_by toxicity?
- Rule 6 dating across jurisdictions: an EMA EPAR adopted 2016-09-15 (public after the 2016-11-11 EC decision) states the same model-patient comparisons the FDA review stated in 2017; does the earlier regulator's document set the year for a comparison_stated entry?
- Safety silence: a labeled Warning (Parsabiv 5.2 Worsening Heart Failure) resting on a small imbalance (EPAR adjudicated CHF hospitalisation 2.2% vs 1.2%) that the regulator attributes to the class with unknown mechanism, in a species (dog telemetry) that could show cardiac depression: is it a harm the model's silence was contradicted on, or too weak to score?
- One paper, two species, one prediction (rat gastric emptying plus marmoset emesis, Kawata 2018): one track or two model_ids under the 'two systems sharing one' rule?
- Retrodiction timing when the first public model source (Goto 2023) post-dates the trial and approval but the data were plausibly in the 2020 regulatory filing: does an unpublished filing date make the prediction prospective, or is first public date the test? And can a 'strengthens' entry carry retrodiction: true without becoming 'retro'?
- In-silico model whose dataset predates the trial (Chen 2016: phase 1/2 data only; ClinPharmR 4.2.4.1 says its simulations supported the scheme phase 3 used, phase 3 started 2013-03-12) but whose publication (received 2016-01-25, epub 2016-09-17) post-dates trial completion (2014-06) and the NDA (2015-08-24): is rule 10's 'post-dates' the model run or the first public date? Dose 6 'same' depends on the model-run reading; under first-public dating it is retro.
- When a regulator quotes only the post-hoc refit's number for a prediction the earlier model stated qualitatively (dose 6, safety 7): may the entry carry the refit's quote and compute magnitude on the refit's number for a 'same' scored on the prospective statement? Proposed: quote the prospective source, magnitude direction_only, refit number only in the note.

Missed comparisons:

- EMA EPAR Parsabiv EMA/664198/2016 (adopted 2016-09-15; https://www.ema.europa.eu/en/documents/assessment-report/parsabiv-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf): tier-2 source predating the FDA review, carrying 5/6 Nx and adenine rat data, 'rat/human exposure ratio at the rat NOAEL was between 0.22 and 0.82, therefore, there are no safety margins', and 'QTc changes have also been observed in patients'. Dates dose/safety regulator entries to 2016; the draft cites no EMA document.
- Adenine-diet uremic rat (FDA PharmR NDA 208325 Study r20130076, 0.3 mg/kg SC x4 wk: PTG hyperplasia and aortic calcium prevented; EPAR p.17): a second uremic system sharing the 'uremic-rat' slug; needs its own untested track or an explicit two-system slug. Likewise the double-nephrectomised rat of Goto 2023 (doi 10.1016/j.ejphar.2023.175936).
- Patient-derived parathyroid cells ex vivo: etelcalcetide on dispersed human parathyroid cells (primary HPT tissue), EC50 0.42-20.8 umol/L in 4 of 8 responsive cultures, J Bone Miner Metab, doi 10.1007/s00774-020-01158-2 (abstract via search). An ex_vivo track with no SHPT patient comparison: untested, not absent.
- Patient tests of the rat's non-PTH predictions the draft calls untested: NCT03960437 'The Effect of Etelcalcetide on CKD-MBD' (phase 2, results posted 2023-09-21) and NCT03795558 (etelcalcetide PTH-lowering and cardiac endpoints, completed 2021-12). If either measured vascular calcification or LV mass it is the first patient test of the Walter 2013 calcification prediction and sets outcome_tested.
- Monkey toxicology for evocalcet (PMDA Orkedia review 2018-03-08, pmda.go.jp/files/000235777.pdf, lines ~669-674: QTc prolongation in monkeys, 0.4-fold margin, attributed to hypocalcemia): the small-molecule member's QTc prediction came from monkey, not dog; the rat-dog-tox track cites no evocalcet animal source.
- In-vitro hERG (PharmR line ~1410; EPAR 'Parsabiv is not an inhibitor of the hERG channel'): in-silico/in-vitro prediction that QTc is not direct, borne out by the calcium-linked clinical QTc; folded silently into rat-dog-tox.
- PMDA review reports as tier-2 sources: Orkedia 2018-03-08 sets model_filed true for the evocalcet 5/6 Nx rat and false for the GI model (not in the review); the Upasita (upacicalcet, approved 2021-06-23) English review was not found in two searches and would settle upacicalcet model_filed and the Goto 2023 retrodiction question.
- ASP-7991 (checked, nothing to add): four trials (NCT01675518, NCT01872013, NCT01872026 phase 1; NCT02133404 phase 2 vs cinacalcet/placebo, completed 2014-11-10, no results), no discontinuation notice found; draft's 'adds nothing' stands.

## small-cell-lung-carcinoma-anti-pdcd1-antibodies

Rule questions:

- Combination crediting: EXTENTORCH is a placebo-controlled add-on (toripalimab + etoposide-platinum vs placebo + etoposide-platinum, PFS and OS met). Decision 12 (#70) only governs combination-arm `contra`; the HCC-run rule that a combination credits the antibody only when the model dosed the combination is not among the sixteen decisions. Does a placebo-controlled add-on isolate the class drug for `same` (dose 4 and the toripalimab efficacy datum would become same)?
- Dating from tier 5: when a sponsor press release (CheckMate 331, 2018-10-12) or a DOI'd congress abstract makes a failure public before the registry posting (2020) and the paper (2021), does decision 7 set `year` from it while the tier 1/3 record sets the status, and does the entry then need trial_unreported?
- Track identity: is a rat-chimeric surrogate (4H2) in wild-type syngeneic hosts one system with a product-dosed human-PD-1 knock-in MC38 model (toripalimab)? Pooling makes the 2020 sticky contra pre-empt the knock-in model's own record; splitting opens a 2023 track whose only SCLC test is a combination trial.
- Decision 12 (#69) says a sibling-class surrogate dosed in the model (anti-PD-L1 for an anti-PD-1 class) scores `other`, but decision 13 and the status table make `other` safety-only. Can Sen 2019 (anti-PD-L1 in RP(P) SCLC mice) enter the efficacy cell, and with what status?
- model_kind for a tumour-sequencing patient-selection rule (TMB from CheckMate 032): `human_genetic` is the nearest enum but names a germline/genotype system; no enum covers a biomarker rule derived from patients.
- Imported-datum symmetry in safety: a single 2014 regulator statement in another indication establishes both the monkey's miss and the knockout's hit. The draft opened only the miss at 2014 (imported) and the hit at 2018 (in context). Should both tracks open from the same imported datum?
- `magnitude: opposite` is defined for a predicted harm that never appeared; the draft uses it for a silence-miss (model silent, harm appeared). Which value is meant for silence-misses, or should magnitude be null there?
- Product-dosed pooling (models check): hpd1-knockin-mc38 pools two members' products (camrelizumab, Tu 2019; toripalimab, TR003) on one track, and Tu 2019 names only 'hPD-1 transgenic mice' with no strain or supplier. If a transgenic hPD-1 host is not the exon-2 knock-in, the track's opener moves from 2019 to 2023 (first public record of TR003). Does one product-dosed track pool members, and does 'transgenic' pass as the knock-in by system type?
- model_filed at the entry's date (models check): hpd1-knockin-mc38 opens in 2019 with model_filed true on the strength of a 2023 FDA review; rule 7 says use what was known at the entry's date. Is model_filed a property of the system (any later filing) or of the entry (filed by its year)?
- Retrodiction openers (models check): sclc-rpm-msh2-gemm carries a same-year untested opener before its retro (entries 8-9, per #30/#112), while the sibling retrodiction track sclc-rp-syngeneic-mouse (entry 6, Hiatt 2022) carries a lone retro with no opener. Which convention applies to a track whose first class use is the retrodiction itself?

Missed comparisons:

- BMS press release 2018-10-12, 'Phase 3 CheckMate -331 study does not meet primary endpoint of overall survival' (tier 5, patient side; biospace.com mirror of the BMS release) and the ESMO Immuno-Oncology 2018 LBA presentation (Reck et al., Ann Oncol 2018;29 suppl 10): the OS miss was public in 2018, before the 2020-01-09 registry posting; sets year 2018 for dose 3 / efficacy 4 under decision 7. CheckMate 451's miss was likewise announced by BMS in November 2018 (not independently verified here).
- Toripalimab human-PD-1 knock-in MC38 model (FDA 761240 multidisciplinary review 2023, bundle lines 4447-4464, product-dosed, 0.3-10 mg/kg) as its own track rather than pooled under mc38-mouse-pd1-blockade with the 2014 rat-chimeric 4H2 surrogate in wild-type hosts; its only SCLC test is EXTENTORCH (Cheng 2025, PMID 39541202, PMC11565370, quote verified).
- Mtb-infected PD-1 knockout track: imported patient comparison exists, tuberculosis reactivation on anti-PD-1 (Picchi et al. 2018, Clin Microbiol Infect, PMID 29269089, doi 10.1016/j.cmi.2017.12.003); under the safety transfer rule it could move the track from untested to same (imported), as the draft's own confidence_note anticipates.
- Sen et al. 2019 (Cancer Discov, PMC6563834): anti-PD-L1 dosed in Rb1/Trp53/p130-derived syngeneic SCLC models, alone and with PARP/CHK1 inhibitors; a sibling-class surrogate that decision 12 (#69) says scores `other`, which the draft excluded. Taniguchi et al. 2022 (PMC9449677, WEE1 + anti-PD-L1 in SCLC GEMMs) is the same case.
- 2024 mismatch-repair-deficient Rb1/Trp53/Msh2 SCLC mouse (PMC11564195): higher mutational burden and improved survival on checkpoint blockade, a mouse retrodiction of the TMB rule track; whether the antibody was anti-PD-1 or anti-PD-L1 was not confirmed in this check.
- PASSION located: Fan et al. 2021, J Thorac Oncol, PMID 33166719, doi 10.1016/j.jtho.2020.10.002 (epub 2020-11-06); camrelizumab plus apatinib single-arm phase 2, NCT03417895 results posted 2024-08-07 (confirmed). A combination, so no status change; belongs in patient_trials rather than 'not located'.

## type-1-diabetes-mellitus-slc5a2-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Dose cell when the filed model gave only an mg/kg dose-response, stated no exposure, occupancy or regimen target, and the dose inherited from T2D worked: is that `untested` (no prediction to test) or `same` under the table row 'reached, drug worked'? The v3 table has no row for a model that set no target.
- Prospective versus retrodiction when the model paper's online date precedes the trial paper's online date by 30 days but the trial finished 15 months earlier (Vallon 2014 epub 2013-11-13; Cherney 2014 epub 2013-12-13, NCT01392560 completed 2012-08): does 'the clinic already knew' run from trial completion, meeting presentation, or publication?
- A member-drug model in a different physiological state (insulin-replete DIO rat, Devenny 2012) that showed the harm the filed insulin-deficient model missed: is it its own safety track (`same`, direction only) or set aside as a different system, as the draft did?
- A regulator-imposed dose restriction on safety grounds with no model margin (EMA kept dapagliflozin 5 mg only; FDA rejected empagliflozin 2.5 mg): confirm decision 16 leaves dose `untested` and scores the harm once in safety, rather than `contra` with blocked_by toxicity.
- A stated null in a filed model whose physiology precludes the harm (NDA 206111 ZDF rat: hyperinsulinaemic, plasma insulin rose on empagliflozin, 'ketone bodies were unremarkable') set against T1D DKA: `contra` as a failed stated prediction (decision 4 safety row) or `untested` as a condition the model never tested (#35/#72, #87)? #58 settles null-null but not null-in-model/harm-in-patients. [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models]

Missed comparisons:

- Devenny 2012 (https://doi.org/10.1038/oby.2012.59, PMID 22402735, epub 2012-03-08): dapagliflozin raised fasting beta-hydroxybutyrate in diet-induced obese rats, a member-drug rodent model that showed the ketone signal before any T1D trial; the draft sets it aside in judgement. No source compares it with patients, so a candidate track only (rule question 3).
- Vallon 2013 (https://doi.org/10.1152/ajprenal.00409.2012, PMID 23152292, epub 2012-11-14): Sglt2 knockout in Akita mice prevented glomerular hyperfiltration, the same system and prediction as the akita-mouse track one year earlier by genetic deletion; model_first_use_year 2013 should be 2012 (no status change).
- Tahara 2014 (https://doi.org/10.1111/jphp.12223, PMID 24533859, epub 2014-02-17): ipragliflozin in STZ type 1 diabetic rats, the third member on the stz-rat track; same system, no status change, worth citing against Kaku 2019.
- EMA variation assessment report WS/1344 (EMA/CHMP/213138/2019, 31 January 2019, https://www.ema.europa.eu/documents/variation-report/edistride-h-c-4161-ws-1344-epar-assessment-report-variation_en.pdf): tier-2 record of the dapagliflozin T1D DKA (9.87/7.58/4.40 per 100 patient-years for 5 mg/10 mg/placebo, long-term pool), 'No new non-clinical data have been submitted in this application', only the 5 mg dose recommended, and a post-authorisation 'Nonclinical mechanistic model studies ... Ketoacidosis' commitment. The draft says it was not obtained; it changes no status.
- FDA EMDAC briefing document, 13 November 2019 (empagliflozin 2.5 mg, T1D, 14-2 vote): not retrieved (fda.gov/media/132422 returns 404); unverified, tier 2 if found; the draft cites the vote from press coverage only.
- Perkins 2020 (https://doi.org/10.1111/dom.13945, PMC7064984, open access) also carries a beta-hydroxybutyrate exposure-response Emax model built on EASE-3 and qualified by predicting the EASE-2 BHB changes ('minor blood BHB level elevation ... was confirmed'): a safety-cell retrodiction on the empa-t1d-exposure-response-sim track, not drafted. [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models]

## type-2-diabetes-mellitus-ccr2-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Assay-sensitivity failure: when the pre-specified PoC gate (FPG vs placebo) is missed by the member AND by the active comparator (pioglitazone also not different from placebo; placebo baseline 14-21 mg/dL higher), is the member's miss `contra`, or is the comparison inadmissible (`untested`)?
- Does `retro` admit partial agreement (`source_calls_it: partial`) the way `same` does, or does a retrodiction that reproduces direction on one readout (glucose) but not the mechanism readout (HOMA-IR, insulin) fall to `contra` under decision 10?
- Year of a corrected entry when the status-setting higher-tier record (EU CTR posting 2022-03-01) post-dates the first public tier-5 comparison (press release 2011): does the entry carry 2011 (earliest public, rule 6) or 2022 (when the status-setting data were public)?
- Retrodiction by publication date vs model-run date: the DIO/db/db mouse data were presented at the same EASD session (Sept 2011) as the clinical result, and the hCCR2 knock-in paper was received June 2013. Is a model presented simultaneously with the clinic a retrodiction? And should the `retrodiction` flag be a property of the model/track rather than per entry (the draft sets it false on dose, true on efficacy and safety for the same paper)?
- Engagement when model and patients both show ABSENCE of the class's canonical engagement marker (no CCL2 rise, no monocyte fall, measured in the CCX140-B trial): is that scoreable as engagement at all, or only as the safety/selectivity match the draft used?
- Decision 7 on an all-untested track: may the opener (model_first_use_year, proxy source) and a later entry dated to that proxy source's posting both stand, or is the second a no-status-change entry that belongs in patient_trials? (efficacy 5/6 here; engagement 2 is the same pattern and was confirmed.) [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models]

Missed comparisons:

- MCP-1 (CCL2) promoter polymorphism as a patient-level predictor (human_genetic, prediction_level patient): the CCX140-B T2D trial stratified randomisation by MCP-1 polymorphism status with the objective 'Assessment of MCP-1 polymorphism on efficacy responses to CCX140-B treatment' (EU CTR 2009-016051-22 CSR synopsis, https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/trial/2009-016051-22/results). Result not reported in the synopsis; a track at untested.
- Sullivan et al. 2013 Metabolism, 'Experimental evidence for the use of CCR2 antagonists in the treatment of type 2 diabetes', doi:10.1016/j.metabol.2013.06.008 (PMID 23953944, epub 2013-08-15): a mouse-active analogue of CCX140-B in DIO and db/db mice, presented at EASD Sept 2011 alongside the clinical data per the 15 Sep 2011 press release. Same system as the Weisberg track (agree, not a new model_id) but it is the member's own model and should be the model_src for the CCX140-B pairing; the press release then makes the 2011 comparison sponsor-stated.
- Patient side only, changes no status under the primary-endpoint rule: de Zeeuw 2015 Lancet Diabetes Endocrinol doi:10.1016/S2213-8587(15)00261-2 (PMID 26268910), 52-week CCX140-B 5/10 mg in T2D patients with nephropathy (NCT01447147); the longest member exposure in T2D patients, HbA1c secondary only. Belongs in patient_trials.

## type-2-diabetes-mellitus-dpp4-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Are clinical-pharmacology models built on human Phase 1 data (cofrogliptin PopPK/PD + MBMA for HbA1c, Cui 2024; omarigliptin and cofrogliptin C-QTc models) atlas model systems under model_kind in_silico, or out of scope? If in scope, the MBMA's inputs are published trials of non-member gliptins (sitagliptin etc.): is that a model (tier 1 model side) or a meta-analysis of patient trials (tier 4, patient side only, decision 2)?
- Decision 5 and a different harm in the same organ: the monkey necrotic dermatosis was predicted and did not occur (regulator: 'unique only to monkeys' -> contra), yet pemphigoid entered the teneligliptin (2016) and omarigliptin (2018) labels. Does a skin harm of another pathology count as the prediction holding (same), and is animal silence on an autoimmune blistering disease a 'harm the species could have shown' (contra) or not (untested)?
- Decision 16 vs safety for a predicted harm at a misplaced margin: teneligliptin's hERG/monkey model put QTc prolongation at >=15-17x unbound exposure; humans showed it at 160 mg (4x the 40 mg maximum, which was clean). The rule says count once: is this a safety same (predicted, occurred), a dose-ceiling same with margin_stated true (effective exposure tolerated), or a dose contra for the margin being ~4x too wide?
- Decision 10 and late-published preclinical packages: cofrogliptin's monkey PK-PD/allometric prediction of biweekly dosing was published 2026-01-05, after the Phase 3 (completed 2022-01), but the studies are IND-enabling pharmacology run before the clinic. Dated at publication as a prospective same (decision 7), or retrodiction/retro because the paper's comparison post-dates the patient result?
- comparison_stated for regulator reviews: a PMDA review presents the sponsor's rodent OGTT data and the HbA1c trials in one document but explicitly juxtaposes model and patient only for QTc and skin. Is a review comparison_stated: true for every cell it covers, or only where the reviewer draws the comparison?
- Same-mechanism non-members as model-side evidence: the regulator's cutaneous-toxicity comparison for teneligliptin rests on vildagliptin/saxagliptin monkey data and the Januvia DPP-8/9 package. Decision 13 sets cross-class evidence aside; is a sibling drug of the same mechanism outside the member list 'class' evidence (product_in_model: false, allowed) or set aside?

Missed comparisons:

- Regulator packages for two members are public and were not used: teneligliptin PMDA Review Report, English, 5 April 2012 (https://www.pmda.go.jp/files/000153594.pdf; filed 2011-08-26) and omarigliptin (Marizev) PMDA review, Japanese, 2015 (https://www.pmda.go.jp/drugs/2015/P20151007002/170050000_22700AMX01014000_A100_2.pdf). Both are tier 2, carry the rodent OGTT/plasma DPP-4 data (model_filed: true for rodent-dpp4-ogtt), the human PK/PD and HbA1c results, and the nonclinical tox vs clinical safety discussions. Every model_filed: null and the 'review documents not retrieved' confidence notes should be redone from them.
- Cynomolgus necrotic-skin-lesion / DPP-8-9 cutaneous toxicity model (animal-tox track): teneligliptin PMDA review 3.(iii).A.(2).5)-(2).6) and 3.(iii).B.(3): necrotic dermatosis at 100 mg/kg/day (13 wk) and 75 mg/kg/day (52 wk), NOAEL 30 mg/kg/day = 13-25x clinical AUC; applicant and PMDA: 'no clear increase was observed in the incidence of serious necrotic dermatosis or other skin-related adverse drug reactions in clinical studies of teneligliptin ... skin cutaneous toxicity is most likely to be unique only to monkeys' (p. 27). Decision 5 canonical case (regulator calls the finding species-specific) -> contra, magnitude opposite, 2012. Mechanism source cited there: the Januvia package / Lankas et al. 2005 Diabetes doi:10.2337/diabetes.54.10.2988 (rat/dog DPP-8/9 toxicity syndrome) — the selectivity model every member was designed against; no track.
- hERG + dog/monkey telemetry QT model vs thorough-QT (animal-tox track, two members): teneligliptin review 3.(i).B.(2): hERG IC50 3.45 uM (15x unbound Cmax at 40 mg), monkey QTc prolongation at 30 mg/kg (NOEL 10 mg/kg = 17x), dog i.v. 7 mg/kg; 'in the Thorough QT/QTc study, QTc interval prolongation was observed only time points near tmax after administration of teneligliptin 160 mg, and no clinically significant QTc interval prolongation was observed at 40 mg' -> predicted harm appeared at supratherapeutic dose: same (direction), 2012, comparison_stated true, and a dose-ceiling same with margin_stated true (decision 16). Omarigliptin Marizev review: hERG IC50 105 uM (IC20 30 uM = 76.9x unbound Cmax), dog telemetry QT/QTc prolongation only at 75 mg/kg (NOEL 10 mg/kg = 40.8x), TQT P010 25 mg + 175 mg: max placebo-corrected dQTcP 3.66 ms [90% CI 1.56, 5.76] -> same, 2015. The draft credits QT only to the in-silico C-QTc model (Krishna 2016).
- Patient-side label harms the draft does not show (tier 2, PMDA 'Summary of investigation results'): teneligliptin 2014-10-21 hepatic dysfunction + interstitial pneumonia added to Clinically Significant Adverse Reactions (https://www.pmda.go.jp/files/000153316.pdf; 6 + 6 cases, 3 + 4 causality not excluded); teneligliptin 2016-11-22 pemphigoid (https://www.pmda.go.jp/files/000215042.pdf; 15 class cases over 3 FY); omarigliptin 2018-04-19 pemphigoid (https://www.pmda.go.jp/files/000223960.pdf; 8 cases, 3 causality not excluded). Possible model side for ILD: rat lung phospholipidosis discussed at ~20x margin in the teneligliptin review 3.(iii).B.(2); for pemphigoid the animals were silent (omarigliptin review: no skin/eye histopathology in 9-month dog at 788x or 3-month monkey at 48x) — see rule questions.
- Monkey PK-PD -> biweekly regimen for cofrogliptin (member's own model, dose track): Gou et al., Front Pharmacol, epub 2026-01-05, doi:10.3389/fphar.2025.1702101: 'following a single oral dose of 10 mg/kg cofrogliptin, monkeys exhibited sustained DPP-4 inhibition of approximately 80% through day 14' and allometric scaling 'align[s] with its clinical dosing frequency'; patient side NCT04556851 / Gao 2025 doi:10.1111/dom.16014 (Q2W, HbA1c -0.63%/-0.59%). The drafter dropped it because it 'appeared after the Phase 3 read out'; that is not a v3 rule (year = public date, decision 7) unless it is a retrodiction (decision 10) — rule question.
- Patient PK/PD for prusogliptin (engagement, patients, 2025): Liu et al. Clin Pharmacokinet doi:10.1007/s40262-025-01501-8, NCT05146869 (no results posted): 'maximum inhibitory efficacy of DPP-4 increased with higher dose levels ranging from 62.1 to 89.4%' with model side Yeh 2021 doi:10.1016/j.lfs.2021.119574 (rat/dog/diabetic-mouse plasma DPP-4 inhibition). Mentioned in a judgement only; it is a dated patient-subjects entry under decision 3 and should be on the strip.
- Zhang 2020, J Med Chem, doi:10.1021/acs.jmedchem.0c00374 (epub 2020-06-12): the cofrogliptin (HSK7653) discovery paper Gou 2026 cites for the once-every-2-weeks regimen; the prospective model-side source for a cofrogliptin entry on nonrodent-pk-allometry (patient side Bai 2023 / Gao 2025, NCT04556851) dated before the Phase 3, if its animal PK states the interval. Gou 2026 itself is a retrodiction (CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models).

## type-2-diabetes-mellitus-gcgr-antagonists

Rule questions:

- Decision 15: is an in-vivo pharmacological challenge in dosed humans (exogenous glucagon blocked, read as glucose/glucose production) 'direct' like an ex-vivo blockade of a stimulus, or 'downstream' because the readout is physiology? The draft calls it direct and admits the stricter reading.
- Decision 10: does a post-hoc study run for another purpose (veterinary feline diabetes, Mott 2024) that neither cites nor is motivated by the human result qualify as a retrodiction track, or is it outside 'model systems compared with patients for the class' and should carry no entry?
- Decision 12: when an on-target class harm (transaminases, LDL-C, BP, hyperglucagonaemia) is predicted or missed by modality-specific model systems, do the safety entries carry `modality` (as drafted) or stay pooled as the brief says for on-target harms?
- Decisions 10 and 12: a model built to explain a non-member sibling's human harm (Guan 2015, MK-0893 LDL-C) that then anticipates the same harm with member drugs: is its first member comparison a prospective `same` (fresh entry after a retrodiction) or `retro`, given the retrodicted human result belongs to a drug outside the member list?
- Decision 2, tier 5: does a trade-press article quoting the sponsor's statement count as the sponsor press release, or must the release itself (or an SEC filing) be the cited source?
- Decision 4 (models check): does askable silence apply to a genetic characterisation model (Gcgr-/- knockout) as to a dosed tox package? ALT was measurable in mice and the knockout papers never reported it; patients showed dose-dependent aminotransferase rises (Kelly 2015). The draft leaves the knockout uncharged (safety 8) because its stated predictions govern.
- Decision 7: may strengthens: true carry a second match on a track (same after same, safety 9/15/16), or is it reserved for a second miss that removes the first miss's excuse, with further matches going into patient_trials?
- Decision 10 vs the member list: a class patient result from a same-mechanism, same-modality programme outside the tile's four members (MK-0893 LDL-C) motivated Guan 2015; the models check proposes retro for safety 13 on that reading, the drafter untested because MK-0893 is a non-member.
- Decision 10 (models check): on an in-silico track built after the result (Tan 2025), should the opening untested entry carry retrodiction: true as well, or only the comparison entry?

Missed comparisons:

- Gcgr-/- knockout mouse, a genetic model of full antagonism with no track: Yang 2011 BMC Genomics doi:10.1186/1471-2164-12-281 (hepatic metabolic alterations, 'implications on anti-glucagon therapies', 2011-06-01, before the LY2409021 liver-fat finding Guzman 2017 doi:10.1111/dom.12958) and Yu 2011 PLoS One doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0023397 (alpha-cell hyperplasia then PNETs; hyperglucagonaemia matched in Kelly 2015 / Pettus 2018, PNET never assessable). Drafter should open it as its own model_id and score safety on it.
- Mahvash disease, human GCGR loss-of-function (model_kind human_genetic): Zhou 2009 Pancreas doi:10.1097/MPA.0b013e3181b2bb03, PMID 19657311 (homozygous P86S: hyperglucagonaemia, alpha-cell hyperplasia, islet tumour, no hypoglycaemia). Compared with GRA-dosed patients it matches on hyperglucagonaemia and absence of hypoglycaemia (Kelly 2015); PNET risk untestable in 6-month trials. No track in the draft.
- Guan 2015 J Lipid Res doi:10.1194/jlr.M060897 (online 2015-09-15): mouse model of GRA-induced LDL-C rise via cholesterol absorption, dosed with MK-0893 and 'structurally diverse GRAs'. A sibling-dosed model side is admissible (product_in_model false); it predates the member LDL-C findings (PF-06291874 Kazierad 2016, 2016-05-12; volagidemab Pettus 2022), so under decision 10 its first member comparison is a fresh prospective entry, not excluded by the member-only rule as the draft's safety #4 judgement says.

## type-2-diabetes-mellitus-gcgr-glp1r-agonists

Rule questions:

- Decision 16 conflicts with itself here: the model stated 'no overt adverse effects' at the effective dose (Day 2009) and patients' dose was capped by nausea, a harm mice cannot show. The first clause gives dose contra/blocked_by toxicity; the count-once clause ('in safety when the model did not predict it') sends the harm to safety; and the species-capacity qualifier is written for silence only, not for stated predictions. Which governs?
- Dose tie vs decision-16 window: dose[2] contra (the model's dual-active exposure never reached) sits beside efficacy same (co-primaries met at the reached, GLP-1R-sufficient exposure). Decision 4 case 1 makes efficacy untested when the target is never reached; decision 16 makes it untested only when never tested at an effective exposure. Which 'effective exposure' governs, the model's or the trial's?
- Engagement: when a direct human occupancy readout the model never used (PET GCGR occupancy) is low while the model's own downstream readouts moved, does it score engagement at all; and when the source attributes the low occupancy to under-dosing at the highest safe dose, is that counted once in dose only?
- Safety: is a pre-specified heart-rate rise (+7 bpm vs placebo, +4 vs liraglutide, MK-8521) a 'harm' for the askable-silence rule when it is also GLP-1R class pharmacology? Decision 13 sets cross-class evidence aside, but this is the member's own trial.
- One paper, two species (Henderson 2016: DIO mouse and cynomolgus): must the second species be its own model_id track even when the drafter read only the abstract, given the monkey's stated no-vomiting prediction changes the safety cell?
- Safety (cyno-monkey): the 'no vomiting' is a tolerability observation in 4-/8-week toxicity studies at >=10x the clinical per-kg dose, not a pre-specified emesis endpoint. Is that a stated prediction (contra on its own terms) or askable silence (contra only via species capacity)? Same contra either way, but the rule path differs. [models]
- Decision 10: Bosch 2025 asserts the 4GI-HbA1c prediction was made 'prior to the initiation' of the Ph2b (started 2017-08-02) but offers no dated record (model first public 2021-12-24, comparison 2025, earliest poster c. 2024). Does an unsourced sponsor claim of priority ever lift retro to same? [models]

Missed comparisons:

- Cynomolgus monkey track for cotadutide (Henderson 2016, doi:10.1111/dom.12735, full text): 'Bodyweight loss was not associated with any adverse clinical signs or vomiting in monkeys' is a stated safety prediction in a species that can vomit; patients: GI disorders 72% vs 40% (Ambery 2018, doi:10.1016/S0140-6736(18)30726-8), nausea 35% / vomiting 17% (Nahra 2021, doi:10.2337/dc20-2151). The draft folds the monkey into model_id dio-mouse; it is a second system (its own model_id) and a safety contra on its own track.
- db/db mouse track for SAR425899 (Evers 2017, doi:10.1021/acs.jmedchem.7b00174): chronic dose-dependent glucose lowering in diabetic db/db mice, product in model; patient side NCT02973321 (results 2021-02-09; Schiavon 2021, doi:10.1111/dom.14394). No track and no model_src for SAR425899 anywhere in the draft.
- Human GLP-1 + glucagon co-infusion (model_kind human_challenge): Tan 2013 (doi:10.2337/db12-0797; energy expenditure rose with glucagon and glucagon+GLP-1, GLP-1 blunted the glucose rise) and Cegla 2014 (doi:10.2337/db14-0242; food intake -13%, energy expenditure +53 kcal/day). Its energy-expenditure prediction was tested in dosed humans by Corbin 2023 (doi:10.1002/oby.23633: smaller metabolic adaptation, no 24-h energy-expenditure difference). No track.
- In-silico candidate: the 4GI QSP model of human glucose regulation built 'to support the development of a glucagon/GLP-1 dual agonist' (doi:10.1002/psp4.12752, online 2021-12-24; abstract only). Validated on GLP-1RAs; a track only if it made a dated dual-agonist prediction, else a retrodiction.
- Programme-fate sources the draft asserts without citing: AstraZeneca Q1-2023 results statement removed cotadutide from the pipeline (strategic, no press release; pharmaceutical-technology.com 'az-terminate-cotadutide-coagonist'); Janssen returned efinopegdutide/JNJ-64565111 to Hanmi in 2019 after phase 2 (fiercebiotech.com 'j-j-dumps-hanmi-obesity-drug-following-midphase-trials'). Neither is a missed trial failure. Sanofi's SAR425899 discontinuation and Merck's reason for terminating MK-8521-004 could not be verified in budget.
- Obese diabetic cynomolgus monkey for the Sanofi exendin series (Evers 2018, doi:10.1021/acs.jmedchem.8b00292; abstract: 'favorable blood glucose and body weight lowering'): a candidate track for SAR425899 only once the compound identity is confirmed (Tillner 2019 cites Evers 2017, not 2018); flagged, not opened. [CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models]

## type-2-diabetes-mellitus-gck-activators

Rule questions:

- Dose: does a semi-mechanistic model fitted to the drug's own patient MAD data (LY2599506 GIG model) count as a 'model' with model_change: true that can turn a contra cell back to same? It is a human-to-human extrapolation, not a preclinical system, and its trials stopped before efficacy was fully shown.
- Safety: when a model's only public sources are pharmacology papers (no tox review, no NDA), is their silence on a harm the species can show (rodent triglycerides) enough to score contra when patients show it?
- Efficacy: can a human-genetic rationale for a hepatoselective drug (TTP399) count as the model for later, non-hepatoselective members (dorzagliatin), or is it a separate model_id limited to the drugs designed from it?
- Dating: TTP399 Phase 2 results were presented in 2016 (ADA), before Vella 2019. Does a conference abstract or press release set the entry year under rule 6?
- Model-informed drug development papers (Liu 2017 sinogliatin PK/PD) published after the first-in-patient data, claiming a learn-research-confirm projection: without a dated pre-trial record of the projection, does the default fall to retro (checker) or to the paper's framing (drafter)?
- Two-arm drug-target MR (GCK vs GCKR proxies) under one model_id: when one arm reproduces the class harm and the other contradicts it, which arm is the class proxy? Safety 7 scores the GCK arm contra on dorzagliatin's TG rise.

Missed comparisons:

- safety 2011: MK-0941 raised triglycerides (and hypoglycaemia) in patients (Meininger 2011, doi 10.2337/dc11-1200), while the rodent model of record (Eiki 2011) was silent on lipids that rodents can show (De Ceuninck 2013). Arguably contra (silence contradicted), which would keep later rodent-model safety entries from flipping to same.
- dose 2011: MK-0941 dose reached and the Week-14 primary met (Meininger 2011): the 'reached, drug worked' row gives dose same in 2011 before the AZD1656 contra.
- efficacy patient_trials: PF-04991532 (hepatoselective member) met the 12-week HbA1c primary in NCT01336738 (450 and 750 mg vs placebo, p=0.0017 and 0.0003; results posted 2013-08-06) and NCT01338870 (results 2013-04-23). No status change.
- efficacy patient_trials: PF-04937319 (nerigliatin) met the 12-week HbA1c primary at 100 mg (-0.45 to -0.47%) in two studies, Amin 2015 doi 10.1111/dom.12474. No status change.
- safety: LY2599506 Phase 2s (NCT01024244, NCT01029795) were terminated for 'nonclinical safety findings'. The model flagged a harm before patients showed it, and nobody compared it in patients, so it is not scored but is worth a note.
- safety: Baker 2014 (doi 10.1111/bph.12504) is a rodent model that showed no triglyceride rise, against the patient hyperlipidaemia signal. It is a second rodent result that conflicts with De Ceuninck.

## type-2-diabetes-mellitus-glp1r-agonists

Rule questions:

- Rule 5/12: when the only human null for an on-target class harm is a pooled cohort in which non-member drugs (here liraglutide) dominate, does it count for the member class at all, or only for the member share, and how is that share recorded?
- Rule 7: when the patient source (Kapitza, Jan 2015) predates the publication of the model source (Lau, Sept 2015) but the model was used prospectively before the clinic, is the paired entry dated to the later publication, or to the patient source with the model's use year as proxy?
- Safety silence: a single tier-5 liver-injury case in another indication (obesity), which the sponsor says sits at class-background rates. Is that a contradicted silence, or does it need a tier 1-4 patient source before it can be scored?
- Ala316Thr genetics (safety 5/6): the analysis was done before, but first published after, the SUSTAIN-6 topline press release (2016-04-28). Decision 10 requires post-dating AND being motivated by the patient result; does first-public date alone make it retro? Scored same here.

Missed comparisons:

- Dose (semaglutide, peptide): earlier patient PK source Kapitza 2015, J Clin Pharmacol, doi:10.1002/jcph.443 (PMID 25475122), and phase 2 dose-finding Nauck 2016 Diabetes Care doi:10.2337/dc15-0165 (epub 2015-09-10); either dates the semaglutide dose comparison to 2015 instead of the 2017 label.
- Safety (danuglipron, small molecule, imported_from obesity): Pfizer 2025-04-14 discontinuation after one case of possible drug-induced liver injury in an obesity dose-optimisation study (tier 5; e.g. https://www.cnbc.com/2025/04/14/pfizer-scraps-daily-weight-loss-pill-danuglipron.html). Pfizer says overall liver-enzyme rates matched approved GLP-1 RAs; scorable only if a tier 1-3 tox source states hepatic silence, otherwise untested at most.
- No missed efficacy failures found for other members: TTP273 phase 2 positive (vTv 2017, placebo-subtracted HbA1c -0.86/-0.71%), CJC-1134-PC phase 2 HbA1c fell vs placebo (NCT00638716/NCT00674466), efpeglenatide AMPLITUDE programme positive; LY2428757 discontinuation reason not found.
- Safety (semaglutide): SUSTAIN-6 headline press release 2016-04-28 (https://www.globenewswire.com/news-release/2016/4/28/1600310/0/en/semaglutide-significantly-reduces-the-risk-of-major-adverse-cardiovascular-events-in-the-sustain-6-trial.html) predates Scott 2016 publication (2016-06-01); the safety 6 confidence_note should record it.

## type-2-diabetes-mellitus-gpr119-agonists

Rule questions:

- Dose (decision 4): when no model source states an exposure, occupancy or regimen target for any member drug, does PD engagement in patients (PYY 5-fold) plus steady-state exposure above the human single-dose-active level count as 'target reached' (contra derived_from efficacy), or is the dose cell untested?
- Track identity (decision 8): the mouse OGTT/incretin model (Chu 2007/2008, AR231453) and the rat ZF/SD/nSTZ model (Matsumoto 2018, DS-8500a) share one model_id labelled 'mice' — is 'rodent GPR119 OGTT' one system, or do rat and mouse need separate tracks?
- Same drug, split populations (decision 6): firuglipel met its HbA1c or 24-h WMG primary in three Japanese trials and failed its HbA1c primary in one North-American metformin add-on trial with a working comparator — does one population's failure make 'the model predicted wrongly for a drug' when the same drug matched elsewhere, and is magnitude null or within_2x on the Japanese data?
- Scope (decisions 5 and 8): are in-vitro DDI/transporter panels compared with a clinical DDI study (Polli 2013, GSK1292263 vs statins) a safety track for the atlas, or outside the four questions?
- Dating a human ex-vivo model (decisions 7 and 10): Habib 2013 was published while the GSK T2D trials (run 2009-10, published 2014) were unpublished but after Katz 2012 (JNJ-38431055 raised GLP-1/GIP) — prospective or retrodiction?
- Engagement with a mixed readout (decisions 11 and 15): GLP-1/GIP/insulin flat but PYY 5-fold up — the draft records source_calls_it: partial on a contra, while decision 11 defines partial as same + partial; which status does a readout split take?
- Scope refinement (decisions 6, 8, 12; CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models): a molecule-specific off-target DDI track (Polli 2013, GSK1292263 vs statins) is in scope by the letter of decisions 8 and 12, but on a single-modality tile its 2012 `same` becomes the safety headline until the on-target track scores in 2017/2018; should member-specific off-target liabilities carry a harm-scope flag or stay out of the headline?
- Null-null (#58; CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models): does a hedged null ('the possibility that GPR119 agonists may be relatively poor stimuli in the human colon should, however, be considered', Habib 2013, offered beside a culture-sensitivity explanation) count as the model making the null a prediction?
- Track assignment (decision 8; CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models): the split of the nSTZ rat (member drug's own model, clinically tested comparators) from the mouse OGTT track is supported by decision 8; do draft efficacy index 3 and safety index 2 (model_src Matsumoto 2018, DS-8500a) then move to t2d-nstz-rat-repeat-ogtt?

Missed comparisons:

- Human primary colonic L-cell cultures (ex_vivo, human): Habib 2013, doi:10.1007/s00125-013-2887-z, 'a GPR119 agonist did not significantly stimulate secretion' of GLP-1 (GPR40/120 and GPBAR1 agonists did). A human ex-vivo engagement model whose prediction agrees with the flat GLP-1 in Nunez 2014; no track in the draft.
- Rat nSTZ repeat-dose OGTT retrodiction: Matsumoto 2018, doi:10.1124/jpet.118.250019 (abstract) — GSK1292263 and MBX-2982, 'which had been clinically tested previously', still lowered glucose in nSTZ rats (DS-8500a merely larger). A post-hoc rodent run that did not reproduce the human failure: contra with retrodiction: true (decision 10), unscored in the draft; the drafter's own judgement says it could not read it, but the abstract carries it.
- In-vitro transporter/CYP panel vs clinical statin DDI for GSK1292263: Polli 2013, doi:10.3109/00498254.2012.739719 — in vitro predicted BCRP and OATP1B1 inhibition; clinic (300 mg BID) showed 1.34-1.39x statin AUC consistent with intestinal BCRP/CYP3A4 and no OATP1B1 inhibition (simvastatin acid ratio 1.05). A stated in-vitro-to-human comparison for a member drug with no track (scope question below).
- Sponsor discontinuation the draft says it could not find: Daiichi Sankyo removed DS-8500a from its pipeline in May 2017 as a portfolio decision (FierceBiotech 'Daiichi cuts 6 medicines in last quarter'; BioSpace 'Daiichi Sankyo Abandons 6 Clinical Programs in Q1 Update'); tier 5, patient side only.
- Inagaki 2017 (NCT02222350), doi:10.1136/bmjdrc-2017-000424, epub 2017-09-29: 28-day placebo-controlled DS-8500a trial, primary 24-h WMG met (-0.74 and -1.05 mmol/L at 10 and 75 mg), no hypoglycaemia. Listed in the draft with null doi and quote.
- MBX-2982 Phase 2 (NCT01035879, 4 weeks, no posted results): only a secondary database summary (NCATS Inxight, drugs.ncats.io/substance/B5TRY67L51) reports lower mean and post-meal glucose with higher insulin and GLP-1; no sponsor press release found, so at best tier 5 with trial_unreported: true. JNJ-38431055 (Katz 2012, doi:10.1111/j.1463-1326.2012.01587.x, no 24-h WMG effect after 14 days) is a non-member and stays out per decision 12.

## type-2-diabetes-mellitus-hsd11b1-inhibitors

Rule questions:

- Decision 4 #32 vs #53: a 28-day trial whose glucose PRIMARY was missed with clear target engagement and whose sponsor's own conclusion calls it a failure (Roche BP21850), but with one significant secondary (weight -1.67 kg) that the model also predicted — #32 literally gives untested; #53 ('failed = predicted effect absent') gives contra. Which governs when the source's conclusion calls a miss rather than a match?
- Askable silence when the safety track is a genetic model (11β-HSD1 KO) and the drug's nonclinical tox package is unpublished: does the KO's silence on a dose-dependent heart-rate rise and one SVT discontinuation (BI 187004, 2022/2023) score contra, is an in-range HR rise a 'harm' at all, and may a sponsor-run GLP tox study that no source describes be presumed silent?
- When a tier-5 press release (2015) precedes the tier-1 paper (2022) on the same trial by seven years and both say contra, should the track carry two entries (2015 tier-5 trial_unreported, 2022 tier-1) or one entry dated 2015 with the tier-1 source? The brief's 'higher-tier record sets the status' and 'year = earliest public date' both apply but do not say which carries the year.
- Decision 6 (#3) vs decision 11 (#110), raised by the models check: in one source (Heise 2014) the predicted HPA/adrenal-androgen rise appeared with RO5093151 but not with RO5027383, both with urinary-confirmed 11β-HSD1 inhibition. Does the second drug's null score contra on the safety track (decision 5), partial (decision 11, though the split is by drug not readout), or nothing? Affects the crd-human-genetic and hsd11b1-ko-mouse safety entries alike.

Missed comparisons:

- Roche NCT00823680 primary-endpoint result exists as a tier-3 registry results record the draft did not find: EU register EudraCT 2008-001122-13, CSR synopsis BP21850 (report Aug 2010, first published 2015-06-26), https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/trial/2008-001122-13/results — 'failed to show treatment-related improvements in mean daily blood glucose ... despite clear inhibitory effects of both compounds on 11ß-HSD1 activity'. This decides efficacy entry 3.
- BI 187004 phase 2 failure was announced seven years before Bianzano 2023 by tier-5 sources: Vitae Pharmaceuticals 8-K EX-99.1 2015-06-29 (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1157602/000110465915048184/a15-14878_1ex99d1.htm: 'Primary efficacy data (fasting plasma glucose) from the metformin arm did not meet Boehringer Ingelheim's predefined endpoint criteria') and 2015-12-17 (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1157602/000110465915085551/a15-25158_1ex99d1.htm: monotherapy arm unmet; 'Boehringer Ingelheim has informed Vitae of its intention to terminate the program'). Dates the class contra to 2015 on efficacy and dose.
- HSD11B1 knockout mouse as an EFFICACY model has no entry: Kotelevtsev 1997 (doi:10.1073/pnas.94.26.14924) states the -/- mice 'resist hyperglycemia provoked by obesity or stress' and Morton 2004 (Diabetes 53:931) shows resistance to diet-induced obesity; BI 187004 at complete inhibition (Bianzano 2023) contradicts it. The hsd11b1-ko-mouse track should carry an efficacy opener (1997, untested) and a 2015/2022 contra.
- Human genetic model with no track: cortisone reductase deficiency (loss-of-function HSD11B1 heterozygous R137C/K187N, Lawson 2011 doi:10.1073/pnas.1014934108; H6PD, Draper 2003 doi:10.1038/ng1214, Lavery 2008 doi:10.1210/jc.2008-0743) is 'typified by increased cortisol clearance and androgen excess', the ACTH-driven adrenal androgen rise later seen with RO5093151 (Heise 2014) and BI 187004. A model_kind human_genetic safety track (same) is missing.
- Member drug with a public patient result outside the context: S-707106 phase I/IIa in Cushing's syndrome / autonomous cortisol secretion (Kyushu, JCEM 2021, doi:10.1210/clinem/dgab450; OGTT-AUC responder primary not met) — not scoreable for T2D efficacy, but the draft's 'S-707106 never reported' should be qualified to the T2D trial, and its HPA/safety data are importable (imported_from MONDO Cushing).
- Checked and nothing to add: BMS-823778 human data are CYP2C19 PK only (doi:10.1111/bcp.13421; doi:10.1124/dmd.117.078824), so the cynomolgus acute-PD model in Li 2018 (ED50 0.6 mg/kg) was never compared with human PD and needs no track.

## type-2-diabetes-mellitus-insr-agonists

Rule questions:

- Regimen extension failure: when a model's protracted profile predicted both the filed once-daily regimen (worked, 2011-12) and an exploratory three-times-weekly regimen (failed phase 3 non-inferiority, 2013), is the dose cell contra on that track (rule 4 row 7 / #91, sticky under decision 6), or does the filed regimen govern with the 3TW failure recorded in patient_trials?
- Dating a pairing whose model side was published after the trial (Davies 2016 epub Aug 2016; Byrd 2017 toxicology Jan 2017): is year the trial's epub year as rule 7 reads literally, or the year the pairing first became possible (2017, as drafted)?
- A sponsor-authored overview that itself states the model-vs-human comparison (Jacober 2016): admissible as a comparison_stated source, or a review under rule 2 that must be replaced by the primary clamp paper, turning the entry into a pairing?
- A predicted absence of harm (reduced in-vitro mitogenicity) met by a numerical malignancy excess the regulator attributes to pre-existing disease (efsitora, 1.4% vs 1.0%): does the regulator's attribution keep the track same, or is that drug untested with source_calls_it: unresolved?
- Engagement in a clamp-readout class: the draft accepts T1D-patient clamps as dosed humans for the Lilly dog track (2016) but dates the Novo track's same to a T2D clamp (2021) rather than the 2012 T1D degludec clamps; the brief admits healthy volunteers but does not say whether patients of another disease count for engagement.
- Single-dose PD model vs regimen-dependent on-target harm: when a PD model's own readout (blood glucose) is the harm's channel but the harm patients showed is an excess vs a titrated daily comparator that regulator and trial authors attribute to initiation/titration, is the model's silence 'askable' (safety contra, harm measurable in the species) or is it a failure under conditions the model never tested (#35, #72, untested)? Raised on the STZ-rat efsitora QWINT-5 entry (CHECKER_BRIEF_v3 / models).

Missed comparisons:

- Degludec three-times-weekly regimen: FDA 2015 SumR (bundle 203313 SumR lines 705-712) says the protracted flat profile 'led to Novo Nordisk's evaluation of ... three times a week (3TW) dosing' and 3TW 'is not being pursued'; both phase 3 trials failed non-inferiority (Zinman 2013, Lancet Diabetes Endocrinol, doi:10.1016/S2213-8587(13)70013-5, PMID 24622318; ETD 0.34% and 0.26%, more hypoglycaemia). Absent from dose, efficacy and patient_trials; candidate dose contra (regimen target too low, rule 4 row 7 / #91) dated 2013 on the novo-pig-clamp-rat-dog-pk track.
- Efsitora's own model system (Lilly STZ-diabetic rat: single SC dose gives normoglycaemia for at least 1 week; in-vitro IR/IGF-1R and SAOS-2/H4IIE proliferation; Moyers 2022 JPET doi:10.1124/jpet.122.001105, PMID 35840338; FDA 761408 IntegratedR 2026, bundle lines 1125-1127 and 9719-9723, human t1/2 ~17 days line 1260) is mentioned only in the dose judgement; under decision 8 it is a separate track (e.g. lilly-stz-rat-weekly) with its own engagement/dose/efficacy entries (QWINT-2, NCT05362058, registry 2025-05-16).
- Icodec's selection model (dog intravenous half-life screen with rat SC potency; Kjeldsen 2021 J Med Chem doi:10.1021/acs.jmedchem.1c00257, PMID 33944562) is cited only in the dose judgement; FDA 761326 PharmR line 92 also names diabetic rats and normoglycaemic dogs/pigs with unexplained cross-species potency differences (rat 50% of HI, dog/pig ~2x glargine, lines 93-94, 332-333).
- Safety, hypoglycaemia on the Novo track (no safety entry exists for it): the pig clamp / sponsor claim of 'close to even distribution' of glucose lowering is contradicted by FDA 761326 SumR 2026 (bundle lines 184-186: 'peak glycemic response on day 2 and nadir on day 7 post-injection, resulting in higher level 2/3 hypoglycemia rates on days 2-4'; lines 442-443 'Rates of level 2/3 hypoglycemia were generally higher with insulin icodec'), an on-target harm measurable in normoglycaemic animals (761326 PharmR line 366). The 2024-07-10 CRL for T1D on 'an increased risk of hypoglycemia ... unfavorable benefit-risk' (SumR lines 211-216, ONWARDS 6) is importable with imported_from MONDO:0005147.
- Safety, degludec hypoglycaemia: FDA 2015 SumR lines 707-709 states the flat profile 'might enable true once-daily coverage with a lower risk of hypoglycemia', a stated prediction that BEGIN Once Long met (Zinman 2012 abstract: lower nocturnal hypoglycaemia) and DEVOTE later confirmed; a candidate safety same on the pig-clamp track, currently absent.
